[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
# Offline Backup Guide - Cyber Attack Protection

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
## Overview
When cyber attacks strike, your digital life can be encrypted, deleted, or held hostage in seconds. Offline backups are your insurance policy against digital disasters.

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **3 Copies of Important Data**
- Original (on your device)
- Local backup (external drive)
- Remote backup (offsite/cloud)

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **2 Different Storage Media Types**
- Hard drive + USB flash drive
- SSD + Optical disc (DVD/Blu-ray)
- Physical paper + Digital file

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **1 Offsite Backup**
- Bank safe deposit box
- Trusted relative's home
- Cloud storage (encrypted)

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Priority 1 - Identity & Legal (Update Annually)**
- [ ] Passports (color copies)
- [ ] Birth certificates
- [ ] Social Security cards
- [ ] Driver's licenses
- [ ] Marriage/Divorce certificates
- [ ] Military discharge papers
- [ ] Power of attorney documents
- [ ] Will and estate documents

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Priority 2 - Financial (Update Monthly)**
- [ ] Bank account numbers and routing info
- [ ] Investment account statements
- [ ] Credit card information
- [ ] Loan documents
- [ ] Tax returns (7 years)
- [ ] Insurance policies
- [ ] Property deeds/titles
- [ ] Vehicle registrations

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Priority 3 - Medical (Update After Changes)**
- [ ] Medical history summary
- [ ] Current medications list
- [ ] Allergy information
- [ ] Insurance cards
- [ ] Doctor contact information
- [ ] Vaccination records
- [ ] Emergency medical directives
- [ ] Prescription copies

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Priority 4 - Digital Life (Update Weekly)**
- [ ] Password manager backup
- [ ] 2FA recovery codes
- [ ] Cryptocurrency wallet keys
- [ ] Software license keys
- [ ] Email account recovery info
- [ ] Website backups
- [ ] Contact lists
- [ ] Calendar data

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Paper Backups**
1. **Print** important documents on acid-free paper
2. **Laminate** critical one-page documents
3. **Store** in waterproof/fireproof container
4. **Organize** in labeled folders by category
5. **Index** contents for quick access

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **USB/External Drive Backups**
1. **Use** quality drives from reputable manufacturers
2. **Encrypt** drives with strong passwords
3. **Label** with date and contents
4. **Test** quarterly to ensure readability
5. **Replace** every 3-5 years

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Optical Media Backups (DVD/Blu-ray)**
1. **Burn** at slower speeds for reliability
2. **Verify** data after burning
3. **Store** in cases away from light/heat
4. **Create** multiple copies
5. **Re-burn** every 5-10 years

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Home Storage**
- **Fireproof Safe**
 - Minimum 1-hour fire rating
 - Waterproof seal
 - Bolt to floor/wall
 - Hide in unexpected location

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
- **Hidden Locations**
 - Inside old product containers
 - Behind picture frames
 - Inside books (hollowed)
 - Sealed in walls/floors (renovation)

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Offsite Storage**
- **Bank Safe Deposit Box**
 - Most secure option
 - Climate controlled
 - Requires bank access hours
 - Annual fee ($30-200)

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
- **Trusted Individual**
 - Family member in different city
 - Reciprocal storage agreement
 - Sealed envelope system
 - Annual verification

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
- **Commercial Storage**
 - Climate-controlled unit
 - Security features
 - 24/7 access available
 - Insurance options

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Daily**
- Password changes
- Financial transactions
- Important emails

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Weekly**
- Digital files
- Photos/videos
- Work documents

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Monthly**
- Financial statements
- Account summaries
- Contact lists

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Quarterly**
- Full system backup
- Verify all backups readable
- Update storage locations

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Annually**
- Replace old media
- Update legal documents
- Review and purge outdated

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Grab-and-Go USB Drive**
Create a single encrypted USB with:
- [ ] Identity document scans
- [ ] Financial account list
- [ ] Insurance information
- [ ] Medical summary
- [ ] Contact list
- [ ] Recent photos
- [ ] Password manager export
- [ ] Recovery codes

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Paper Envelope System**
Prepare sealed envelopes containing:
- [ ] Account numbers (partial)
- [ ] Emergency contacts
- [ ] Medical information
- [ ] Insurance policy numbers
- [ ] Attorney contact
- [ ] Next of kin info

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Immediate Actions**
1. **DISCONNECT** - Unplug internet/network cables
2. **DOCUMENT** - Photograph screens/messages
3. **ISOLATE** - Separate backup drives
4. **PRESERVE** - Don't modify anything
5. **ACTIVATE** - Use offline backups only

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Recovery Process**
1. **Clean System** - Complete OS reinstall
2. **Verify Backups** - Check for infection
3. **Restore Gradually** - Priority data first
4. **Monitor Closely** - Watch for reinfection
5. **Update Security** - Patch vulnerabilities

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Security Best Practices**
- Never store passwords with backups
- Use different passwords for each backup
- Don't label drives obviously ("BACKUP")
- Rotate storage locations periodically
- Test restore process regularly

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Cost-Effective Solutions**
- Share safe deposit box with family
- Trade offsite storage with friend
- Use free cloud storage (encrypted)
- Repurpose old devices for storage
- Print important docs at library

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Red Flags to Watch**
- Backup drives running constantly
- Files with strange extensions
- Backup software warnings
- Unusual network activity
- System performance issues

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Monthly Check**
- [ ] All drives readable
- [ ] Encryption keys work
- [ ] Paper copies legible
- [ ] Offsite backups accessible
- [ ] Cloud storage active

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
### **Restore Test**
- [ ] Pick random file
- [ ] Attempt restoration
- [ ] Verify integrity
- [ ] Document issues
- [ ] Update procedures

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
**Remember: Backups are only useful if they:**
1. **Exist** - Actually created
2. **Work** - Tested regularly
3. **Accessible** - Can retrieve when needed
4. **Current** - Recently updated
5. **Secure** - Protected from threats

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
*"The best time to create backups was yesterday. The second best time is now."*

[src: modern_threats/cyber/offline_backup_guide.md]
**See also:** [REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md) for CISA and cybersecurity resources. [financial_preparedness.md](../../long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md) for protecting critical financial documents.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
This guide covers more advanced first aid topics that are critical in a disaster when professional medical help is unavailable. This is not a substitute for professional medical training.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
In a trauma situation, you must address the most life-threatening injuries first. The MARCH algorithm helps you prioritize:

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
* **M - Massive Hemorrhage:** Stop major bleeding. This is the first priority. Apply direct, firm pressure to the wound. If that doesn't work, use a tourniquet.
* **A - Airway:** Ensure the person has a clear airway. If they are unconscious, tilt their head back and lift their chin to open the airway.
* **R - Respirations:** Make sure the person is breathing. If not, begin CPR.
* **C - Circulation:** Check for a pulse. Treat for shock by laying the person down, keeping them warm, and elevating their feet.
* **H - Hypothermia/Head Injury:** Prevent hypothermia by keeping the person warm and dry. Check for signs of a head injury.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
Proper wound care is essential to prevent infection.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
* **Clean the Wound:**
 1. Wash your hands thoroughly with soap and water.
 2. Gently clean the wound with soap and purified water. Do not use hydrogen peroxide or iodine on the wound itself, as this can damage the tissue.
 3. Rinse the wound thoroughly.
* **Dress the Wound:**
 1. Apply a thin layer of antibiotic ointment.
 2. Cover the wound with a sterile dressing.
 3. Change the dressing daily, or more often if it becomes wet or dirty.
* **Monitor for Infection:** Watch for signs of infection, including:
 * Redness and swelling around the wound.
 * Pus or drainage.
 * Increased pain.
 * Fever.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
* **Treatment:**
 * Rest.
 * Drink plenty of fluids.
 * Take over-the-counter fever reducers like acetaminophen or ibuprofen.
* **When to Worry:** A fever accompanied by a stiff neck, severe headache, or confusion can be a sign of a serious infection and requires immediate medical attention.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
* **Treatment:**
 * The biggest danger is dehydration. Drink plenty of fluids, especially water with a small amount of salt and sugar to replace lost electrolytes.
 * Avoid dairy products and fatty foods.
* **When to Worry:** If the diarrhea is bloody, or if it is accompanied by a high fever or severe abdominal pain, seek medical help.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
### Respiratory Infections (Colds and Flu)

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
* **Treatment:**
 * Rest.
 * Drink plenty of fluids.
 * Over-the-counter cold and flu medications can help manage symptoms.
* **When to Worry:** If you have difficulty breathing, a persistent high fever, or chest pain, seek medical help.

[src: medicine/advanced_first_aid_guide.md]
This guide provides basic information. In a real emergency, you will have to make difficult decisions. The best thing you can do is get proper first aid training before a disaster strikes.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
This guide lists reputable sources for first aid, CPR, and emergency medical training.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **American Red Cross** - [redcross.org/take-a-class](
 First Aid, CPR/AED, babysitting, lifeguarding. Online and in-person options.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **American Heart Association** - [heart.org/cpr](
 BLS, Heartsaver CPR/AED, First Aid. Widely accepted for workplace and healthcare.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **National Safety Council** - [nsc.org/firstaid](
 First Aid, CPR, workplace safety.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **Stop the Bleed** - [stopthebleed.org](
 Free training for controlling severe bleeding. Often offered by hospitals and emergency services.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **CDC First Aid** - [cdc.gov/firstaid](
 Basic first aid guidance.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **Ready.gov First Aid** - [ready.gov/first-aid-kit](
 Kit contents and basic use.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* **NIH MedlinePlus First Aid** - [medlineplus.gov/firstaid](
 Condition-specific first aid information.

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]
* Hands-on practice with manikins
* Certification valid for 2 years (typical for CPR/AED)
* Coverage of choking, bleeding, shock, and cardiac arrest
* Pediatric module if you care for children

[src: medicine/first_aid_cpr_sources.md]

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
This guide covers the immediate first aid for sprains and the management of common aches and pains.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
## 1. Treating Sprains: The R.I.C.E. Method

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
A sprain is an injury to a ligament (the tissue that connects bones to other bones). The most common location for a sprain is the ankle. For the first 24-48 hours after an injury, follow the R.I.C.E. method to reduce pain and swelling.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
* **Rest:** Stop all activity and rest the injured joint. Do not put weight on it.
* **Ice:** Apply a cold pack (a bag of frozen vegetables works well) to the injured area for 15-20 minutes at a time, every 2-4 hours. Wrap the cold pack in a towel to prevent frostbite.
* **Compression:** Wrap the injured area with an elastic bandage (like an ACE bandage). It should be snug, but not so tight that it cuts off circulation.
* **Elevation:** Keep the injured limb elevated above the level of your heart as much as possible.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
### Things to Avoid for the First 48 Hours (H.A.R.M.)

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
* **Heat:** Avoid hot showers, heat packs, or saunas.
* **Alcohol:** Alcohol can increase swelling and mask pain, which can lead to further injury.
* **Running:** Or any other form of strenuous exercise.
* **Massage:** Can increase swelling and bleeding in the injured tissue.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
For common aches and pains (headaches, muscle soreness, etc.), over-the-counter pain relievers can be effective. **Always read the label and do not exceed the recommended dose.**

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
* **Acetaminophen (Tylenol):** A good choice for general pain relief.
* **Ibuprofen (Advil, Motrin):** An anti-inflammatory, which makes it particularly good for sprains and other injuries where swelling is a factor.
* **Naproxen (Aleve):** Another anti-inflammatory, similar to ibuprofen.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
**Important Note:** Do not give aspirin to children or teenagers, as it has been linked to a rare but serious condition called Reye's syndrome.

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
This guide is for minor injuries. You should seek professional medical help if:

[src: medicine/sprains_and_pain_guide.md]
* You suspect a bone is broken.
* The pain is severe and does not improve with over-the-counter medication.
* The swelling does not go down after 48 hours.
* You cannot put any weight on the injured limb.
* The joint feels unstable or "wobbly."

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
This guide summarizes the **Run. Hide. Fight.** approach to surviving an active shooter incident. Based on [Ready.gov Active Shooter]( and [DHS guidance](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
* **Getting away is the top priority.** Leave belongings behind. Evacuate if safe to do so.
* **Warn others** nearby if you can do so safely.
* **Call 911** once you reach safety.
* **Know exits:** Before emergencies, identify two nearby exits when visiting buildings. Plan escape routes.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
* **If you cannot escape safely,** find a secure hiding place out of the shooter's view.
* **Lock and barricade** doors. Turn off lights. Silence electronic devices. Stay quiet.
* **Spread out** - do not hide in groups; make it harder for the shooter to target multiple people.
* **Communicate with police** silently (texts, window signs) if possible.
* **Stay in place** until law enforcement confirms the danger has passed.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
* **Last resort** - when in immediate danger and you cannot run or hide.
* **Commit to your actions.** Act aggressively to stop the shooter.
* **Use improvised weapons:** chairs, fire extinguishers, scissors, books - to distract and disarm.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
* Familiarize yourself with emergency plans at locations you frequent.
* Participate in active shooter drills.
* Learn first aid and tourniquet training.
* Report suspicious activity to authorities.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
* **Keep hands visible and empty** when encountering law enforcement.
* **Follow their instructions** immediately.
* **Seek professional help** to cope with trauma.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/active_shooter_response.md]
**See also:** [Ready.gov Active Shooter]( [FBI Run.Hide.Fight. video]( [reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
Emergencies can occur without warning, and you may not be at home. This guide helps you plan for disasters while traveling between work, home, and other regularly visited locations.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
Document the following and keep a copy in your wallet or an accessible location:

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
* **Normal and alternative routes** to home, work, school, and other frequent destinations.
* **Public transportation options:** Bus, train, ferry - line numbers, stops, fares.
* **Daily driving directions** and alternative routes to and from work.
* **Transportation contacts:** Taxi companies, commuter rail, bus lines.
* **Local traffic and transportation information** (radio stations, apps, websites).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
* **Go-bag or car kit:** Water, snacks, first aid, flashlight, phone charger, cash.
* **Comfortable shoes** for walking if transit or roads are disrupted.
* **Emergency contact list** (on paper, not only on your phone).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
* **Identify shelter locations** along your routes (schools, libraries, hospitals, stores).
* **Coordinate with family** on meeting points if you cannot get home.
* **Know your employer's emergency plan** - evacuation, lockdown, communication.
* **Update your plan** when routes or schedules change.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/commuter_emergency_plan.md]
**See also:** [Ready.gov Plan for Locations]( [communications/communications_guide.md](../communications/communications_guide.md), [reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
This guide provides an actionable checklist for what to do before, during, and after an earthquake.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
* [ ] **Secure Your Space:**
 * Anchor heavy furniture (bookshelves, dressers, etc.) to the walls.
 * Secure your water heater to the wall studs.
 * Use museum putty or earthquake straps to secure valuable objects on shelves.
 * Install flexible gas and water lines.
* [ ] **Create a Plan:**
 * Identify safe spots in each room (under a sturdy table, against an interior wall).
 * Establish a family meeting place outside your home.
 * Know how to shut off your gas, water, and electricity.
* [ ] **Build Your Kits:**
 * Have a comprehensive first aid kit.
 * Have a bug-out bag for each member of your family.
 * Store at least one gallon of water per person, per day.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
## During the Earthquake: Drop, Cover, and Hold On

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
* **If you are indoors:**
 * **Drop** to the ground.
 * **Cover** your head and neck with your arms and get under a sturdy table or desk.
 * **Hold On** to your shelter until the shaking stops.
 * Stay away from windows, bookcases, and other heavy objects that could fall.
* **If you are outdoors:**
 * Move to an open area away from buildings, trees, and power lines.
 * Drop to the ground and stay there until the shaking stops.
* **If you are in a car:**
 * Pull over to a clear location, away from bridges and overpasses.
 * Stay in the car with your seatbelt fastened until the shaking stops.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
* [ ] **Check for Injuries:** Administer first aid as needed.
* [ ] **Check for Hazards:**
 * Look for fires.
 * Smell for gas. If you smell gas, shut it off at the meter and open the windows.
 * Look for structural damage.
* [ ] **Communicate:**
 * Listen to your emergency radio for instructions from local authorities.
 * Use text messages to contact your out-of-state contact.
* [ ] **Expect Aftershocks:** Be prepared for more shaking. Drop, cover, and hold on.
* [ ] **Help Your Community:** Check on your neighbors, especially those who are elderly or have special needs.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/earthquake_plan.md]
This plan provides a basic framework. You should adapt it to your specific needs and location.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/extreme_heat_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after periods of extreme heat.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/extreme_heat_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Stay Informed:** Know the heat index and heat advisory levels in your area.
* [ ] **Cooling Plan:** Identify air-conditioned locations (libraries, malls, cooling centers) if you don't have AC.
* [ ] **Hydration:** Have plenty of water. Avoid alcohol and caffeine during heat events.
* [ ] **Vulnerable People:** Check on older adults, young children, and those with chronic conditions. Never leave anyone in a parked car.
* [ ] **Home Prep:** Install window reflectors, use fans, ensure AC units are serviced.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Heat]( [CDC Extreme Heat](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/extreme_heat_preparedness.md]
## During Extreme Heat: Stay Cool and Hydrated

[src: scenario_specific_plans/extreme_heat_preparedness.md]
* **Indoors:**
 * Stay in air conditioning when possible. Use fans to improve ventilation.
 * Take cool showers or baths.
 * Avoid using the stove or oven.
* **Outdoors:**
 * Limit outdoor activity to early morning or evening.
 * Wear lightweight, light-colored, loose-fitting clothing.
 * Wear sunscreen and a hat.
 * Drink water frequently. Don't wait until you're thirsty.
* **Know the Signs of Heat Illness:**
 * Heat cramps, heat exhaustion (heavy sweating, weakness, dizziness, nausea), heat stroke (high body temp, confusion, loss of consciousness). Heat stroke is a medical emergency-call 911.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/extreme_heat_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Continue to hydrate** and rest.
* [ ] **Check on vulnerable neighbors.**
* [ ] **Avoid sudden return to strenuous activity.**
* [ ] **See also:** [REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md) for additional resources.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/flood_safety.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a flood.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/flood_safety.md]
* [ ] **Know Your Risk:** Check if you live in a flood zone. Visit [FEMA Flood Map Service Center]( to view flood maps.
* [ ] **Create an Emergency Kit:** Water (1 gallon per person per day, 3+ days), non-perishable food, first aid kit, flashlight, batteries, important documents in waterproof container.
* [ ] **Develop a Plan:** Identify evacuation routes to higher ground. Have a family meeting place and out-of-area contact.
* [ ] **Protect Property:** Elevate utilities (furnace, water heater, electrical panel) if in a flood-prone area. Install sump pump with battery backup.
* [ ] **Purchase Flood Insurance:** Standard homeowners insurance does not cover flooding. See [floodsmart.gov](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/flood_safety.md]
* **If flood watch or warning is issued:**
 * Move to higher ground immediately. Do not wait.
 * Avoid walking or driving through floodwaters. Six inches of moving water can knock you down; one foot can sweep away a vehicle.
 * Turn around, don't drown.
* **If you must evacuate:**
 * Take your emergency kit. Shut off gas, electricity, and water if instructed.
 * Follow designated evacuation routes.
* **If trapped in a building:**
 * Go to the highest level. Do not climb into a closed attic.
 * Call 911 if possible and signal for help.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/flood_safety.md]
* [ ] **Return only when authorities say it is safe.**
* [ ] **Avoid floodwaters:** They may be contaminated or electrically charged.
* [ ] **Document damage:** Take photos for insurance claims.
* [ ] **Check utilities:** Have a professional inspect gas, electric, and water before use.
* [ ] **Clean and disinfect:** Wear protective gear. Discard contaminated food and water.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Flood]( [FEMA Flood Resources](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/hurricane_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a hurricane.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/hurricane_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Know Your Zone:** Determine if you are in an evacuation zone. Sign up for local alerts.
* [ ] **Build a Kit:** Water (1 gallon per person per day, 7+ days), non-perishable food, first aid, medications, flashlight, batteries, cash, important documents.
* [ ] **Strengthen Your Home:** Install storm shutters or board-up capability. Secure loose outdoor items. Trim trees.
* [ ] **Plan Evacuation:** Know your evacuation routes. Have a destination and fuel your vehicle ahead of time.
* [ ] **Review Insurance:** Ensure adequate coverage. Flood insurance has a 30-day wait period.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Hurricanes](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/hurricane_preparedness.md]
* **If evacuation is ordered:**
 * Leave immediately. Follow official evacuation routes.
 * Take your emergency kit and pets.
* **If sheltering in place:**
 * Stay indoors, away from windows and glass doors.
 * Go to an interior room, closet, or bathroom on the lowest level.
 * Avoid elevators. Use stairs.
 * Listen to NOAA Weather Radio or local news for updates.
* **During the eye:** The calm center is temporary. Dangerous winds will return from the opposite direction.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/hurricane_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Wait for the all-clear** before going outside.
* [ ] **Avoid floodwaters and downed power lines.**
* [ ] **Check on neighbors,** especially elderly or those with special needs.
* [ ] **Document damage** for insurance. Take photos before cleanup.
* [ ] **Use generators outdoors only** to prevent carbon monoxide poisoning.
* [ ] **See also:** [FEMA Hurricane]( community recovery resources.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/landslide_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a landslide.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/landslide_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Know Your Risk:** Steep slopes, areas that have slid before, and regions with heavy rain or wildfires are at higher risk.
* [ ] **Consult Experts:** Have a geological assessment if you live on or below steep slopes.
* [ ] **Emergency Plan:** Identify evacuation routes. Have a family meeting place.
* [ ] **Emergency Kit:** Water, food, first aid, flashlight, batteries, important documents.
* [ ] **Property Measures:** Install flexible pipe fittings, consider retaining walls or drainage improvements. Avoid building on steep slopes.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Landslides]( [USGS Landslides](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/landslide_preparedness.md]
## During a Landslide: Evacuate if Possible

[src: scenario_specific_plans/landslide_preparedness.md]
* **If you suspect imminent danger:**
 * Evacuate immediately. Inform neighbors if safe to do so.
* **If you cannot evacuate:**
 * Curl into a tight ball and protect your head.
 * Move to the side of the flow, not in the path.
* **Watch for warning signs:**
 * Cracks in the ground, tilting trees or fences, unusual sounds (rumbling, trees cracking).
 * Sudden changes in water flow (muddy water, new springs).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/landslide_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Stay away from the slide area**-additional slides may occur.
* [ ] **Check for injured or trapped people** without entering the slide area. Call 911.
* [ ] **Listen for unusual sounds** (trees cracking, boulders rolling) that may indicate more movement.
* [ ] **Report broken utilities** to the appropriate authorities.
* [ ] **See also:** [FEMA Landslide]( community recovery resources.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a pandemic or widespread infectious disease outbreak.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Build Supplies:** Two-week supply of food, water, medications, and household essentials. Include supplies for pets.
* [ ] **Medical Supplies:** First aid kit, fever reducers, tissues, soap, hand sanitizer, face masks (if recommended).
* [ ] **Plan for Disruption:** Schools, workplaces, and services may close. Have backup childcare and work-from-home arrangements.
* [ ] **Stay Informed:** Know trusted sources (CDC, WHO, local health department). Sign up for health alerts.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Pandemic]( [CDC Pandemic Preparedness](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
## During a Pandemic: Protect Yourself and Others

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
* **Hygiene:**
 * Wash hands frequently with soap and water (20 seconds).
 * Avoid touching face, eyes, nose, mouth.
 * Cover coughs and sneezes with elbow or tissue.
* **Distance:**
 * Follow local guidance on social distancing and gatherings.
 * Stay home when sick.
* **Masks:** Use face masks when recommended by health authorities.
* **Vaccination:** Get recommended vaccines when available.
* **Mental Health:** Stay connected with family and friends (virtually). Limit news consumption if it causes stress.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
## After a Pandemic Wave: Recovery and Preparedness

[src: scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Restock supplies** for the next wave or future event.
* [ ] **Support community recovery** and those who were affected.
* [ ] **Update your plan** based on lessons learned.
* [ ] **See also:** [REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md), [financial_preparedness.md](../long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/power_failure_plan.md]
This guide provides an actionable checklist for managing an extended power failure (more than 24 hours).

[src: scenario_specific_plans/power_failure_plan.md]
* [ ] **Assess the Situation:** Determine if the outage is localized to your home or is widespread. Check on your neighbors.
* [ ] **Grab Your Gear:** Get out your flashlights, headlamps, and emergency radio.
* [ ] **Check Your Supplies:** Do a quick inventory of your water, food, and batteries.
* [ ] **Conserve Water:** Fill bathtubs and sinks with water. This water can be used for flushing toilets and for cleaning.
* [ ] **Food Safety:** Keep your refrigerator and freezer doors closed as much as possible. A full freezer will keep food frozen for about 48 hours. A refrigerator will keep food cool for about 4 hours.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/power_failure_plan.md]
* [ ] **Power Down:** Unplug all sensitive electronics to protect them from power surges when the electricity is restored.
* [ ] **Set Up Your Off-Grid Kitchen:** Get out your camp stove or other off-grid cooking method. Plan your meals.
* [ ] **Manage Your Water:** Start using your stored water. If you have a water filter, identify a source of water you can use if the outage continues.
* [ ] **Stay Informed:** Listen to your emergency radio for updates from local authorities.
* [ ] **Check on Your Community:** Check on elderly or vulnerable neighbors. This is a good time to start building community resilience.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/power_failure_plan.md]
* [ ] **Food Management:** If the outage continues beyond 48 hours, you will need to cook or otherwise preserve the food in your freezer. A mass cook-off with your neighbors is a great way to build morale and share resources.
* [ ] **Sanitation:** If the water supply is affected, switch to your emergency sanitation plan (e.g., the twin-bucket toilet system).
* [ ] **Security:** Maintain a low profile at night. Keep lights and noise to a minimum.
* [ ] **Power Management:** If you have a generator or solar panels, use them judiciously. Only power essential devices.
* [ ] **Mental Health:** An extended power outage can be stressful. Maintain a routine, stay busy, and check in with your family and neighbors.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tornado_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a tornado.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tornado_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Identify a Safe Room:** A basement, storm cellar, or an interior room on the lowest floor with no windows (bathroom, closet, hallway).
* [ ] **Practice Drills:** Know how to get to your safe room quickly. Include pets in your plan.
* [ ] **Emergency Kit:** Water, non-perishable food, first aid kit, flashlight, batteries, whistle, important documents.
* [ ] **Sign Up for Alerts:** Wireless Emergency Alerts (WEA), NOAA Weather Radio, and local emergency alerts.
* [ ] **Know the Signs:** Dark or greenish sky, large hail, roaring noise (like a freight train), rotating funnel cloud.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Tornadoes](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tornado_preparedness.md]
## During a Tornado: Take Cover Immediately

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tornado_preparedness.md]
* **If you are in a structure (home, school, office):**
 * Go to the lowest level. Get under a sturdy table or desk.
 * Cover your head and neck with your arms. Use pillows, blankets, or a mattress if available.
 * Stay away from windows and exterior walls.
* **If you are in a mobile home:**
 * Get out. Go to the nearest sturdy building or designated storm shelter.
* **If you are outdoors with no shelter:**
 * Lie flat in a ditch or low-lying area. Cover your head.
 * Do not get under an overpass or bridge.
* **If you are in a vehicle:**
 * Drive to the nearest sturdy shelter. If you cannot, stay in the car with seatbelt on, head below windows, or get out and lie in a low area.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tornado_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Watch for hazards:** Downed power lines, broken gas lines, sharp debris.
* [ ] **Check on others:** Help neighbors and those who may be injured.
* [ ] **Listen to officials:** Stay out of damaged areas until cleared.
* [ ] **Document damage** for insurance claims.
* [ ] **See also:** [FEMA Tornado](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tsunami_preparedness.md]
This guide provides actionable steps for what to do before, during, and after a tsunami.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tsunami_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Know Your Zone:** If you live, work, or visit coastal areas, know if you are in a tsunami hazard zone.
* [ ] **Evacuation Routes:** Learn the quickest route to high ground or inland. Practice the route.
* [ ] **Emergency Kit:** Water, food, first aid, flashlight, batteries, whistle, important documents in waterproof bag.
* [ ] **Natural Warnings:** A strong or long earthquake, a sudden drop or rise in ocean level, or a loud ocean roar can indicate an approaching tsunami.
* [ ] **Sign Up for Alerts:** Tsunami Warning System, local emergency notifications.
* [ ] **See also:** [Ready.gov Tsunami]( [NOAA Tsunami](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tsunami_preparedness.md]
## During a Tsunami: Evacuate Immediately

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tsunami_preparedness.md]
* **If you are in a tsunami hazard zone and feel a strong earthquake:**
 * Drop, cover, and hold on. Then move to high ground or inland immediately.
 * Do not wait for an official warning.
* **If you hear a tsunami warning:**
 * Evacuate at once. Follow evacuation routes. Go on foot if possible-roads may be clogged.
* **If you are at the beach and the water recedes unusually:**
 * This is a natural warning. Move to high ground immediately.
* **Stay away from the coast** until officials announce the all-clear. Tsunamis can involve multiple waves over hours.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/tsunami_preparedness.md]
* [ ] **Return only when officials say it is safe.**
* [ ] **Avoid floodwaters**-they may be contaminated or hide dangerous debris.
* [ ] **Watch for downed power lines and gas leaks.**
* [ ] **Document damage** for insurance claims.
* [ ] **See also:** [FEMA Tsunami](

[src: scenario_specific_plans/winter_storm_preparedness.md]
* **Prepare your home:** Insulate walls and attics, caulk and weatherstrip doors and windows. Clear gutters and downspouts.
* **Heating fuel:** Ensure you have adequate heating fuel. If you use wood, have a good supply of dry wood.
* **Emergency kit:** Assemble a kit with non-perishable food, water, blankets, flashlights, batteries, a first-aid kit, and a battery-powered or hand-crank radio.
* **Vehicle:** Keep your vehicle's gas tank full. Check antifreeze, tires, and windshield wiper fluid.
* **Communication plan:** Establish a family communication plan in case you are separated.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/winter_storm_preparedness.md]
* **Stay indoors:** Avoid unnecessary travel. If you must go out, dress in layers and wear waterproof, insulated boots.
* **Conserve heat:** Close off unused rooms. Stuff towels or rags in cracks under doors. Cover windows at night.
* **Food and water:** Eat regularly to maintain body heat. Drink plenty of fluids to prevent dehydration.
* **Carbon monoxide:** Use generators, charcoal grills, and camping stoves outdoors only. Keep them away from windows.
* **Frozen pipes:** To prevent pipes from freezing, keep faucets dripping slightly. Open cabinet doors to allow warmer air to circulate around plumbing.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/winter_storm_preparedness.md]
* **Check on neighbors:** Especially the elderly or those with disabilities.
* **Power outages:** Report outages to your utility company. Avoid downed power lines.
* **Travel:** Be aware of icy roads and sidewalks. Clear snow and ice from walkways.
* **Food safety:** Discard perishable food that has been at room temperature for more than two hours.

[src: scenario_specific_plans/winter_storm_preparedness.md]
**See also:** [home_repair_guide](../home_repair/home_repair_guide.md) for frozen-pipe prevention and repair. [REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md) for Ready.gov and FEMA resources.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
# Urban Food Security: Long-Term Storage and Off-Grid Cooking

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
This guide covers the essentials of building a long-term food supply and cooking it when the power grid is down.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
Your goal is to build a rotating pantry of shelf-stable foods that your family will actually eat.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
For a truly long-term (20+ years) foundation, focus on these items. When stored properly in airtight containers in a cool, dark, dry place, their shelf life is practically indefinite.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
1. **Grains:** White rice, wheat, corn, oats.
2. **Legumes:** Dried beans (pinto, black, kidney), lentils, split peas.
3. **Sweeteners:** Honey, sugar.
4. **Salt:** Essential for flavor and food preservation.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
### Building Your Pantry: A Practical Approach

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
* **Start Small:** Begin by buying a few extra cans of food you normally eat each time you go to the grocery store. Get into the habit of "store what you eat, eat what you store."
* **Mid-Term Storage (1-10 years):**
 * **Canned Goods:** Meats, fish, vegetables, fruits, soups.
 * **Dried Goods:** Pasta, white flour, sugar, spices.
 * **Oils & Fats:** Vegetable oil, shortening (rotate these more frequently, about every 1-2 years).
 * **Powdered Drinks:** Powdered milk, juice mixes, instant coffee/tea.
* **Storage Containers:** For bulk items like rice and beans, use food-grade buckets with gasket-sealed lids. You can add oxygen absorbers to these containers before sealing to extend shelf life even further.
* **Rotation is Key:** First In, First Out (FIFO). When you buy a new can of beans, put it at the *back* of the shelf and use the one at the *front*. This ensures nothing expires.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
When the power is out, you still need to boil water and cook food. **NEVER use charcoal grills or propane stoves indoors.** They produce deadly carbon monoxide.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
* **Propane/Gas Grill:** The easiest and most common solution. Always have a spare, full propane tank.
* **Charcoal Grill:** A classic. Store charcoal in a dry place.
* **Rocket Stove:** A highly efficient stove that can cook a full meal with just a handful of twigs. You can buy one or build a simple one from bricks or cinder blocks.
* **Solar Oven:** Uses the sun's energy to cook food. It's slow, but it's silent and requires no fuel. You can buy one or find many DIY plans online.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
* **Portable Butane Stove:** These are small, single-burner stoves often used for camping or catering. They are generally considered safe for indoor use *with adequate ventilation* (e.g., an open window).
* **Alcohol Stove:** Another small, portable option. Denatured alcohol is a readily available fuel.
* **Canned Heat (Sterno):** Excellent for warming food or boiling small amounts of water. Long shelf life and relatively safe for indoor use.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
* **Propane:** Store tanks outdoors, upright, and away from direct sunlight.
* **Charcoal / Wood:** Keep in a dry location, off the ground.
* **Butane / Alcohol / Canned Heat:** Store in a cool, dark place.

[src: water_and_food/urban_food_guide.md]
By combining a well-stocked, rotating pantry with a reliable off-grid cooking method, you can ensure your family stays fed and healthy during a power outage or other urban disaster.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
# Urban Water Procurement and Purification Guide

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
In a disaster, clean drinking water is your most immediate priority. This guide outlines how to secure and purify water in an urban environment.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
The easiest and safest water is what you store *before* a disaster.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **How Much:** Store a **minimum** of **1 gallon (4 liters) per person, per day**. Aim for at least a 3-day supply, with a 2-week supply being ideal. Don't forget to account for pets.
* **Best Containers:**
 * Commercially bottled water (easiest, safest).
 * Food-grade water storage containers (available at camping or surplus stores).
 * Thoroughly cleaned and sanitized 2-liter soda bottles or other sturdy, sealable containers.
* **Sanitizing Your Own Containers:**
 1. Wash the container and rinse thoroughly.
 2. Mix 1 teaspoon of unscented liquid household bleach with a quart of water.
 3. Pour the bleach solution into your container, close it, and shake well so it touches all interior surfaces.
 4. Rinse thoroughly with clean water.
* **Storage Location:** Store water in a cool, dark place. Keep plastic containers away from gasoline, pesticides, or other chemical fumes, as vapors can penetrate the plastic over time.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
## 2. Finding Water Sources in an Urban Environment

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
If your stored water runs out, you can find water in several places within your home.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **Hot Water Heater Tank:** A typical tank holds 30-50 gallons. Turn off the power or gas supply first, then open the drain valve at the bottom.
* **Toilet Tank (NOT the bowl):** The water in the upper tank is clean (unless you use chemical cleaners).
* **Pipes:** There will be water trapped in your home's plumbing. You can drain it from the lowest point in the house.
* **Ice Cubes:** Melt any ice you have in the freezer.
* **Canned Goods:** The liquid from canned fruits and vegetables is safe to drink.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
**External Sources (Use with Extreme Caution):**
* Rainwater (collect from downspouts)
* Rivers, streams, or ponds

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
**!! DANGER !! - DO NOT DRINK WATER FROM:**
* Radiators or hot water boilers used for home heating.
* Waterbeds (often treated with chemicals).
* Swimming pools or spas (high chemical content).
* Any water you suspect is contaminated with fuel, fire-fighting foam, or other toxic chemicals.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
If you use water from any source other than your own clean, stored containers, you **MUST** purify it.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
If the water is cloudy or has debris, you must filter it first.
* Pour the water through a coffee filter, a clean cloth (like a t-shirt), or several layers of paper towels.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **Boiling (Most Effective):**
 1. Bring the water to a rolling boil.
 2. Let it boil for **at least 1 full minute**. (At altitudes above 5,000 ft / 1,500m, boil for 3 minutes).
 3. Let the water cool before drinking.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **Chemical Disinfection (Bleach):**
 *Use only regular, unscented household bleach (5.25% sodium hypochlorite).*
 1. Place the water in a clean container.
 2. Add 16 drops of bleach per gallon of water (or 8 drops per 2-liter bottle).
 3. Stir or shake the container.
 4. Let it stand for **30 minutes**.
 5. The water should have a faint chlorine smell. If it doesn't, repeat the dosage and let it stand for another 15 minutes.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **Water Purification Tablets:**
 * Follow the manufacturer's instructions precisely. These are a great item for a bug-out bag.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
* **Portable Water Filters:**
 * Many commercial camping filters are very effective at removing bacteria and protozoa.
 * **IMPORTANT:** Most portable filters do **not** remove viruses. It is often recommended to disinfect the water with bleach or boiling *after* filtering.

[src: water_and_food/urban_water_guide.md]
By following these steps, you can ensure you have a safe supply of drinking water during an urban disaster.

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
# 6-Month Supply Planning Guide - Long-Term Preparedness

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
## Overview
Moving beyond the standard 72-hour emergency kit to true long-term sustainability requires systematic planning for 6+ months of self-sufficiency. This guide provides comprehensive frameworks for extended supply planning across all essential categories.

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Food Storage (6 Months = 180 Days)**
| Category | Per Person/Day | Family/Day | 6-Month Total | Storage Method |
|----------|----------------|------------|---------------|----------------|
| **Calories** | 2,200 | 8,800 | 1,584,000 cal | Mixed storage |
| **Protein** | 50g | 200g | 36 kg (79 lbs) | Freeze-dried, canned |
| **Carbohydrates** | 275g | 1,100g | 198 kg (436 lbs) | Grains, pasta |
| **Fats** | 78g | 312g | 56 kg (123 lbs) | Oils, nuts, seeds |
| **Water** | 1 gallon | 4 gallons | 720 gallons | Tanks, barrels |

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Essential Food Categories**
- **Grains**: 300 lbs (rice, wheat, oats, quinoa)
- **Legumes**: 100 lbs (beans, lentils, chickpeas)
- **Proteins**: 80 lbs (canned meat, fish, freeze-dried)
- **Dairy**: 60 lbs (powdered milk, cheese)
- **Vegetables**: 120 lbs (canned, dehydrated, freeze-dried)
- **Fruits**: 80 lbs (canned, dried, freeze-dried)
- **Fats/Oils**: 40 lbs (coconut oil, olive oil, nuts)
- **Seasonings**: 20 lbs (salt, spices, herbs)

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Q1 (Jan-Mar): Winter Deep Storage**
- **Focus**: Calorie-dense foods, vitamin D supplements
- **Menu Planning**: Hearty stews, soups, grains
- **Special Needs**: Extra heating fuel, preserved vegetables
- **Rotation**: Use oldest canned goods, refresh dried foods

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Q2 (Apr-Jun): Spring Renewal**
- **Focus**: Fresh supplement integration, seed starting
- **Menu Planning**: Lighter meals, fresh sprouts
- **Special Needs**: Garden preparation supplies
- **Rotation**: Deep clean storage areas, inventory refresh

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Q3 (Jul-Sep): Summer Preservation**
- **Focus**: Fresh food preservation, dehydration
- **Menu Planning**: Fresh integration with stored foods
- **Special Needs**: Cooling/preservation equipment
- **Rotation**: Process fresh harvest, update inventories

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Q4 (Oct-Dec): Harvest Integration**
- **Focus**: Final preservation, winter preparation
- **Menu Planning**: Comfort foods, holiday considerations
- **Special Needs**: Final harvest processing
- **Rotation**: Prepare for winter deep storage cycle

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Zone 1: Daily Access (Kitchen)**
- **Capacity**: 2-week rotating supply
- **Temperature**: 65-75 degF
- **Items**: Current consumption items
- **Rotation**: Weekly from Zone 2

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Zone 2: Monthly Access (Pantry)**
- **Capacity**: 2-month supply
- **Temperature**: 60-70 degF
- **Items**: Canned goods, dry goods
- **Rotation**: Monthly from Zone 3

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Zone 3: Quarterly Access (Storage Room)**
- **Capacity**: 6-month bulk storage
- **Temperature**: 50-60 degF
- **Items**: Bulk grains, long-term storage
- **Rotation**: Quarterly inspection and rotation

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Zone 4: Emergency Cache (Secure Area)**
- **Capacity**: 1-month emergency only
- **Temperature**: 40-60 degF
- **Items**: Emergency rations, water
- **Access**: Emergency situations only

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Month 1-2: Foundation Phase**
**Investment: $800-1,200**

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Week 1-2: Water Security**
- [ ] **Water Storage**: 55-gallon barrels (2-4 units)
- [ ] **Water Treatment**: Filters, purification tablets
- [ ] **Water Testing**: pH strips, bacteria tests

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Week 3-4: Basic Food Storage**
- [ ] **Grains**: 50 lbs rice, 25 lbs wheat
- [ ] **Legumes**: 25 lbs beans, 10 lbs lentils
- [ ] **Canned Goods**: 50 cans vegetables, 30 cans protein

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Week 5-8: Storage Infrastructure**
- [ ] **Containers**: Food-grade buckets with gamma lids
- [ ] **Shelving**: Heavy-duty steel shelving units
- [ ] **Climate Control**: Dehumidifiers, thermometers

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Month 3-4: Expansion Phase**
**Investment: $600-900**

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Diversification Strategy**
- [ ] **Protein Variety**: Freeze-dried meats, fish
- [ ] **Nutritional Balance**: Vitamins, supplements
- [ ] **Cooking Fuel**: Propane, wood, charcoal
- [ ] **Preservation Tools**: Dehydrator, vacuum sealer

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Month 5-6: Optimization Phase**
**Investment: $400-600**

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **System Refinement**
- [ ] **Inventory Management**: Spreadsheets, labeling
- [ ] **Rotation Systems**: FIFO organization
- [ ] **Quality Control**: Regular inspections
- [ ] **Skill Development**: Food preservation training

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Visual Inspection Checklist**
- [ ] **Containers**: Check for damage, pest intrusion
- [ ] **Labels**: Verify dates, contents accuracy
- [ ] **Environment**: Temperature, humidity readings
- [ ] **Organization**: FIFO rotation compliance

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Quality Indicators**
| Food Type | Check For | Action If Found |
|-----------|-----------|-----------------|
| **Canned Goods** | Bulging, rust, dents | Discard immediately |
| **Dried Foods** | Moisture, insects | Repackage or discard |
| **Grains** | Weevils, rancidity | Freezer treatment or discard |
| **Oils** | Rancid smell, cloudiness | Replace immediately |

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Quarterly Deep Assessment**
- **Inventory Reconciliation**: Compare actual vs. recorded
- **Nutritional Analysis**: Ensure balanced diet capability
- **Shelf Life Review**: Identify items approaching expiration
- **System Improvements**: Identify storage or rotation issues

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Budget Allocation (6-Month Goal: $3,000-4,500)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Phase 1: Water & Basics (33%)**
- **Water Systems**: $400-600
- **Basic Grains/Legumes**: $300-500
- **Storage Infrastructure**: $300-500
- **Total**: $1,000-1,600

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Phase 2: Diversification (40%)**
- **Protein Sources**: $500-700
- **Vegetables/Fruits**: $400-600
- **Cooking/Preservation**: $300-500
- **Total**: $1,200-1,800

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Phase 3: Optimization (27%)**
- **Quality Improvements**: $300-400
- **Inventory Systems**: $200-300
- **Skill Development**: $200-400
- **Total**: $700-1,100

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Bulk Purchasing**
- **Cooperatives**: Join buying clubs for 20-30% savings
- **Wholesale**: Restaurant supply stores for bulk items
- **Seasonal**: Buy in-season for preservation

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Progressive Building**
- **Monthly Budgets**: $150-250/month over 18 months
- **Sale Stocking**: Stock up during sales (50-75% normal price)
- **Generic Brands**: Quality generics for 30-40% savings

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Base Meal Templates**
1. **Grain + Legume + Vegetable** (Bean and rice variations)
2. **Protein + Starch + Vegetable** (Meat and potato variations)
3. **Soup/Stew Base** (One-pot meal variations)
4. **Breakfast Rotation** (Oatmeal, pancakes, eggs)

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Weekly Menu Structure**
| Day | Breakfast | Lunch | Dinner | Snacks |
|-----|-----------|-------|--------|---------|
| **Mon** | Oatmeal + fruit | Bean soup | Rice + protein + veg | Nuts |
| **Tue** | Pancakes | Leftover dinner | Pasta + sauce | Dried fruit |
| **Wed** | Eggs + toast | Sandwich | Stew + bread | Granola |
| **Thu** | Cereal | Salad | Rice + beans | Crackers |
| **Fri** | Smoothie | Soup | Fish + potato | Trail mix |
| **Sat** | French toast | Leftovers | Comfort meal | Popcorn |
| **Sun** | Eggs + hash | Big meal | Simple dinner | Fruit |

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Winter Menus (Nov-Feb)**
- **Focus**: Warming, calorie-dense meals
- **Emphasis**: Soups, stews, hot cereals
- **Special**: Holiday comfort foods

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Spring Menus (Mar-May)**
- **Focus**: Lighter, cleansing meals
- **Emphasis**: Fresh sprouts, lighter proteins
- **Special**: Incorporate early garden produce

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Summer Menus (Jun-Aug)**
- **Focus**: Fresh integration, preservation
- **Emphasis**: Fresh vegetables, lighter cooking
- **Special**: Raw food integration, fermentation

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Fall Menus (Sep-Nov)**
- **Focus**: Harvest integration, comfort preparation
- **Emphasis**: Preservation cooking, hearty meals
- **Special**: Holiday preparation

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Quantity Metrics**
- [ ] **180-Day Food Supply**: Complete caloric coverage
- [ ] **720 Gallons Water**: Drinking + cooking + hygiene
- [ ] **90% Rotation Rate**: Minimal waste from expiration
- [ ] **30-Day Menu Cycle**: No repeated meals for month

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Quality Metrics**
- [ ] **Nutritional Balance**: All macro/micronutrients covered
- [ ] **Palatability**: Family approval rating >80%
- [ ] **Variety**: 150+ different meal combinations
- [ ] **Cooking Capability**: Multiple preparation methods

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **System Metrics**
- [ ] **Inventory Accuracy**: <5% discrepancy in counts
- [ ] **Storage Efficiency**: <10% spoilage annually
- [ ] **Cost Efficiency**: <$8 per person-day average
- [ ] **Accessibility**: <15 minutes to locate any item

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Planning Mistakes**
 **Calorie Underestimation** - Plan for 2,200+ calories per person
 **Monotonous Menus** - Include variety and comfort foods
 **Inadequate Water** - 1 gallon per person minimum daily
 **No Rotation System** - FIFO essential for quality maintenance

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Storage Mistakes**
 **Poor Environmental Control** - Temperature and humidity critical
 **Inadequate Pest Protection** - Sealed containers essential
 **Overcrowding** - Allow air circulation and access
 **Mixed Storage** - Separate foods by type and shelf life

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
### **Financial Mistakes**
 **All-At-Once Purchasing** - Build gradually over 12-18 months
 **Brand Loyalty** - Focus on nutrition and shelf life
 **Ignoring Sales** - Stock up during 50%+ off sales
 **No Budget Planning** - Set monthly limits and stick to them

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Green Status (>4 Months Supply)**
- **Action**: Normal rotation and restocking
- **Monitoring**: Monthly inventory checks
- **Planning**: Continue normal expansion

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Yellow Status (2-4 Months Supply)**
- **Action**: Accelerate restocking efforts
- **Monitoring**: Bi-weekly inventory checks
- **Planning**: Reduce consumption rate 10%

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Red Status (<2 Months Supply)**
- **Action**: Emergency supply mode activation
- **Monitoring**: Weekly inventory checks
- **Planning**: Ration planning and alternative sourcing

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
#### **Critical Status (<1 Month Supply)**
- **Action**: Strict rationing protocols
- **Monitoring**: Daily consumption tracking
- **Planning**: Emergency procurement and community resources

[src: long_term_sustainability/6_month_supply_planning_guide.md]
*"A six-month food supply isn't just about having food - it's about having the peace of mind that comes from true preparedness."*

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
# Community Resilience Building - Network Development Guide

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
## Overview
Individual preparedness has limits. True long-term resilience requires strong community networks that can coordinate resources, share knowledge, and provide mutual support during extended disruptions. This guide provides frameworks for building and maintaining resilient community networks.

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Primary Networks (Core Survival)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Neighborhood Circles (15-25 People)**
**Optimal Size**: 20 people (5-6 households)
**Geographic Range**: 2-3 blocks or 0.5 mile radius
**Meeting Frequency**: Weekly during normal times, daily during crises

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Core Functions**
- **Immediate Response**: First 24-48 hours of crisis
- **Resource Sharing**: Daily needs and emergency supplies
- **Security**: Neighborhood watch and protection
- **Communication Hub**: Information relay point

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Organization Structure**
- **Circle Coordinator**: Rotating monthly role
- **Resource Manager**: Tracks shared supplies
- **Communications Lead**: Maintains contact systems
- **Security Coordinator**: Organizes watch schedules

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Formation Steps**
1. **Week 1-2**: Identify interested neighbors
2. **Week 3-4**: First informal gathering, gauge interest
3. **Month 2**: Establish regular meeting schedule
4. **Month 3**: Define roles and responsibilities
5. **Month 4+**: Begin skill sharing and resource mapping

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Skill Circles (10-15 People)**
**Focus**: Specific skill development and sharing
**Meeting Frequency**: Bi-weekly workshops/practice sessions

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Medical Circle**
- **Participants**: Medical professionals, first aid trained
- **Resources**: Medical supplies, knowledge sharing
- **Training**: CPR, first aid, emergency medicine
- **Equipment**: Shared medical kit, emergency supplies

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Food Production Circle**
- **Participants**: Gardeners, farmers, food preservers
- **Resources**: Seeds, tools, preservation equipment
- **Training**: Gardening, preservation, nutrition
- **Equipment**: Shared tools, processing equipment

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Technical Circle**
- **Participants**: Mechanics, electricians, engineers
- **Resources**: Tools, repair knowledge, parts
- **Training**: Repair skills, alternative energy
- **Equipment**: Shared tool library, workshop space

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Security Circle**
- **Participants**: Military, police, security-minded civilians
- **Resources**: Training, equipment, coordination
- **Training**: Self-defense, situational awareness
- **Equipment**: Communication gear, basic protection

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Secondary Networks (Extended Support)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Resource Hubs (50-100 People)**
**Geographic Range**: 1-2 mile radius
**Function**: Larger resource pooling and distribution

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Community Resource Center**
- **Location**: Church, school, community center
- **Storage**: Bulk supplies, shared equipment
- **Distribution**: Coordinated resource sharing
- **Governance**: Representative council from neighborhoods

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Tool Library System**
- **Inventory**: 200-500 tools and equipment pieces
- **Check-out System**: Borrowing protocols
- **Maintenance**: Repair and replacement fund
- **Training**: Tool use and safety workshops

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Seed Library**
- **Collection**: Locally adapted varieties
- **Exchange**: Annual seed swap events
- **Education**: Growing and seed saving workshops
- **Preservation**: Climate-controlled storage

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Economic Cooperatives (30-75 People)**
**Focus**: Alternative economic systems during disruption

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Buying Cooperative**
- **Bulk Purchasing**: Food, fuel, supplies at wholesale
- **Cost Savings**: 20-40% savings through volume
- **Quality Control**: Vetting of suppliers and products
- **Distribution**: Coordinated pickup and delivery

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Skill Exchange Network**
- **Time Banking**: Hour-for-hour skill trading
- **Service Directory**: Available skills and services
- **Quality Assurance**: Feedback and rating system
- **Coordination**: Central scheduling system

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Local Currency System**
- **Currency Design**: Community-specific exchange medium
- **Acceptance Network**: Local businesses and individuals
- **Backing System**: Hours, goods, or services backing
- **Administration**: Elected oversight committee

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Phase 1: Foundation (Months 1-3)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Community Assessment**
**Goal**: Map existing networks and identify key people

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Neighborhood Mapping Exercise**
1. **Door-to-Door Survey**: Introduce yourself, gauge interest
2. **Skills Inventory**: What can each person contribute?
3. **Resource Mapping**: What does each household have to share?
4. **Interest Assessment**: Willingness to participate level
5. **Leadership Identification**: Natural leaders and organizers

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Asset Mapping**
- **Physical Assets**: Buildings, tools, equipment, land
- **Human Assets**: Skills, knowledge, connections
- **Institutional Assets**: Churches, schools, businesses
- **Natural Assets**: Water sources, growing spaces

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Communication Survey**
- **Preferred Methods**: Phone, email, text, in-person
- **Technology Access**: Internet, smartphones, radios
- **Backup Systems**: Alternative communication plans
- **Information Preferences**: Meeting style, frequency

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Initial Network Formation**
**Goal**: Establish first working groups

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Introductory Community Meeting**
- **Venue**: Neutral, accessible location
- **Agenda**: Introductions, purpose discussion, next steps
- **Outcome**: Interest groups formation
- **Follow-up**: Contact information exchange

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Working Groups Establishment**
- **Communication Group**: Newsletter, phone tree, bulletin board
- **Skills Group**: Workshops, training coordination
- **Resources Group**: Sharing systems, bulk buying
- **Emergency Group**: Preparedness planning, response protocols

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Phase 2: Development (Months 4-12)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **System Implementation**
**Goal**: Put working systems in place

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Communication Systems**
1. **Phone Tree**: Rapid information dissemination
2. **Newsletter**: Monthly community updates
3. **Bulletin Boards**: Physical information posting
4. **Digital Groups**: Email lists, social media groups
5. **Emergency Protocols**: Crisis communication procedures

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Resource Sharing Systems**
1. **Tool Library**: Check-out system and storage
2. **Bulk Buying**: Quarterly group orders
3. **Skill Exchange**: Time bank or trading system
4. **Emergency Cache**: Community emergency supplies
5. **Transportation**: Ride sharing, emergency transport

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Regular Activities**
- **Monthly Meetings**: Community updates and planning
- **Skill Workshops**: Monthly skill-sharing sessions
- **Social Events**: Quarterly community building events
- **Emergency Drills**: Annual preparedness exercises

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Leadership Development**
**Goal**: Develop sustainable leadership structure

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Rotating Leadership**
- **Term Length**: 6-month terms for key roles
- **Succession Planning**: Leadership pipeline development
- **Training Programs**: Leadership skill development
- **Mentorship**: Experienced leaders guide new ones

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Committee Structure**
- **Steering Committee**: Overall coordination (5-7 people)
- **Functional Committees**: Specific area focus (3-5 people each)
- **Working Groups**: Task-specific teams (varies)
- **Advisory Council**: Experienced advisors (no voting)

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Network Expansion**
**Goal**: Connect with broader networks

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Inter-Community Connections**
- **Neighboring Networks**: Exchange visits and coordination
- **Regional Networks**: Broader area coordination
- **Specialized Networks**: Connect to expertise networks
- **Mutual Aid Agreements**: Formal support agreements

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Institutional Partnerships**
- **Local Government**: Emergency management coordination
- **Businesses**: Resource and service partnerships
- **Non-profits**: Skill and resource sharing
- **Educational Institutions**: Training partnerships

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Advanced Capabilities**
**Goal**: Develop sophisticated response capabilities

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Emergency Response Teams**
- **Medical Response**: First aid and triage capabilities
- **Search and Rescue**: Trained search teams
- **Communications**: Ham radio and backup systems
- **Logistics**: Resource coordination and distribution

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
##### **Alternative Systems**
- **Local Currency**: Full alternative economy
- **Local Production**: Food and goods production
- **Energy Systems**: Community renewable energy
- **Governance**: Consensus decision-making systems

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Engagement Levels**
- **Core Participants**: 10-15% of community (daily engagement)
- **Active Participants**: 25-35% of community (weekly engagement)
- **Occasional Participants**: 40-50% of community (monthly engagement)
- **Aware Non-participants**: 60-70% know about network

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Activity Participation**
- **Meeting Attendance**: 40-60% of active participants
- **Skill Workshops**: 60-80% of interested participants
- **Social Events**: 70-90% of community members
- **Emergency Drills**: 50-70% of community members

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Communication Effectiveness**
- **Information Reach**: 90%+ of community in 24 hours
- **Accuracy Rate**: 95%+ of information accurate
- **Response Time**: <2 hours for non-emergency information
- **Feedback Loop**: 80%+ of messages acknowledge receipt

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Resource Sharing**
- **Tool Library Usage**: 60%+ utilization rate
- **Skill Exchange**: 20+ hours per month community-wide
- **Bulk Buying**: 30%+ participation rate
- **Emergency Response**: <1 hour mobilization time

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Incremental Trust Building**
1. **Low-Risk Interactions**: Social events, information sharing
2. **Small Favors**: Minor assistance, skill sharing
3. **Shared Projects**: Group activities with common goals
4. **Crisis Response**: Support during small emergencies
5. **Long-term Commitment**: Multi-year relationship building

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Trust Indicators**
- **Resource Sharing**: Willingness to lend valuable items
- **Information Sharing**: Open communication about challenges
- **Conflict Resolution**: Ability to resolve disputes internally
- **Leadership Acceptance**: Community support for leaders
- **Mutual Support**: Helping without expectation of return

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Prevention Strategies**
- **Clear Expectations**: Written agreements and protocols
- **Regular Check-ins**: Monthly satisfaction surveys
- **Early Intervention**: Address issues before escalation
- **Mediation Training**: Multiple trained mediators available

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Resolution Process**
1. **Direct Communication**: Encourage direct resolution
2. **Peer Mediation**: Neutral community member mediation
3. **Committee Review**: Formal committee assessment
4. **Community Decision**: Full community involvement if needed
5. **External Mediation**: Professional mediation if required

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Level 1: Neighborhood Alert**
- **Trigger**: Local emergency or concern
- **Method**: Phone tree, door-to-door
- **Timeline**: 30 minutes for full neighborhood
- **Response**: Neighborhood coordinator assessment

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Level 2: Community Mobilization**
- **Trigger**: Significant local emergency
- **Method**: All communication channels
- **Timeline**: 2 hours for full community
- **Response**: Community emergency response activation

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Level 3: Regional Coordination**
- **Trigger**: Area-wide emergency
- **Method**: Ham radio, emergency networks
- **Timeline**: 4 hours for regional coordination
- **Response**: Mutual aid agreements activation

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Emergency Resource Pool**
- **Location**: Secure, accessible community storage
- **Contents**: 72-hour supplies for 20% of community
- **Access**: Emergency coordinator key holders
- **Replenishment**: Quarterly inventory and restocking

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Skill Mobilization**
- **Medical Team**: Respond within 15 minutes
- **Technical Team**: Infrastructure assessment and repair
- **Communications Team**: Information management
- **Logistics Team**: Resource coordination and distribution

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Membership Dues**
- **Structure**: Sliding scale based on ability to pay
- **Amount**: $20-50 per household annually
- **Uses**: Communication, meeting space, emergency supplies
- **Administration**: Elected treasurer oversight

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Activity Fees**
- **Skill Workshops**: $5-15 per workshop
- **Social Events**: Break-even pricing
- **Bulk Buying**: 5-10% markup for administrative costs
- **Tool Library**: Annual membership fee

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Fundraising Activities**
- **Community Events**: Annual fundraising events
- **Grants**: Apply for community development grants
- **Donations**: Accept donations from supporters
- **Services**: Provide services to broader community

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Renewable Resources**
- **Skills**: Continuously developed and shared
- **Knowledge**: Documented and preserved
- **Relationships**: Maintained through regular interaction
- **Systems**: Self-reinforcing and improving

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
#### **Non-Renewable Management**
- **Physical Resources**: Maintenance and replacement funds
- **Leadership Energy**: Rotation and development systems
- **Meeting Spaces**: Multiple options and relationships
- **Communication Tools**: Backup systems and redundancy

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Month 1: Assessment**
- [ ] Complete neighborhood mapping
- [ ] Identify key people and assets
- [ ] Assess communication preferences
- [ ] Document existing networks

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Month 2: Initial Outreach**
- [ ] Host introductory meeting
- [ ] Form working groups
- [ ] Establish communication systems
- [ ] Plan regular activities

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Month 3: System Development**
- [ ] Implement tool sharing
- [ ] Start skill workshops
- [ ] Establish meeting rhythm
- [ ] Create emergency protocols

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Month 6: Network Strengthening**
- [ ] Expand participation
- [ ] Develop leadership
- [ ] Connect with other networks
- [ ] Plan emergency drill

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Year 1: System Maturation**
- [ ] Conduct emergency drill
- [ ] Evaluate and improve systems
- [ ] Plan expansion activities
- [ ] Document lessons learned

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Leadership Pitfalls**
 **Single Point of Failure** - Avoid dependence on one leader
 **Burnout** - Rotate responsibilities to prevent exhaustion
 **Control Issues** - Share decision-making authority
 **Lack of Succession** - Always develop next generation leaders

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Participation Pitfalls**
 **Elite Capture** - Ensure broad community representation
 **Exclusivity** - Keep networks open to new participants
 **Free Riders** - Balance giving and receiving expectations
 **Conflict Avoidance** - Address issues directly and quickly

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
### **Sustainability Pitfalls**
 **Over-ambition** - Start small and grow gradually
 **Under-funding** - Plan for financial sustainability
 **Activity Fatigue** - Balance activity level with participation
 **Goal Drift** - Maintain focus on preparedness objectives

[src: long_term_sustainability/community_resilience_building.md]
*"Individual preparedness gets you through the first week. Community resilience gets you through the first year. Together, we thrive."*

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
This guide helps you build financial resilience for emergencies and extended disruptions.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* **Goal:** 3-6 months of essential expenses in a liquid account (savings, money market).
* **Where:** FDIC-insured account, accessible without penalty.
* **What counts as essential:** Rent/mortgage, utilities, food, medications, insurance.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
1. **Shelter and utilities**
