2. **Food and water**
3. **Medications and medical**
4. **Insurance (health, home/renters)**
5. **Communication (phone/internet if critical)**
6. **Transportation (for work or evacuation)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* Keep a small amount of cash at home for power outages or when ATMs/banks are unavailable.
* Consider small denominations for easier use during local disruptions.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* Keep copies of important documents in a safe, portable location (fireproof safe, safe deposit box, or digital backup).
* Include: IDs, insurance policies, property deeds, bank/account info, medical records, contact list.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* **Homeowners/Renters:** Verify coverage for disasters relevant to your area (flood, earthquake, etc.). Flood insurance is separate.
* **Health:** Understand deductibles, out-of-network care, and prescription coverage.
* **Auto:** Check comprehensive coverage for natural disasters.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* Reduce nonessential debt to improve cash flow during emergencies.
* Know your options for forbearance or payment relief with lenders, insurers, and utilities.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* Understand sick leave, remote work options, and unemployment eligibility.
* Have a backup plan if your primary income is disrupted.

[src: long_term_sustainability/financial_preparedness.md]
* [reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md](../reference/REFERENCE_DOCUMENTS.md) - Links to free financial and disaster prep resources
* [barter_and_trade/barter_and_trade_guide.md](../barter_and_trade/barter_and_trade_guide.md) - Alternative economy options
* [scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md](../scenario_specific_plans/pandemic_preparedness.md) - Pandemic-specific financial impacts

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
# Local Food Production Systems - Self-Sufficiency Guide

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
## Overview
True food security comes from local production capability. This comprehensive guide covers establishing sustainable food production systems that can provide 50-90% of a household's nutritional needs through multiple growing methods and preservation techniques.

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Family of 4 - Annual Food Production Goals**

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Vegetable Production Targets**
| Vegetable Type | Annual Need (lbs) | Growing Space | Harvest Seasons | Storage Method |
|----------------|-------------------|---------------|-----------------|----------------|
| **Tomatoes** | 100 lbs | 100 sq ft | Summer | Canning, drying |
| **Onions** | 40 lbs | 50 sq ft | Fall | Root cellar |
| **Carrots** | 60 lbs | 40 sq ft | Fall/Winter | Root cellar |
| **Potatoes** | 200 lbs | 100 sq ft | Fall | Root cellar |
| **Beans** | 40 lbs | 60 sq ft | Summer | Drying |
| **Squash** | 80 lbs | 80 sq ft | Fall | Root cellar |
| **Greens** | 50 lbs | 40 sq ft | Spring/Fall | Fresh/frozen |
| **Peppers** | 30 lbs | 40 sq ft | Summer | Drying, freezing |

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Total Space Requirements**
- **Minimum Garden**: 500 square feet (intensive methods)
- **Optimal Garden**: 1,000 square feet (standard spacing)
- **Maximum Production**: 2,000 square feet (surplus for storage)

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Urban/Suburban Properties (0.1-0.5 acres)**

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Intensive Growing Methods**
**Available Space: 2,000-10,000 sq ft**

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Square Foot Gardening**
- **Productivity**: 2-4x conventional gardening
- **Space Needed**: 4x8 foot raised beds
- **Yield Example**: 32 sq ft = 200 lbs vegetables annually
- **Investment**: $200-300 per 32 sq ft bed

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Vertical Growing Systems**
- **Trellises**: Beans, peas, cucumbers, tomatoes
- **Tower Gardens**: Strawberries, herbs, lettuce
- **Wall Systems**: Herbs, microgreens
- **Space Efficiency**: 3-5x ground space equivalent

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Container Systems**
- **Patio Containers**: 20-50 gallon containers
- **Grow Bags**: 15-30 gallon fabric bags
- **Window Boxes**: Herbs, microgreens
- **Mobility**: Can relocate for optimal sun/protection

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Small Livestock Options**
**Space: 200-500 sq ft for housing**

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Backyard Chickens**
- **Space**: 4 sq ft per bird in coop, 10 sq ft in run
- **Production**: 6 hens = 1,500 eggs annually
- **Investment**: $500-800 initial setup
- **Feed Cost**: $300-400 annually

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Rabbits**
- **Space**: 3x3 foot cage per breeding doe
- **Production**: 3 does = 150 lbs meat annually
- **Investment**: $400-600 initial setup
- **Feed Cost**: $200-300 annually

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Aquaponics Systems**
- **Space**: 8x12 foot greenhouse setup
- **Production**: Fish protein + vegetables
- **Investment**: $2,000-3,000 complete system
- **Operating Cost**: $200-400 annually

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Expanded Production Capabilities**
**Available Space: 10,000+ sq ft for cultivation**

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Field Crop Production**
- **Grains**: Wheat, corn, oats (1 acre = 30-50 bushels)
- **Legumes**: Soybeans, field peas (nitrogen fixation)
- **Root Crops**: Potatoes, sweet potatoes (high calorie yield)
- **Forage Crops**: Feed for livestock

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Orchard Development**
- **Tree Spacing**: 20-30 trees per acre
- **Production Timeline**: 3-5 years to significant production
- **Yield**: Mature apple tree = 10-20 bushels annually
- **Varieties**: Select for extended harvest season

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
##### **Larger Livestock Systems**
- **Goats**: 6-10 goats per acre, milk and meat
- **Sheep**: 4-6 sheep per acre, meat and wool
- **Pigs**: 2-4 pigs per acre with rotation
- **Cattle**: 1-2 cattle per acre with good pasture

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Cold Frames**
- **Construction**: Simple box with glass/plastic lid
- **Extension**: Add 6-8 weeks to growing season
- **Crops**: Lettuce, spinach, radishes, carrots
- **Cost**: $50-100 per 4x4 frame

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Hoop Houses/Low Tunnels**
- **Construction**: PVC pipes with plastic covering
- **Extension**: Add 8-12 weeks to growing season
- **Crops**: Most vegetables with cold tolerance
- **Cost**: $2-3 per linear foot

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Greenhouse Systems**
- **Small Greenhouse**: 8x10 foot hobby greenhouse
- **Extension**: Year-round growing capability
- **Crops**: Tomatoes, peppers, herbs, microgreens
- **Cost**: $800-2,000 for kit, $2,000-5,000 built

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Indoor Growing Systems**
- **Microgreens**: 7-14 day harvest cycle
- **Sprouts**: 3-7 day harvest cycle
- **Herbs**: Year-round fresh herbs
- **Investment**: $100-500 for LED grow systems

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Spring Succession (March-May)**
| Week | Plant Type | Variety | Harvest Time |
|------|------------|---------|--------------|
| **Week 1** | Cool crops | Lettuce, spinach | 6-8 weeks |
| **Week 3** | Root crops | Carrots, radishes | 8-12 weeks |
| **Week 5** | Brassicas | Broccoli, cabbage | 10-14 weeks |
| **Week 7** | Warm crops | Tomatoes, peppers | 12-20 weeks |

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Summer Succession (June-August)**
- **Continuous Harvest**: Plant lettuce every 2 weeks
- **Bean Plantings**: Plant beans every 3 weeks
- **Fall Crops**: Start fall crops in mid-summer

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Fall Succession (September-November)**
- **Cold Hardy**: Focus on cold-tolerant varieties
- **Storage Crops**: Plant root vegetables for storage
- **Cover Crops**: Plant soil-building crops

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Canning (Water Bath & Pressure)**
**Suitable For**: Tomatoes, beans, corn, meat
- **Equipment**: Pressure canner, jars, lids
- **Capacity**: 7-quart canner processes 7 quarts
- **Shelf Life**: 2-5 years properly stored
- **Space**: Pantry shelving for jar storage

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Dehydrating**
**Suitable For**: Herbs, fruits, vegetables, meat
- **Equipment**: Electric dehydrator or solar dryer
- **Capacity**: 9-tray dehydrator = 15-20 lbs fresh
- **Shelf Life**: 1-2 years in sealed containers
- **Space**: Minimal storage space required

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Fermentation**
**Suitable For**: Cabbage, cucumbers, other vegetables
- **Equipment**: Fermentation crocks or jars
- **Capacity**: 5-gallon crock = 20 lbs vegetables
- **Shelf Life**: 6 months refrigerated
- **Benefits**: Probiotics, enhanced nutrition

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Root Cellar Storage**
**Suitable For**: Potatoes, carrots, apples, onions
- **Requirements**: 32-40 degF, 85-90% humidity
- **Capacity**: 10x10 root cellar = 500+ lbs storage
- **Shelf Life**: 4-8 months depending on crop
- **Construction**: Underground or insulated storage

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Summer Preservation (June-August)**
- **Week 1-4**: Strawberries, early berries freezing, jam
- **Week 5-8**: Summer vegetables canning, dehydrating
- **Week 9-12**: Tomato season sauce, paste, whole canning
- **Week 13-16**: Late summer pickles, relishes

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Fall Preservation (September-November)**
- **Month 1**: Apple harvest canning, drying, storage
- **Month 2**: Root vegetable harvest root cellar prep
- **Month 3**: Final harvests preservation completion

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Hot Composting (Berkeley Method)**
- **Materials**: 30:1 carbon to nitrogen ratio
- **Timeline**: 18 days to finished compost
- **Yield**: 3x3x3 pile = 1 cubic yard compost
- **Management**: Turn every 6 days, monitor temperature

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Cold Composting**
- **Materials**: Kitchen scraps, yard waste
- **Timeline**: 6-12 months to finished compost
- **Yield**: Continuous addition system
- **Management**: Minimal turning, longer process

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Vermicomposting**
- **Materials**: Kitchen scraps fed to worms
- **Timeline**: 3-4 months to harvest castings
- **Yield**: 1 lb worms process 3-4 lbs scraps weekly
- **Output**: High-quality worm castings

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Nitrogen-Fixing Covers**
- **Crimson Clover**: Plant fall, harvest spring
- **Field Peas**: Cool season nitrogen production
- **Buckwheat**: Fast growing, attracts beneficial insects
- **Benefits**: Add 40-150 lbs nitrogen per acre

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Soil Building Covers**
- **Winter Rye**: Prevents erosion, adds organic matter
- **Crimson Clover**: Nitrogen fixation, soil aeration
- **Mustard**: Natural biofumigation, pest control
- **Management**: Plant after harvest, till before planting

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Native Plant Integration**
- **Pollinator Plants**: Flowers for beneficial insects
- **Habitat Strips**: Undisturbed areas for overwintering
- **Diverse Plantings**: Avoid monocultures
- **Seasonal Blooms**: Flowers throughout growing season

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Natural Predator Support**
- **Ladybugs**: Aphid control, provide pollen sources
- **Lacewings**: General pest control, avoid broad pesticides
- **Parasitic Wasps**: Caterpillar control, plant diverse flowers
- **Ground Beetles**: Soil pest control, maintain ground cover

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Physical Controls**
- **Row Covers**: Exclude flying pests during vulnerable periods
- **Copper Barriers**: Slug and snail control
- **Sticky Traps**: Monitor and control flying pests
- **Hand Picking**: Remove large pests and egg masses

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Biological Controls**
- **Bacillus thuringiensis**: Caterpillar control
- **Neem Oil**: Soft-bodied insect control
- **Diatomaceous Earth**: Crawling insect control
- **Beneficial Nematodes**: Soil grub control

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Year 1: Foundation Investment**
**Initial Costs: $2,000-3,500**
- **Infrastructure**: Raised beds, tools, basic equipment
- **Soil Preparation**: Compost, amendments
- **Seeds/Plants**: First year's planting
- **Basic Preservation**: Canner, dehydrator

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
**Expected Production**: $800-1,200 value
**Net Cost**: $800-2,700

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Year 2-3: Expansion Phase**
**Annual Costs: $500-800**
- **Seeds/Plants**: Annual replacements
- **Soil Amendments**: Compost, fertilizers
- **Equipment Additions**: Specialized tools
- **Preservation Supplies**: Jars, lids

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
**Expected Production**: $1,500-2,200 value
**Net Benefit**: $700-1,700 annually

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Year 4+: Mature Production**
**Annual Costs: $300-500**
- **Seeds/Plants**: Reduced as perennials mature
- **Maintenance**: Tool replacement, amendments
- **Preservation**: Ongoing supplies

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
**Expected Production**: $2,000-3,000 value
**Net Benefit**: $1,500-2,700 annually

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **5-Year Financial Summary**
- **Total Investment**: $5,000-7,000
- **Total Production Value**: $8,000-12,000
- **Net Benefit**: $3,000-5,000
- **Payback Period**: 2-3 years
- **Ongoing Annual ROI**: 30-50%

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
#### **Non-Financial Benefits**
- **Food Security**: Independence from supply chains
- **Nutrition Quality**: Fresh, organic produce
- **Physical Activity**: Healthy outdoor exercise
- **Mental Health**: Stress relief, connection to nature
- **Skill Development**: Valuable self-sufficiency skills

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Beginner Goals (Year 1)**
- [ ] **25% Fresh Vegetables**: Provide fresh vegetables 3 months/year
- [ ] **Basic Preservation**: Learn 2-3 preservation methods
- [ ] **Soil Building**: Establish composting system
- [ ] **Season Extension**: Build 1-2 cold frames

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Intermediate Goals (Year 2-3)**
- [ ] **50% Vegetable Needs**: Meet half of annual vegetable needs
- [ ] **Protein Production**: Add small livestock (chickens/rabbits)
- [ ] **Orchard Establishment**: Plant fruit trees
- [ ] **Advanced Preservation**: Master all preservation methods

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Advanced Goals (Year 4+)**
- [ ] **75% Food Production**: Produce majority of household food
- [ ] **Surplus Generation**: Produce excess for trading/selling
- [ ] **Seed Saving**: Maintain seed stock for independence
- [ ] **Community Integration**: Share knowledge and resources

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Planning Mistakes**
 **Overplanting First Year** - Start small and expand gradually
 **Ignoring Soil Health** - Invest in soil before expecting production
 **No Succession Planting** - Plan for continuous harvests
 **Inadequate Water Access** - Ensure reliable irrigation

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Production Mistakes**
 **Monoculture Planting** - Diversify for pest/disease resistance
 **Poor Timing** - Follow local planting calendars
 **Inadequate Spacing** - Follow spacing recommendations
 **Neglecting Maintenance** - Regular weeding and care essential

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Preservation Mistakes**
 **Inadequate Equipment** - Invest in proper preservation tools
 **Poor Storage Conditions** - Control temperature and humidity
 **No Processing Plan** - Plan preservation during growing season
 **Safety Shortcuts** - Follow food safety guidelines strictly

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Low Production Issues**
**Problem**: Poor yields despite good care
- **Cause**: Poor soil, inadequate sunlight, wrong timing
- **Solution**: Soil test, relocate beds, adjust planting schedule

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Pest Problems**
**Problem**: Significant crop damage from pests
- **Cause**: Lack of beneficial habitat, poor plant health
- **Solution**: Diversify plantings, improve soil health, IPM

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Preservation Failures**
**Problem**: Food spoilage during storage
- **Cause**: Improper technique, poor storage conditions
- **Solution**: Review methods, improve storage environment

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
### **Seasonal Planning Issues**
**Problem**: Food gaps between harvests
- **Cause**: Poor succession planting, inadequate preservation
- **Solution**: Stagger plantings, increase preservation capacity

[src: long_term_sustainability/local_food_production_systems.md]
*"The best time to plant a tree was 20 years ago. The second best time is now. The same applies to food production systems."*

[src: vulnerable_people/vulnerable_people_guide.md]
This guide covers the specific needs of children, the elderly, and pets during a disaster. A community is only as strong as its most vulnerable members.

[src: vulnerable_people/vulnerable_people_guide.md]
* **Involve Them in Planning:** Talk to children about the family disaster plan in an age-appropriate and non-frightening way. Let them help pack their own small "go bag" with their favorite toys, books, and snacks.
* **Know Their Information:** Make sure children have a physical copy of important contact information, including an out-of-state contact. Teach them how and when to call 911.
* **Comfort and Routine:** In the stressful environment of a disaster, maintaining a sense of routine is critical for children. Try to stick to regular meal times and bedtimes as much as possible. Comfort items like a favorite stuffed animal or blanket are essential.

[src: vulnerable_people/vulnerable_people_guide.md]
* **Support Network:** Establish a support network of friends, family, and neighbors who can check on elderly individuals during an emergency. At least one person in the network should have a key to their home.
* **Medical Needs:**
 * Keep a detailed, written list of all medications, dosages, and allergies.
 * Have at least a one-week supply of all necessary medications.
 * If medications require refrigeration, have a plan with a small cooler and ice packs.
* **Mobility:** Plan for transportation in case of an evacuation, especially if they have mobility issues. Know the evacuation plan of their assisted living facility, if applicable.
* **Emergency Kit:** In addition to the standard items, their kit should include:
 * Extra batteries for hearing aids.
 * Spare glasses.
 * Any necessary assistive devices.

[src: vulnerable_people/vulnerable_people_guide.md]
* **Evacuate Together:** If it is not safe for you, it is not safe for your pets. **Never leave your pets behind.**
* **Plan Your Destination:** Most emergency shelters do not accept pets. Identify pet-friendly hotels, boarding facilities, or friends and family outside your immediate area where you can take your pets.
* **Identification:** Make sure your pets are wearing collars with up-to-date ID tags. A microchip is highly recommended.
* **Pet Emergency Kit:**
 * Food and water for at least 3 days.
 * A sturdy leash, harness, and carrier.
 * Medications and a copy of their medical records in a waterproof bag.
 * A current photo of you with your pet (to prove ownership).
* **After a Disaster:** Keep pets on a leash and close to you. Their familiar environment may be gone, and they can easily become disoriented.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
In a disaster, the failure of water and sanitation systems can lead to the rapid spread of disease. This guide covers the essentials of maintaining sanitation and hygiene to keep your family healthy.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
When the sewer system is down, you must have a way to safely dispose of human waste.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
This is the simplest and most effective solution.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
* **You Will Need:**
 * Two 5-gallon buckets with tight-fitting lids.
 * A toilet seat that can snap onto a bucket (optional, but recommended for comfort).
 * Carbon material: Sawdust, wood shavings, shredded newspaper, or even dry leaves.
 * Heavy-duty trash bags.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
* **Setup and Use:**
 1. **Bucket #1 (Urine):** This bucket is for urine only. Urine is sterile and can be safely poured out in a designated spot away from your main living area.
 2. **Bucket #2 (Feces):**
 * Line the bucket with a trash bag.
 * After each use, completely cover the waste with a generous layer of your carbon material. This is the most important step. The carbon material helps to absorb moisture, reduce odor, and begin the composting process.
 * Keep the lid on tight when not in use.
 3. **Disposal:** When the bucket is full, tie off the bag securely. Store it in a designated, secure area away from your living space until normal waste collection services resume.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
**IMPORTANT:** Never mix urine and feces. This is what creates dangerous pathogens and strong odors.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
Staying clean is crucial for preventing skin infections and other illnesses.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
* **Handwashing:**
 * Hand sanitizer (at least 60% alcohol) is a good option when water is scarce.
 * You can create a simple handwashing station with a water container with a spigot, a bucket to catch the runoff, and soap.
* **Bathing:**
 * A "sponge bath" with a washcloth and a small amount of water is very effective.
 * Unscented baby wipes are excellent for quick clean-ups.
* **Dental Care:** Continue to brush your teeth. Use bottled or purified water.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
Unmanaged trash will attract pests and spread disease.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
* **Separate Your Waste:** Separate wet food scraps from dry trash.
* **Secure Storage:** Store all garbage in sturdy bags in containers with tight-fitting lids. If possible, keep the containers in a garage or shed, away from your main living area.
* **Burying (If Necessary):** If waste collection is suspended for a long time, you may need to bury your garbage. Choose a location that is at least 200 feet away from any water source.

[src: sanitation_and_hygiene/sanitation_and_hygiene_guide.md]
By managing waste and maintaining personal hygiene, you can create a healthier and safer environment for your family during a prolonged urban disaster.

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
# Emergency Preparedness Cheat Sheets
## Printable Quick Reference Cards

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**Print these pages and keep them with your emergency kit!**

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
1. **Print this document**
2. **Cut along dotted lines** (or keep full page)
3. **Laminate if possible** (or use clear plastic bag)
4. **Store in emergency kit**
5. **Keep copy in wallet/phone**

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**Why paper?** Your phone will die. Paper works always.

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #1: Water Purification Quick Guide
### Emergency Water Treatment Methods

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BOILING (Most Reliable)**
- Bring to rolling boil: 1 minute
- Above 6,500 ft elevation: 3 minutes
- Kills: ALL bacteria, viruses, protozoa
- Does NOT remove: Chemicals, sediment

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BLEACH (Cheap & Effective)**
- Use UNSCENTED bleach only (5-9% sodium hypochlorite)
- CLEAR water: 8 drops (1/8 tsp) per gallon
- CLOUDY water: 16 drops (1/4 tsp) per gallon
- Mix well, wait 30 minutes
- Should smell slightly of chlorine
- If no smell, repeat dose and wait 15 min
- Does NOT remove: Sediment, chemicals

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**TABLETS**
- Iodine or chlorine dioxide
- Follow package directions (usually 1 tab/liter)
- Wait 30 minutes minimum
- May need taste-neutralizer tablet

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SOLAR DISINFECTION (SODIS)**
- Clear plastic bottles only
- Fill with clear water
- Lay in direct sunlight
- 6 hours (sunny) or 2 days (cloudy)
- FREE method!

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**FILTERS**
- Personal (LifeStraw): 1,000-4,000 liters
- Gravity (bag): 2-10 liters per fill
- Ceramic (Berkey): 1,000-5,000 gallons
- Check filter specs for what it removes

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**WATER NEEDS**
- Minimum: 1 gallon/person/day
- Comfortable: 2-5 gallons/person/day
- 72 hours: 3 gallons minimum
- 2 weeks: 14 gallons minimum

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**EMERGENCY SOURCES**
- Water heater tank: 30-50 gallons
- Toilet tank (NOT bowl): 2-3 gallons
- Pipes: Drain from lowest point
- Ice cubes: Melt for water
- Canned vegetables: Liquid is safe

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #2: Calorie & Nutrition Quick Guide
### Daily Food Requirements

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**CALORIE NEEDS**
- Adult Male: 2,000-2,500 calories/day
- Adult Female: 1,800-2,200 calories/day
- Teenager (13-18): 2,200-2,800 calories/day
- Child (6-12): 1,600-2,000 calories/day
- Child (under 6): 1,000-1,400 calories/day
- Heavy activity: Add 500-1,000 calories
- Pregnancy: Add 300 calories
- Nursing: Add 500 calories

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SURVIVAL MINIMUM**
- Short-term (days): 800-1,200 calories/day
- Long-term: 1,500-2,000 calories/day minimum
- Below 1,200: Risk of malnutrition

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**MACRONUTRIENTS**
- Carbohydrates: 45-65% of calories
- Protein: 10-35% of calories
- Fats: 20-35% of calories

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**CRITICAL VITAMINS/MINERALS**
- Vitamin C: 75-90 mg/day (scurvy prevention)
- Vitamin A: 700-900 mcg/day (eyesight)
- Iron: 8-18 mg/day (anemia prevention)
- Calcium: 1,000-1,300 mg/day (bones)
- B Vitamins: Varies (energy, nerves)
- Zinc: 8-11 mg/day (immune system)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**EMERGENCY FOOD PRIORITIES**
1. Carbs (energy): Rice, pasta, oats, flour
2. Protein: Beans, canned meat, peanut butter
3. Fats: Cooking oil, nuts, seeds
4. Salt: Critical for survival
5. Vitamins: Multivitamin supplement
6. Variety: Prevents deficiency

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SHELF LIFE**
- Honey: Indefinite
- Rice (white): 30 years
- Dried beans: 30 years
- Pasta: 30 years
- Flour: 5-10 years
- Canned goods: 2-5 years
- Dried fruit: 1-2 years

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #3: First Aid Quick Reference
### Emergency Medical Response

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**LIFE-THREATENING (Call 911)**
- Not breathing: Start CPR immediately
- Severe bleeding: Apply direct pressure
- Chest pain: Aspirin + call 911
- Allergic reaction: EpiPen + call 911
- Head injury + unconscious: Call 911
- Burns (large area): Cool + call 911

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**ABCs OF FIRST AID**
- **A**irway: Is it clear?
- **B**reathing: Are they breathing?
- **C**irculation: Do they have a pulse?

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BLEEDING**
- Direct pressure: Use clean cloth
- Elevate wound above heart
- Pressure points if needed
- Tourniquet: LAST RESORT only
- Don't remove impaled objects

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BURNS**
- Cool (not cold) water: 10-20 minutes
- Cover with clean, dry cloth
- DO NOT use ice
- DO NOT pop blisters
- Seek medical attention if severe

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**FRACTURES**
- Immobilize injured area
- Don't try to straighten
- Apply ice (20 min on, 20 min off)
- Elevate if possible
- Seek medical attention

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SHOCK**
- Lay person down
- Elevate feet 12 inches
- Keep warm with blanket
- DO NOT give food/water
- Monitor breathing

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**VITAL SIGNS (Normal)**
- Pulse: 60-100 beats/minute
- Breathing: 12-20 breaths/minute
- Temperature: 97-99 degF (36-37 degC)
- Blood pressure: 120/80 mmHg

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BASIC KIT CONTENTS**
- Bandages (various sizes)
- Gauze pads (44)
- Medical tape
- Antibiotic ointment
- Pain reliever (ibuprofen, acetaminophen)
- Tweezers, scissors
- Disposable gloves
- CPR face shield
- Thermometer
- Emergency blanket

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #4: Emergency Shelter & Temperature
### Survival in Extreme Conditions

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**RULE OF THREES**
- 3 minutes: Without air
- 3 hours: Without shelter (extreme weather)
- 3 days: Without water
- 3 weeks: Without food

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**HYPOTHERMIA PREVENTION**
- Stay dry (wet = cold)
- Layer clothing (base, mid, outer)
- Cover head (30% heat loss)
- Cover extremities (hands, feet)
- Stay active (generate heat)
- Eat high-calorie food
- Emergency blanket (reflects 90% heat)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**HYPOTHERMIA TREATMENT**
- Get out of cold/wind
- Remove wet clothing
- Warm core first (chest, neck, head)
- Warm drinks if conscious
- DO NOT rub extremities
- Seek medical attention

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**HEAT EXHAUSTION PREVENTION**
- Drink water frequently
- Rest in shade
- Light-colored clothing
- Wet clothing/bandana on neck
- Avoid midday activity

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**HEAT EXHAUSTION TREATMENT**
- Move to cool location
- Remove excess clothing
- Cool with water/fanning
- Drink water slowly
- Rest until recovered

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**EMERGENCY SHELTER PRIORITIES**
1. Protection from wind
2. Protection from precipitation
3. Insulation from ground
4. Heat retention

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**TARP SHELTER (5 minutes)**
- A-frame: String rope, drape tarp, stake corners
- Lean-to: One side staked high, other side low
- Diamond: Stake center, stretch corners

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**LAYERING SYSTEM**
- Base: Wicks moisture (NOT cotton)
- Mid: Insulates (fleece, down)
- Outer: Protects from wind/rain
- Adjust layers before you sweat

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]

### Complete Checklist

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**WATER (1 gal/person/day 3)**
 3 gallons per person

 Feminine products

 Multi-tool/knife

 Local maps
 Work gloves
 Plastic sheeting

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**DOCUMENTS & MONEY**
 ID copies (waterproof)
 Insurance documents
 Medical info/prescriptions
 Emergency contacts (written)
 Cash ($200-500 small bills)
 Credit cards

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SPECIAL ITEMS**
 Infant supplies (if applicable)
 Pet supplies (if applicable)
 Glasses (spare pair)
 Comfort items (photos, toys)

## CHEAT SHEET #7: Water Storage Quick Math
### How Much Do You Need?

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**BASIC CALCULATION**
- 1 gallon per person per day
- Multiply by number of days
- Multiply by number of people

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**EXAMPLES**
- 1 person 3 days = 3 gallons
- 4 people 3 days = 12 gallons
- 4 people 14 days = 56 gallons
- 4 people 30 days = 120 gallons

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**CONTAINER SIZES**
- 2-liter bottle = 0.5 gallon
- 5-gallon jug = 5 gallons (obviously)
- 55-gallon drum = 55 gallons
- Case of water (24 bottles) = 3 gallons
- Water heater tank = 30-50 gallons

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**STORAGE SPACE**
- 3 gallons = 12 lbs = 1 case water
- 14 gallons = 56 lbs = 3 five-gallon jugs
- 30 gallons = 120 lbs = 6 five-gallon jugs
- 55 gallons = 220 lbs = 1 drum (HEAVY!)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**QUICK CONVERSIONS**
- 1 gallon = 4 quarts = 8 pints = 16 cups
- 1 gallon = 3.78 liters
- 1 liter = 0.264 gallons
- 8 oz = 1 cup = 237 ml

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #8: Food Rotation Schedule
### Prevent Waste, Stay Fresh

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**ROTATION SYSTEM**
- FIFO: First In, First Out
- Date everything with marker
- Store new items in back
- Use oldest items first
- Check expiration dates monthly

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SHELF LIFE GUIDE**
**Indefinite (if stored properly):**
- White rice
- Honey
- Salt
- Sugar
- Dried beans
- Wheat (whole kernel)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**10-30 Years:**
- Freeze-dried meals
- Powdered milk
- Dried pasta
- Rolled oats

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**5-10 Years:**
- Canned meats
- Canned vegetables
- Canned fruits
- Flour (vacuum sealed)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**2-5 Years:**
- Canned goods (typical)
- Peanut butter
- Cooking oil
- Crackers (sealed)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**1-2 Years:**
- Energy bars
- Dried fruit
- Nuts
- Granola

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**6-12 Months:**
- Breakfast cereal
- Chips/snacks
- Coffee/tea

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**ROTATION STRATEGY**
1. Buy extra of what you eat
2. Date with marker
3. Use oldest first
4. Replace as you use
5. Check every 6 months

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**MONTHLY CHECK**
 Inspect for damage/rust
 Check expiration dates
 Rotate items
 Update inventory list
 Top off as needed

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #9: Emergency Signals
### Get Rescued Faster

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SOS (Morse Code):**
- ... --- ... (3 short, 3 long, 3 short)
- Flash with light
- Sound with whistle
- Any pattern of 3 = distress

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**GROUND-TO-AIR SIGNALS**
(Make large, visible from air)
- X = Need medical assistance
- F = Need food and water
- = Proceeding in this direction
- LL = All is well
- V = Need assistance
- Use rocks, branches, bright clothing

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**WHISTLE SIGNALS**
- 1 blast = "Where are you?"
- 2 blasts = "Come to me"
- 3 blasts = EMERGENCY
- Repeat every minute

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**MIRROR SIGNAL**
- Reflect sunlight at aircraft/rescuers
- Flash 3 times, pause, repeat
- Can be seen for miles
- Use any reflective surface

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**FIRE SIGNALS**
- 3 fires in triangle = distress
- Daytime: Add green branches for smoke
- Nighttime: Bright flames
- Space apart 100 feet

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**AUDIO SIGNALS**
- 3 of anything = distress
- 3 gunshots
- 3 shouts
- 3 whistle blasts
- 3 horn honks

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**VISUAL SIGNALS**
- Bright clothing waved
- Flashlight at night
- Reflective mirror
- Flares (if available)
- Orange smoke (daytime)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
## CHEAT SHEET #10: Medication Stockpile
### Essential OTC & Prescriptions

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**OVER-THE-COUNTER (OTC)**
 Pain/Fever: Ibuprofen, Acetaminophen
 Allergy: Diphenhydramine (Benadryl)
 Diarrhea: Loperamide (Imodium)
 Constipation: Docusate (Colace)
 Antacid: Tums, Pepto-Bismol
 Cough/Cold: Dextromethorphan
 Antibiotic ointment: Neosporin
 Anti-itch: Hydrocortisone cream
 Eye drops: Artificial tears

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**PRESCRIPTIONS**
 Minimum: 7-day supply in kit
 Ideal: 30-90 day supply at home
 Rotate to keep fresh
 Include dosage information
 Store properly (cool, dry, dark)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SPECIAL NEEDS**
 Insulin (if diabetic)
 EpiPen (if allergic)
 Inhaler (if asthmatic)
 Blood pressure meds
 Heart medications
 Seizure medications
 Mental health medications

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**SUPPLEMENTS (Optional)**
 Multivitamin (daily)
 Vitamin C (immune support)
 Vitamin D (bone health)
 Calcium (bone health)
 Zinc (immune support)
 Iron (if anemic)
 B-complex (energy)

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**STORAGE TIPS**
- Cool, dry, dark location
- Original containers with labels
- Check expiration dates
- Rotate as you use
- Keep list of allergies
- Document dosages

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**These cheat sheets are designed to:**
- Fit standard paper (8.511)
- Be cut into cards
- Be laminated for durability
- Work without electricity
- Provide critical information fast

[src: CHEAT_SHEETS.md]
**Storage suggestions:**
- Emergency kit (main copy)
- Vehicle (copy)
- Wallet (contact card)
- Work bag (copy)
- Give to family members

### NIST Special Publication 800-34
**Title:** Contingency Planning Guide for Federal Information Systems
**Source:** National Institute of Standards and Technology
**Type:** Government Publication (Free PDF)
**URL:**
**Description:** The primary U.S. government resource providing guidance for IT contingency planning. Includes templates, sample plans, and FAQs for developing effective Information System Contingency Plans (ISCPs).

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**Key Features:**
- Contingency planning policy statements
- Business impact analysis guidance
- Preventive controls identification
- Recovery strategies for IT systems
- Aligns with ISO 22301, FFIEC, and NIST SP 800-53

**Type:** Free/Open Source Software

**License:** GPL

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
### Basic Emergency Kit Components (FEMA Recommendations)

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**Water:**
- 1 gallon per person per day
- Minimum 3-day supply (72 hours)
- Recommend 2-week supply

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**Food:**
- 3-day supply of non-perishable food minimum
- Consider dietary needs and preferences

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**Additional Items:**
- Battery-powered or hand crank radio
- NOAA Weather Radio
- Flashlight
- First aid kit
- Extra batteries
- Whistle (to signal for help)
- Dust masks or cloth face coverings
- Soap and hand sanitizer
- Prescription medications
- Non-prescription medications (pain relievers, etc.)
- Infant formula and supplies (if applicable)
- Pet food and supplies (if applicable)
- Cash or traveler's checks
- Important family documents (copies)
- Sleeping bags or warm blankets
- Complete change of clothing
- Fire extinguisher
- Matches in waterproof container

#### Ham Radio Emergency Frequencies List
**URL:**
**Type:** Free PDF List
**Description:** Popular ham radio emergency frequencies and their uses.

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**ARES (Amateur Radio Emergency Service)**
- Established: 1935
- Membership: Licensed amateur radio operators
- Function: Volunteer emergency communications
- Registration: Voluntary with local emergency management

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**RACES (Radio Amateur Civil Emergency Service)**
- Established: 1952
- Function: Standby Civil Defense radio service
- Integration: Works with government agencies

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**AUXCOMM (Auxiliary Communications)**
- Function: Supports emergency communications
- Training: FEMA Independent Study courses (IS-100, IS-700)

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
### Why HAM Radio?
- Independent of terrestrial infrastructure
- Functions when commercial systems fail
- No dependency on cell towers or internet
- Direct peer-to-peer communication
- Wide area coverage capability

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
### Training Resources
**FEMA Independent Study Courses:**
- IS-100: Introduction to Incident Command System
- IS-700: National Incident Management System (NIMS)
- Often required for ARES, RACES, AUXCOMM participation

Many organizations offer free or low-cost certifications:

- FEMA Independent Study Courses
- CERT Basic Training
- ICS/NIMS Training
- Amateur Radio License (examination fee only)

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
1. **Assessment Phase:**
 - Identify your specific disaster recovery needs
 - Determine applicable regulatory requirements
 - Assess organizational capabilities and resources

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
2. **Planning Phase:**
 - Download relevant templates and guides
 - Customize for your specific context
 - Engage stakeholders in planning process
 - Align with industry standards and frameworks

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
3. **Implementation Phase:**
 - Deploy selected tools and software
 - Train staff and volunteers
 - Document procedures and contacts
 - Establish communication protocols

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
4. **Testing Phase:**
 - Conduct regular drills and exercises
 - Test backup and recovery systems
 - Validate communication channels
 - Update plans based on lessons learned

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
5. **Maintenance Phase:**
 - Review and update plans annually
 - Monitor for new threats and hazards
 - Stay informed about new resources
 - Maintain training and certifications

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
- **Layer Your Defenses:** Combine multiple approaches (IT systems, physical preparedness, training)
- **Regular Testing:** Test plans and systems regularly, don't wait for actual disasters
- **Community Integration:** Participate in local emergency management programs
- **Documentation:** Keep both digital and physical copies of critical plans
- **Communication:** Establish multiple communication channels and backup methods
- **Training:** Ensure key personnel are trained and certified
- **Updates:** Review and update plans after every drill and actual incident
- **Coordination:** Align with local, state, and federal emergency management frameworks

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
- **HIPAA:** Healthcare organizations must comply with specific security and privacy rules
- **NIST:** Federal agencies and contractors should follow NIST guidelines
- **ISO Standards:** Consider ISO 22301 (Business Continuity) and ISO 27031 (ICT Readiness)
- **Industry-Specific:** Follow industry-specific regulations (financial, education, etc.)
- **Local Requirements:** Check state and local emergency management requirements

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
This is an open research compilation. To suggest additions:
1. Ensure resources are publicly accessible and free/open source
2. Verify links are current and functional
3. Provide clear descriptions and context
4. Indicate any licensing or usage restrictions
5. Note the date of resource publication or last update

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
This library is compiled for educational and preparedness purposes. While all resources listed are publicly accessible and free/open source at the time of compilation, users should:

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
- Verify current availability of resources
- Ensure compliance with applicable laws and regulations
- Seek professional advice for specific situations
- Test and validate plans before relying on them
- Keep resources and plans updated
- Understand licensing terms for software and templates

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
The maintainers of this library make no warranties about the completeness, reliability, or accuracy of this information. Disaster recovery and emergency preparedness are complex fields requiring professional expertise.

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
- **Version 1.0** (November 11, 2025): Initial compilation of disaster recovery resources covering IT systems, natural disasters, medical/first aid, emergency supplies, open source software, business continuity, healthcare, community response, schools, communications, international humanitarian response, pandemic preparedness, and climate adaptation.

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
For the most current versions of these resources, always check the official URLs provided. Many government agencies and organizations update their materials regularly to reflect new threats, technologies, and best practices.

[src: DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md]
**Key Update Schedules:**
- NIST Publications: Reviewed every 3-5 years
- FEMA Frameworks: Updated as needed, major revisions periodically
- WHO/UN Resources: Updated based on global health situations
- Software Projects: Check GitHub for latest releases
- Templates: Verify date stamps and look for newer versions

[src: INDEX.md]
### Comprehensive Reference
- [ Complete Resource Library](DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md) - All resources organized by category

[src: INDEX.md]
Different disaster scenarios require different levels of preparedness:

[src: INDEX.md]
### 72 Hours (Immediate Emergency)
**Scenarios:** Natural disasters, sudden evacuations, power outages
**Focus:** Survival basics - water, food, shelter, first aid
**Portability:** High - must be grab-and-go ready
**Key Question:** "Can I survive for 3 days without outside help?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### 2 Weeks (Extended Emergency)
**Scenarios:** Major storms, regional disasters, supply chain disruptions
**Focus:** Extended supplies, sanitation, communication
**Portability:** Medium - some items stored at home
**Key Question:** "Can my household function for 2 weeks independently?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### 1 Month (Short-Term Self-Sufficiency)
**Scenarios:** Economic disruption, prolonged utility failures, pandemics
**Focus:** Food rotation, water storage, basic medical care
**Portability:** Low - primarily home storage
**Key Question:** "Can we maintain health and sanitation for 30 days?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### 3-6 Months (Medium-Term Sustainability)
**Scenarios:** Economic collapse, extended supply chain failure, long-term displacement
**Focus:** Food variety, nutrition balance, mental health, skill development
**Portability:** None - established home or retreat storage
**Key Question:** "Can we sustain ourselves with minimal external resources?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### 1 Year (Long-Term Preparedness)
**Scenarios:** Major economic depression, prolonged civil unrest, environmental catastrophe
**Focus:** Bulk staples, gardening, preservation, self-sufficiency skills
**Portability:** None - requires significant storage space
**Key Question:** "Can we produce and preserve our own resources?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### Multi-Year (Post-Apocalyptic/Societal Collapse)
**Scenarios:** Complete infrastructure failure, extreme climate change, societal breakdown
**Focus:** Agriculture, renewable energy, community building, complete self-sufficiency
**Portability:** None - permanent location with land
**Key Question:** "Can we rebuild sustainable living systems?"

[src: INDEX.md]
### Water
- **72hrs:** 1 gallon/person/day (3 gallons minimum)
- **2 weeks:** 14 gallons/person + purification methods
- **1 month:** 30 gallons/person + storage rotation + purification
- **3-6 months:** Water source identification, advanced filtration
- **1 year:** Well/spring access, rain catchment systems
- **Multi-year:** Sustainable water source, irrigation systems

[src: INDEX.md]
### Food (Calories per day: 1,200-2,400 depending on age/activity)
- **72hrs:** Ready-to-eat meals, no cooking required
- **2 weeks:** Mix of fresh, canned, and dry goods
- **1 month:** Canned goods, rice, pasta, rotation system
- **3-6 months:** Bulk staples, variety for nutrition, comfort foods
- **1 year:** Bulk wheat, beans, rice, salt, cooking oil
- **Multi-year:** Gardening, seed saving, food production

[src: INDEX.md]
### Shelter
- **72hrs:** Emergency blankets, tarp, portable shelter
- **2 weeks:** Home winterization, backup heating/cooling
- **1 month:** Insulation, weatherproofing, temperature control
- **3-6 months:** Alternative heating, structural repairs
- **1 year:** Sustainable heating/cooling solutions
- **Multi-year:** Off-grid housing, renewable systems

[src: INDEX.md]
### Sanitation
- **72hrs:** Portable toilet, toilet paper, hand sanitizer
- **2 weeks:** Waste disposal system, hygiene supplies
- **1 month:** Composting toilet, greywater system
- **3-6 months:** Established sanitation routine, soap making
- **1 year:** Sustainable hygiene production
- **Multi-year:** Complete waste management system

[src: INDEX.md]
### Medical
- **72hrs:** First aid kit, basic medications
- **2 weeks:** Extended medical supplies, prescription stockpile
- **1 month:** Comprehensive first aid, dental supplies
- **3-6 months:** Natural remedies, medical skill development
- **1 year:** Medicinal herb garden, advanced medical training
- **Multi-year:** Traditional medicine, injury prevention

[src: INDEX.md]
### Energy
- **72hrs:** Batteries, flashlights, hand-crank radio
- **2 weeks:** Battery bank, small solar charger
- **1 month:** Generator, fuel storage
- **3-6 months:** Solar panels, battery backup
- **1 year:** Off-grid power system
- **Multi-year:** Renewable energy infrastructure

[src: INDEX.md]
### Clothing & Temperature
- **72hrs:** Weather-appropriate clothing, emergency blankets
- **2 weeks:** Layering system, backup clothing
- **1 month:** Seasonal clothing rotation, repair supplies
- **3-6 months:** Clothing repair/modification skills
- **1 year:** Textile crafting, preservation techniques
- **Multi-year:** Textile production (spinning, weaving)

[src: INDEX.md]
### Nutrition (Preventing Deficiencies)
- **72hrs:** Balanced emergency rations
- **2 weeks:** Multivitamins, varied food sources
- **1 month:** Vitamin C, calcium, iron supplementation
- **3-6 months:** Diverse food storage, fermented foods
- **1 year:** Complete nutrition planning, sprouting
- **Multi-year:** Diverse agriculture, animal products

[src: INDEX.md]

- FEMA (Federal Emergency Management Agency)
- NIST (National Institute of Standards and Technology)
- CDC (Centers for Disease Control)
- USDA (Food preservation, gardening)

- State emergency management agencies
- WHO (World Health Organization)
- UN OCHA (Humanitarian coordination)

3. Progress to [2 Weeks](TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md) after completing 72-hour kit
4. Continue building timeline by timeline

[src: INDEX.md]
### For Specific Needs
1. Identify your immediate concern (water, food, medical, etc.)
2. Navigate to the relevant topic guide
3. Download applicable templates and resources
4. Cross-reference with appropriate timeline

[src: INDEX.md]
### For Comprehensive Planning
1. Review all timeline guides
2. Create a spreadsheet tracking your progress
3. Set goals for each timeline phase
4. Review topic guides for detailed implementation

[src: INDEX.md]
### For Community/Business
1. Start with [Complete Resource Library](DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md)
2. Review FEMA and NIST frameworks
3. Download business continuity templates
4. Customize for your specific needs

[src: INDEX.md]
### Family Planning
- **Infants:** Formula, diapers, baby food (see [Food Guide](TOPIC_FOOD.md))
- **Elderly:** Medications, mobility aids, special dietary needs
- **Pets:** Food, water, medications, carriers
- **Medical Conditions:** Extended medication supply, medical equipment

[src: INDEX.md]
### Climate Considerations
- **Cold Climates:** Heating, insulation, cold-weather clothing
- **Hot Climates:** Cooling, sun protection, increased water needs
- **Humid Climates:** Mold prevention, moisture control, food preservation
- **Arid Climates:** Water conservation, dust protection

[src: INDEX.md]
### Location Type
- **Urban:** Limited space, apartment-friendly solutions, community resources
- **Suburban:** Moderate space, vehicle access, neighborhood cooperation
- **Rural:** Land access, self-sufficiency, isolation challenges

[src: INDEX.md]
### Immediate Actions (Do First)
- [ ] Create 72-hour kit for each family member
- [ ] Store 3 days of water (1 gallon/person/day)
- [ ] Assemble basic first aid kit
- [ ] Document important papers (copies)
- [ ] Establish family communication plan
- [ ] Learn CPR and basic first aid

[src: INDEX.md]

- [ ] Build 2-week food supply
- [ ] Water purification method
- [ ] Emergency sanitation plan
- [ ] Backup power (batteries/solar)
- [ ] Medical supply stockpile
- [ ] Community connection (CERT, neighbors)

[src: INDEX.md]
### Medium-Term Goals (3-12 Months)
- [ ] 1-month food supply
- [ ] Water storage system
- [ ] Alternative heating/cooling
- [ ] Comprehensive medical kit
- [ ] Emergency communication (ham radio)
- [ ] Food preservation skills

[src: INDEX.md]
### Long-Term Goals (1+ Years)
- [ ] 3-6 month food supply
- [ ] Sustainable water source
- [ ] Off-grid power capability
- [ ] Garden and food production
- [ ] Seed bank
- [ ] Advanced survival skills

- Cross-references (related guides)
- Return links (navigation)

- Safety warnings

- Real-world examples

# Disaster Recovery Library - File Structure & Organization

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
This library is organized into **three navigation systems** to help you find exactly what you need:

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
1. **Timeline-Based Guides** - Progress from immediate (72hr) to long-term (years)
2. **Topic-Specific Guides** - Deep dives into specific subjects
3. **Comprehensive Reference** - Complete resource compilation

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
```
disaster/
 INDEX.md # Master navigation hub START HERE
 README.md # Repository overview
 LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md # This file - organization guide

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
DISASTER_RECOVERY_RESEARCH_LIBRARY.md # Complete resource compilation

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md # Created - Immediate emergency (3 days)
 TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md # Extended emergency
 TIMELINE_1_MONTH.md # Short-term self-sufficiency
 TIMELINE_3_6_MONTHS.md # Medium-term sustainability
 TIMELINE_1_YEAR.md # Long-term preparedness
 TIMELINE_LONG_TERM.md # Multi-year/post-apocalyptic

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
TOPIC_WATER.md # Created - Water: purification, storage, sources
 TOPIC_FOOD.md # Food: storage, preservation, calories
 TOPIC_SHELTER.md # Shelter: construction, temperature
 TOPIC_SANITATION.md # Sanitation: hygiene, waste management
 TOPIC_MEDICAL.md # Medical: first aid, nutrition, medicine
 TOPIC_ENERGY.md # Energy: solar, wind, off-grid
 TOPIC_AGRICULTURE.md # Agriculture: gardening, seeds, livestock
 TOPIC_CLOTHING.md # Clothing: layering, temperature
 TOPIC_IT_SYSTEMS.md # IT: data recovery, backups
 TOPIC_COMMUNICATIONS.md # Communications: radio, alerts
```

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]

TIMELINE_3_6_MONTHS.md (develop sustainability)

```

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
### Government Resources
- **FEMA** - Disaster recovery frameworks, templates, guides
- **NIST** - IT contingency planning, cybersecurity
- **CDC** - Health emergencies, sanitation, disease prevention
- **USDA** - Food preservation, gardening, nutrition
- **Ready.gov** - Family preparedness, checklists
- **State/Local** - Regional emergency management
- **EPA** - Water safety, environmental hazards
- **WHO** - International health emergencies
- **UN OCHA** - Humanitarian assistance

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
### Open Source Software
- **Sahana Eden** - Emergency management platform
- **Ushahidi** - Crisis mapping
- **DRLM** - Linux disaster recovery
- **Backup Tools** - Bacula, Restic, Velero, Duplicity

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
### Non-Profit Organizations
- **American Red Cross** - Preparedness guides, first aid
- **Salvation Army** - Disaster services
- **Amateur Radio Emergency Service (ARES)** - Communications
- **Community CERT** - Emergency response training

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
### Educational Institutions
- **University Extensions** - Food preservation, gardening
- **National Center for Home Food Preservation** - Canning, preserving
- **State Universities** - Regional preparedness resources

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]

[src: LIBRARY_STRUCTURE.md]
**Time:** Several days for complete plan
