[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
# Location-Specific Disaster Preparedness Guide
## Urban vs Suburban vs Rural vs Mountain Environments

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Critical Differences in Preparedness Based on Location**

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
1. [Quick Comparison Matrix](#quick-comparison-matrix)
2. [Urban Environment](#urban-environment-citiesapartments)
3. [Suburban Environment](#suburban-environment-single-family-homes)
4. [Rural Environment](#rural-environment-countryside)
5. [Mountain Environment](#mountain-environment-high-altitude)
6. [Location-Specific Checklists](#location-specific-checklists)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
| Factor | Urban | Suburban | Rural | Mountain |
|--------|-------|----------|-------|----------|
| **Population Density** | Very High | Medium | Very Low | Very Low |
| **Space Availability** | Very Limited | Moderate | Abundant | Variable |
| **Water Access** | Municipal (vulnerable) | Municipal (less vulnerable) | Well/spring common | Spring/creek common |
| **Food Storage** | Limited | Good | Excellent | Good |
| **Gardening** | Container only | Yard possible | Large gardens | Short season |
| **Emergency Services** | Minutes | 10-20 min | 30+ min | 1+ hours |
| **Crowd Risk** | Very High | Medium | Low | Very Low |
| **Resource Scarcity** | Rapid (hours) | Fast (1-2 days) | Slow (days) | Isolated |
| **Self-Sufficiency** | Very Difficult | Possible | Easier | Required |
| **Evacuation** | Congested | Moderate | Easy | Difficult terrain |
| **Community Support** | Anonymous | Neighborly | Close-knit | Isolated/close-knit |
| **Climate Control** | Building dependent | Home control | Property control | Extreme weather |
| **Fuel Access** | Difficult storage | Good storage | Excellent storage | Critical need |

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
## Urban Environment (Cities/Apartments)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Space Constraints:**
- Limited storage (typically 500-1000 sq ft living space)
- No yard/land for gardening
- Vertical living (stairs if power out)
- Shared walls/neighbors

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Infrastructure Dependence:**
- 100% reliant on municipal water
- Electric-only heating/cooling common
- No alternative power feasible (solar)
- No well/septic backup

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Security & Crowds:**
- High population density = rapid resource depletion
- Stores empty in hours during crisis
- Crime risk increases faster
- Civil unrest more likely
- Anonymous neighbors (less community)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Mobility Issues:**
- Traffic gridlock during evacuation
- Public transit dependent
- Walking distances to resources
- Limited parking for supplies

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Proximity to Services:**
- Multiple stores within blocks
- Hospitals nearby
- Fire/police response fast (normally)
- Delivery services available

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Community Resources:**
- Community centers
- Public shelters
- Food banks
- Churches/nonprofits

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Building Features:**
- Climate controlled
- Secure entry
- Neighbors nearby (if friendly)
- Often concrete/fire resistant

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage Challenge:** No space for 55-gallon drums

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Solutions:**
- **Water bricks** (3.5 gal, stackable) - under bed, closet
- **Collapsible containers** - fill when needed
- **Bathtub reservoir** (WaterBOB) - 100 gallons in tub when crisis imminent
- **Case water** - rotate in small closet
- **2-liter bottles** - fill empty space (under sinks, top of cabinets)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Quantities (Urban):**
- Minimum: 3 days (3 gal/person) - absolutely required
- Better: 1 week (7 gal/person) - challenging but doable
- Ideal: 2 weeks (14 gal/person) - requires creative storage

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Purification (Essential):**
- **Portable filter** (LifeStraw, Sawyer) - can use any water source
- **Bleach** - small bottle treats thousands of gallons
- **Tablets** - backup method
- Know nearby water sources: pools, fountains, rivers (last resort)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Urban Water Sources in Emergency:**
- Building water heater: 30-50 gallons
- Toilet tanks (NOT bowls): 2-3 gal per toilet
- Pipes: drain from lowest point
- Swimming pools (purify!)
- Decorative fountains (purify!)
- Rivers/lakes (if accessible)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage Locations:**
- Under bed (bins with wheels)
- Top of cabinets
- Closet floor/shelves
- Behind furniture
- Vertical shelving units

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Best Foods for Urban:**
- **Calorie-dense:** Peanut butter, nuts, cooking oil
- **Stackable:** Canned goods, ramen, pasta
- **Compact:** Freeze-dried meals, energy bars
- **Multi-use:** Rice, beans, flour
- **Long shelf life:** Honey, salt, sugar, white rice

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Urban Food Strategy:**
1. **Rotate pantry** - buy extra of what you eat
2. **Vertical storage** - shelving units to ceiling
3. **Under-bed bins** - 20-30 gallon containers
4. **Vacuum seal** - reduce bulk (rice, beans, pasta)
5. **Prioritize calories per cubic inch**

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Cooking Without Power:**
- **Camp stove** (propane/butane) - use ONLY with ventilation near window
- **Sterno** - canned heat, safer indoors
- **NO charcoal/wood indoors** - carbon monoxide DEATH
- **Cold meals** - many foods edible without cooking
- **Building courtyard** - if available, outdoor cooking

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Heating Without Power (DANGER ZONE):**
- **NEVER** use charcoal, gas stove, generator indoors = CO DEATH
- **Safe options:**
 - Layer clothing (base, mid, outer)
 - Emergency blankets
 - Sleeping bags
 - Close off one room, body heat
 - Buddy system (share body heat)
- **Building heat** - if concrete/brick, retains heat longer
- **Neighbors** - can share heat/space if friendly

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Cooling Without AC:**
- **Wet towels** over fans (if power)
- **Cold water** on wrists, neck
- **Ventilation** - cross-breeze through windows
- **Dark clothing** off windows
- **Night cooling** - open windows at night, close during day
- **Public cooling centers** - libraries, malls (if open)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Window Security:**
- **Ground floor:** Bars, security film, boards
- **Curtains** - privacy is security
- **Don't advertise** - keep supplies hidden

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Toilet Options:**
- **Bucket toilet** - 5-gallon bucket + bags + kitty litter
- **Camping toilet** - portable seats available
- **Trash bag system** - double-bag, seal, dispose when able
- **Building** - check if rooftop/courtyard accessible

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Waste Disposal:**
- **Short-term:** Seal in bags, store in bathroom
- **If building plumbing works:** May flush with bucket of water
- **Trash chutes:** If available, may work
- **DO NOT** put in neighbor's area - antisocial and illegal

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Hygiene:**
- **Wet wipes** - primary cleaning method
- **Hand sanitizer** - when water scarce
- **Bucket baths** - 2 gallons warm water sufficient
- **No-rinse soap** - camping soap varieties

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**No Solar Possible** (most apartments prohibit):
- **Portable battery packs** - charge before crisis
- **Car charging** - if you have vehicle
- **Power banks** - multiple units
- **Hand-crank** - radio, flashlight
- **Candles** - light only (fire risk!)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Generator:**
- **NOT in apartment** - CO death risk
- **NOT on balcony** - still CO risk
- **Shared outdoor space** - maybe if allowed and ventilated

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Multi-Unit Building Concerns:**
- **Noise control** - avoid attention
- **Cooking smells** - attracted unwanted attention
- **Light discipline** - don't show you have power
- **Door security** - reinforce, deadbolts, security bar
- **Escape route** - fire escape, alternate exits
- **Neighbors** - know who's friendly, who's threat

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Operational Security (OPSEC):**
- **Don't advertise** - no prep talk to strangers
- **Discrete deliveries** - spread out over time
- **Vehicle** - keep supplies in trunk, not visible
- **Social media** - never post your preps

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Balcony/Window Gardens:**
- **Herbs** - basil, parsley, cilantro (fast growing)
- **Lettuce** - grows in shallow containers
- **Cherry tomatoes** - 5-gallon bucket each
- **Peppers** - container-friendly
- **Strawberries** - hanging baskets
- **Sprouts** - indoor, no sun needed, 3-7 days

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Vertical Gardening:**
- **Wall planters** - maximize space
- **Hanging baskets** - overhead space
- **Stacking planters** - tower gardens
- **Window boxes** - outside if allowed

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Indoor Growing:**
- **Sprouts** - mason jar, 3-7 days
- **Microgreens** - trays, 7-14 days
- **Herbs** - sunny window
- **LED grow lights** - if power available

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Bug-Out Timing:**
- **Early** - before traffic gridlock
- **On foot** - may be only option
- **Routes** - know 3 ways out of city
- **Destinations** - pre-planned, 50-100 miles

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Bug-Out Bag (Urban):**
- **Lightweight** - you may walk far
- **72-hour focus** - get out of city
- **Comfortable shoes** - broken in
- **Cash** - small bills
- **Maps** - paper, offline GPS
- **Water filter** - can't carry much water

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Building Relationships NOW:**
- **Introduce yourself** - know your neighbors
- **Skills exchange** - offer help, receive help
- **Quiet prep** - don't advertise full extent
- **Find like-minded** - casual conversations reveal preppers
- **Elderly neighbors** - they may need help, have wisdom

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Building-Wide Prep:**
- **Security** - coordinate door security
- **Communications** - exchange numbers
- **Skills** - identify doctors, engineers, etc.
- **Shared resources** - rooftop space, tools

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
## Suburban Environment (Single-Family Homes)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Space:**
- 1,500-3,000 sq ft homes
- Garage storage
- Basement/attic
- Yard space
- Shed possible

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Land:**
- Garden plot (500-2,000 sq ft typical)
- Fruit trees possible
- Rainwater collection feasible
- Chickens possible (zoning dependent)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Infrastructure:**
- Municipal water (somewhat vulnerable)
- Natural gas or propane common
- Solar panels feasible
- Generator possible

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Community:**
- Know your neighbors
- Neighborhood watch common
- Block parties = relationships
- Less anonymous than urban

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**False Security:**
- Comfortable = complacent
- Assume services will return
- Too visible (neighbors see everything)
- HOA restrictions may limit prep

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Resource Competition:**
- Still high population density
- Stores empty quickly (1-2 days)
- Gas stations run dry fast
- Healthcare may be overwhelmed

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Commuter Dependence:**
- Often far from work
- May be stranded if away during crisis
- Family members in different locations

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage:**
- **Garage:** 55-gallon drums (2-4 drums)
- **Basement:** Water storage tanks
- **Yard:** Underground cistern
- **Rotation:** Easier with more space

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Quantities (Suburban):**
- Minimum: 2 weeks (14 gal/person)
- Better: 1 month (30 gal/person)
- Ideal: 3 months + purification

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Rainwater Collection:**
- **Gutters** barrels (50-100 gal)
- **Check local laws** - legal in most suburbs
- **First-flush diverter** - filters initial dirty water
- **Mosquito screen** - prevent breeding

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Purification:**
- **Gravity filters** - Berkey-style, 1,000+ gallons
- **Ceramic filters** - long-lasting
- **Chemical** - bleach, tablets as backup

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage Locations:**
- **Pantry** - dedicated food storage
- **Garage shelving** - temperature-stable items
- **Basement** - cool storage ideal
- **Spare bedroom** - rotate stored goods
- **Under stairs** - dead space utilization

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Food Strategy:**
- **6-12 month supply** - very feasible
- **Bulk purchases** - Costco/Sam's Club
- **Rotation system** - FIFO (first in, first out)
- **Variety** - space allows comfort foods

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Suburban Food Storage:**
- **Pantry staples:** Rice (50 lb), beans (50 lb), pasta, flour
- **Canned goods:** 200-300 cans (family of 4, 3 months)
- **Freeze-dried:** Long-term backup (25-year shelf life)
- **Frozen:** If power reliable, extra freezer
- **Refrigerated:** Backup power for fridge

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Yard Garden:**
- **Size:** 400-1,000 sq ft can feed family
- **Raised beds:** Easier maintenance, better soil
- **Square-foot gardening:** Maximize production
- **Succession planting:** Continuous harvest

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Essential Crops:**
- **Tomatoes:** High yield, 50+ lbs per plant
- **Zucchini:** Prolific producer
- **Beans:** Protein, nitrogen-fixing
- **Potatoes:** Calorie-dense, stores well
- **Lettuce/greens:** Fast growing
- **Herbs:** Medicine + cooking

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Fruit Trees:**
- **Dwarf varieties:** Fit suburban lot
- **Apple, pear:** Cold tolerant
- **Cherry, plum:** Moderate space
- **Citrus:** Warm climates
- **Takes 3-5 years** - plant NOW

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Chickens (If Legal):**
- **3-6 hens:** 12-30 eggs per week
- **Coop:** 4 sq ft per bird inside, 10 sq ft outside
- **Feed storage:** 50 lb/month for 6 hens
- **Benefits:** Eggs, pest control, compost

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Heating:**
- **Fireplace/wood stove:** Burn wood (cord = $200-400)
- **Propane heater:** Mr. Heater, ventilation critical
- **Kerosene heater:** Good BTU, ventilation needed
- **Generator + space heater:** Short-term solution

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Cooling:**
- **Generator + AC:** Expensive fuel use
- **Swamp cooler:** Works in dry climates
- **Whole-house fan:** Evening cooling
- **Window units:** More efficient than central

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Solar Power:**
- **Feasible** - roof space available
- **Grid-tie:** Reduces bill, may not work in outage
- **Off-grid:** Battery backup, works in outage
- **DIY:** $3,000-10,000 for basic system
- **Professional:** $15,000-30,000 installed

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Generator:**
- **Portable:** 3,000-7,000 watts ($400-1,200)
- **Standby:** Whole-house, automatic ($5,000-15,000)
- **Fuel:** Gas, propane, or dual-fuel
- **Storage:** Garage or shed (ventilate!)
- **Noise:** Annoys neighbors (run limited hours)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Septic Tank (If You Have):**
- **Advantage:** Not dependent on municipal sewer
- **Still needs water:** For flushing
- **Pump:** May need power (generator)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Municipal Sewer (Most Suburbs):**
- **Vulnerable:** If treatment plant down
- **Backup:** Portable toilet, bucket system
- **Gray water:** Separate from black water

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Composting Toilet:**
- **Long-term solution:** No water, no sewer needed
- **Options:** Commercial ($1,000+) or DIY ($200)
- **Requires:** Separate urine/solid, cover material
- **Zoning:** Check local laws

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Visible Wealth:**
- **Your house = target** if lights on, neighbors dark
- **Curtains/blinds:** Privacy = security
- **Discretion:** Don't advertise supplies

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Perimeter:**
- **Fence:** Privacy + barrier (not security)
- **Motion lights:** Deter criminals
- **Cameras:** Record events
- **Dog:** Best alarm system

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Home Hardening:**
- **Deadbolts:** All doors
- **Strike plates:** 3-inch screws
- **Window film:** Harder to break
- **Safe room:** Interior room, reinforced

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Neighborhood Preparedness:**
- **Block captain:** Organize neighbors
- **Skills inventory:** Who knows what?
- **Resource sharing:** Tools, skills, labor
- **Security:** Neighborhood watch
- **Communication:** Radio, phone tree

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Space & Land:**
- 1-40+ acres common
- Large gardens/farms possible
- Livestock feasible
- Privacy

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Water:**
- Well water common
- Springs, ponds, creeks
- Rainwater collection easy
- Large storage possible

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Self-Sufficiency:**
- Grow majority of food
- Raise animals
- Firewood abundant
- Land = resources

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Low Density:**
- Few neighbors (miles apart)
- Less competition for resources
- Lower crime typically
- Government slower to arrive = less control

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Isolation:**
- Emergency services 30-60+ minutes
- Hospitals 30+ miles
- Limited store access
- No neighbors to help immediately

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Infrastructure Vulnerability:**
- Power lines = long distances = outages
- Water well = electric pump
- Propane delivery dependent
- Road maintenance limited

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Self-Reliance Required:**
- **Must** be able to handle emergencies
- Medical skills critical
- Equipment repair skills needed
- No calling for help = handle it yourself

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Well Water:**
- **Advantage:** Independent water source
- **Critical:** Hand pump backup ($300-1,500)
- **Power:** Generator or solar for electric pump
- **Testing:** Annual water quality tests
- **Yield:** Typically abundant

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Surface Water:**
- **Pond:** Stock, irrigation, backup water
- **Creek/stream:** Continuous source
- **Spring:** Ideal if you have one
- **Treatment:** ALWAYS purify

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage:**
- **1,000+ gallons feasible** - large tanks
- **Cisterns:** Above/below ground
- **Rainwater:** Multiple barrels or large tanks

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Garden:**
- **Large-scale:** 2,000-10,000+ sq ft
- **Variety:** 20-30 different crops
- **Preservation:** Can, freeze, dry, root cellar
- **Greenhouse:** Extend season
- **Cold frames:** Winter growing

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Livestock:**
- **Chickens:** Eggs + meat (25-50 birds)
- **Goats:** Milk, meat, brush clearing (3-6 animals)
- **Pigs:** Meat, efficient feed conversion (1-3)
- **Cattle:** Meat, milk (1-2 for family)
- **Rabbits:** Meat, fast reproduction
- **Feed storage:** Tons needed, rodent-proof

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Hunting & Foraging:**
- **Game:** Deer, turkey, rabbit (know laws)
- **Fishing:** Ponds, streams (stock pond)
- **Foraging:** Berries, nuts, mushrooms (KNOW identification)
- **Trapping:** Supplement meat (practice NOW)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Preservation:**
- **Root cellar:** 50-55 degF, 85-95% humidity, stores months
- **Canning:** Hundreds of jars, pressure canner critical
- **Freezer:** Needs power (generator or solar)
- **Drying:** Dehydrator or sun-drying
- **Smoking:** Meat preservation
- **Fermentation:** Sauerkraut, kimchi, etc.

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Solar:**
- **Roof space abundant:** 5-10kW systems common
- **Ground-mount:** If roof inadequate
- **Off-grid:** Totally feasible with battery bank
- **Cost:** $15,000-40,000 for whole-house

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Wind:**
- **If location suitable:** Consistent 10+ mph winds
- **Turbine:** $10,000-50,000 installed
- **Hybrid:** Solar + wind = reliability

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Generator:**
- **Essential backup:** For well pump minimum
- **Whole-house:** 10-20kW ($3,000-8,000)
- **Propane:** Large tank (500-1,000 gal)
- **Dual-fuel:** Gas + propane flexibility

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Wood Heat:**
- **Abundant fuel:** Cut your own timber
- **Wood stove:** Heat + cooking
- **Cord/year:** 3-6 cords for heating (cut/split yourself = free)
- **Chimney:** Clean annually (fire hazard)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Building:**
- **Larger homes:** 1,500-3,000+ sq ft
- **Outbuildings:** Barn, shop, storage
- **Root cellar:** Food storage
- **Greenhouse:** Year-round growing

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Heating:**
- **Wood:** Primary heat (free if you cut)
- **Propane:** Backup heat
- **Solar passive:** Design orientation

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Perimeter:**
- **Large property:** Harder to secure
- **Natural barriers:** Trees, terrain, water
- **Driveway alarm:** Know when someone coming
- **Dogs:** Alert + deterrent

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Firearms:**
- **Rural norm:** For hunting, predators, defense
- **Training:** Essential for safety and effectiveness
- **Storage:** Secure from children, theft

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Isolation = Vulnerability:**
- **No immediate help** - prepared to defend yourself
- **Community:** Know neighbors miles away
- **Communication:** HAM radio to community

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Tight-Knit:**
- **Everyone knows everyone:** Privacy limited
- **Mutual aid:** Help neighbors, receive help
- **Skills:** Value trades (mechanic, welding, veterinary)
- **Barter:** Common in rural areas

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Altitude:**
- **7,000-12,000+ feet** - physiological effects
- **Thinner air:** Harder to breathe, cook, heat
- **Altitude sickness:** Can be fatal above 8,000 ft

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Short Growing Season:**
- **Frost:** June-August only (high altitude)
- **Cool summers:** Limited crop options
- **Intense sun:** UV damage to plants and people

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Extreme Weather:**
- **Winter:** -20 degF to -40 degF common
- **Snow:** 10-30 feet accumulation
- **Avalanche:** Life-threatening
- **Wildfire:** Dry forests

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Isolation:**
- **Roads close:** Snow blocks access months
- **Remote:** Hours from services
- **No cell service:** Common
- **Self-rescue:** Essential skill

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Water:**
- **Abundant:** Snowmelt, springs, streams
- **Clean:** Less contamination at source
- **Storage:** Cold = less algae/bacteria

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Security:**
- **Remote:** Hard to find, reach
- **Terrain:** Natural defense
- **Low population:** Few people

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Hunting:**
- **Big game:** Elk, deer, moose abundant
- **Fishing:** High-mountain lakes and streams

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
#### Water (Cold Climate Considerations)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Freezing Issues:**
- **Storage must not freeze:** Indoor or insulated
- **Pipes freeze:** Heat tape, insulation
- **Outdoor access frozen:** Winter snow/ice 6+ months

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Sources:**
- **Spring:** May freeze in winter
- **Creek:** Ice over but flows underneath
- **Snow melt:** Abundant but slow (boiling needed)
- **Rain:** Limited at altitude

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Cold-Tolerant Crops:**
- **Potatoes:** Good at altitude
- **Root vegetables:** Carrots, beets, turnips
- **Brassicas:** Cabbage, broccoli, kale
- **Peas:** Early spring crop
- **Greens:** Lettuce, spinach (cold frames)
- **Berries:** Strawberries, raspberries (native varieties)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Greenhouse Essential:**
- **Extend season:** April-October instead of June-August
- **Warmth:** Thermal mass (water barrels), insulation
- **Elevation limit:** Above 9,000 ft, very challenging

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Hunting & Foraging:**
- **Big game:** Elk (400+ lbs meat), deer, moose
- **Small game:** Rabbit, grouse
- **Fishing:** Trout in mountain lakes
- **Foraging:** Pine nuts, berries, wild onions
- **Preserve:** Critical for winter

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Storage:**
- **Root cellar:** Natural cold storage
- **Freezing:** Outdoors works in winter!
- **Canning:** Essential preservation
- **Dried:** Jerky, dried fruit

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Heating (Critical):**
- **Wood stove:** Primary heat source
- **Firewood:** 6-10 cords/winter (harvest in summer)
- **Backup:** Propane, generator
- **Insulation:** R-30 walls, R-50 ceiling minimum
- **Passive solar:** South-facing windows

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Winter Storm Preparation:**
- **Snow removal:** Roof rake (collapse risk)
- **Supplies:** 3+ months food (snowed in)
- **Backup heat:** If primary fails = death
- **Avalanche:** Know zones, avoid building there

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Altitude Considerations:**
- **Cooking:** Water boils at lower temp (takes longer)
- **Baking:** Adjust recipes for altitude
- **Fire:** Less oxygen = harder to start/maintain

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Solar:**
- **Excellent sun:** High altitude = intense
- **Snow:** Panels covered in winter (clear regularly)
- **Cold:** Batteries less efficient in cold
- **System size:** Larger due to short winter days

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Generator:**
- **Essential:** Long winter nights
- **Propane:** Best fuel for cold
- **Shelter:** Insulated shed for reliability

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Wood:**
- **Abundant:** Forest = free fuel
- **Labor:** Cutting, splitting, stacking
- **Year-round:** Always gathering firewood

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Isolation:**
- **No one coming:** Self-rescue essential
- **Wildlife:** Bears, mountain lions (secure food)
- **Weather:** Storm can trap for weeks

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Communication:**
- **No cell:** Common above treeline
- **Satellite phone:** $500-1,500 + service
- **HAM radio:** Long range
- **SPOT/InReach:** Emergency beacon

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Distance to Hospital:**
- **1-3 hours:** On good roads
- **Winter:** May be impossible
- **Helicopter:** Weather-dependent

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Essential Skills:**
- **Wilderness First Aid:** 2-3 day course MINIMUM
- **WFR:** Wilderness First Responder (9 days)
- **WEMT:** Wilderness EMT (ideal)

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
**Medical Supplies:**
- **Comprehensive kit:** Hospital-level preferred
- **Trauma:** Severe bleeding, fractures
- **Altitude sickness:** Diamox, oxygen
- **Hypothermia:** Warming, insulation

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]
### Urban Checklist
- [ ] Water bricks (under bed, closets) - 3-14 gallons/person
- [ ] WaterBOB (bathtub reservoir) - 100 gallons when needed
- [ ] Vertical shelving - maximize storage
- [ ] Freeze-dried meals - compact, light
- [ ] Portable filter (LifeStraw/Sawyer)
- [ ] Camping stove + fuel - ventilation critical
- [ ] Bucket toilet + bags + litter
- [ ] Battery power banks - multiple units
- [ ] Fire escape plan - know 3 exits
- [ ] Building relationships - know neighbors
- [ ] Bug-out bag - lightweight, 72hr
- [ ] Cash - small bills
- [ ] Paper maps - routes out of city

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]

[src: LOCATION_GUIDES.md]

- [ ] 3+ months food - snowed in

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Essential Emergency Medical Skills & Procedures**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**By Emergency Type:**
- [Life-Threatening (ABCs)](#life-threatening-emergencies-abcs)
- [Bleeding Control](#bleeding-control)
- [Shock](#shock)
- [Burns](#burns)
- [Fractures & Sprains](#fractures--sprains)
- [Wounds & Infections](#wounds--infections)
- [Environmental](#environmental-emergencies)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**By Skill Level:**
- [Absolute Beginner](#beginner-priorities)
- [Intermediate](#intermediate-skills)
- [Advanced](#advanced-procedures)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Supplies:**
- [72-Hour Kit](#72-hour-first-aid-kit)
- [Extended Kit](#2-week-first-aid-kit)
- [Long-Term Supplies](#long-term-medical-supplies)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**A = Airway**
- Is airway open and clear?
- Look for: Choking, tongue blocking airway, foreign objects

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**B = Breathing**
- Is person breathing?
- Look, listen, feel for breath (5-10 seconds)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**C = Circulation**
- Is heart beating?
- Check pulse (carotid artery in neck)
- Look for severe bleeding

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**If any ABC fails Immediate action required**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**When to Use:**
- Person not breathing AND no pulse
- Drowning victim
- Cardiac arrest
- Electrocution

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
1. **Check responsiveness:** Tap shoulders, shout "Are you okay?"
2. **Call 911** (or send someone)
3. **Position:** Flat on back on firm surface
4. **Hand position:** Center of chest, between nipples
5. **Compressions:**
 - Place heel of one hand on chest
 - Other hand on top, fingers interlaced
 - Arms straight, shoulders over hands
 - Push hard and fast: 2 inches deep
 - Rate: 100-120 per minute (beat of "Stayin' Alive")
 - Count: 30 compressions
6. **Rescue breaths (if trained):**
 - Tilt head back, lift chin
 - Pinch nose shut
 - Full seal over mouth
 - 2 breaths (1 second each, chest should rise)
7. **Continue:** 30 compressions, 2 breaths
8. **Don't stop** until: Person breathes, help arrives, or you're too exhausted

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Child CPR (1-8 years):**
- Same as adult but:
- One or two hands for compressions
- Compress 2 inches deep
- Gentler breaths (until chest rises)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Infant CPR (under 1 year):**
- Two fingers for compressions (center of chest, just below nipple line)
- Compress 1.5 inches deep
- Gentle puffs of air (cheek full of air)
- Cover infant's mouth AND nose with your mouth

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Compression-Only CPR:**
- If untrained or uncomfortable with breaths
- Continuous chest compressions at 100-120/min
- Better than doing nothing
- Effective for first several minutes

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Important:**
- Don't stop to check pulse during CPR
- Switch rescuers every 2 minutes if possible (exhaustion)
- AED (automated external defibrillator) if available - follow voice prompts

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs of Choking:**
- Can't speak or cough
- Hands clutching throat (universal sign)
- Silent or high-pitched wheezing
- Face turning blue

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
1. **Ask:** "Are you choking?" (if can't speak choking)
2. **Stand behind person**
3. **Make a fist** with one hand
4. **Position fist:** Thumb side against abdomen, just above navel
5. **Grab fist** with other hand
6. **Thrust inward and upward** (J-shaped motion)
7. **Repeat** until object expelled or person becomes unconscious
8. **Each thrust** should be distinct and forceful

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Unconscious Person:**
- Lower to ground
- Begin CPR (compressions may dislodge object)
- Before giving breaths, look in mouth for object
- If visible and easy to remove sweep out with finger
- Continue CPR

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Self-Heimlich:**
- Make fist above navel
- Grab with other hand
- Thrust inward/upward repeatedly
- OR lean over chair/counter edge, press abdomen forcefully

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
1. **Sit down, hold infant face-down on forearm**
2. **Support head/neck** with hand
3. **5 back blows** between shoulder blades (heel of hand)
4. **Flip infant face-up** on other forearm
5. **5 chest thrusts** (two fingers, center of chest, like infant CPR)
6. **Repeat** 5 back blows, 5 chest thrusts until object expelled
7. **Never** blind finger sweep in infant mouth

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
### Severe Allergic Reaction (Anaphylaxis)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Difficulty breathing / wheezing
- Swelling of face, lips, tongue, throat
- Hives / widespread rash
- Rapid pulse
- Dizziness / loss of consciousness
- Nausea / vomiting
- Sense of impending doom

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Action:**
1. **Call 911 immediately**
2. **Use EpiPen (epinephrine auto-injector) if available:**
 - Remove safety cap
 - Jab firmly into outer thigh (through clothing OK)
 - Hold 3-10 seconds (depends on device)
 - Massage area 10 seconds
3. **Lay person down** (unless vomiting/breathing difficulty sit up)
4. **Second dose** may be needed after 5-15 minutes if no improvement
5. **Even if improved**, must go to hospital (reaction can return)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Stockpile Considerations:**
- EpiPen prescription: 2-pack (~$600, or generic ~$100-300)
- Shelf life: 12-18 months
- Store at room temperature (not hot car/freezer)
- Train all family members on use

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Action:**
1. **Wash hands** (if possible)
2. **Rinse wound** with clean water
3. **Apply direct pressure** with clean cloth (5-10 minutes)
4. **Clean wound** thoroughly (soap and water)
5. **Apply antibiotic ointment** (Neosporin, Bacitracin)
6. **Cover with bandage**
7. **Change daily** and check for infection

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Recognize:**
- Blood spurting from wound
- Blood soaking through bandages
- Loss of consciousness possible

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Action - Direct Pressure:**
1. **Expose wound** (cut away clothing if needed)
2. **Apply direct pressure** with cloth/gauze
3. **Press hard** - don't worry about hurting them
4. **Hold pressure 10+ minutes** without peeking
5. **If blood soaks through** add more cloth on top (don't remove first layer)
6. **Keep pressure** until bleeding stops or help arrives

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Pressure Points:**
- **Arm bleeding:** Press brachial artery (inside of upper arm, between bicep and bone)
- **Leg bleeding:** Press femoral artery (groin crease, where thigh meets abdomen)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Tourniquet (Last Resort):**
- Use ONLY if: Severe limb bleeding, direct pressure fails, life-threatening
- Commercial tourniquet (CAT, SOFTT) best
- Improvised: Belt, torn clothing (at least 1.5" wide)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**How to Apply:**
1. **Position:** 2-3 inches above wound (between wound and heart)
2. **Wrap** around limb twice
3. **Tie tight** overhand knot
4. **Insert stick/rod** through knot
5. **Twist** until bleeding stops (will be very painful)
6. **Secure** stick so it can't unwind
7. **Mark time** on person's forehead (permanent marker)
8. **Do NOT remove** - only hospital should remove
9. **Do NOT loosen** or cover with blanket (need to monitor)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Tourniquet Notes:**
- May result in loss of limb (better than loss of life)
- Limb can survive 2 hours with tourniquet
- Bleeding control > limb preservation in life-threatening situation

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
### Internal Bleeding (Suspect After Trauma)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Bruising / discoloration spreading
- Swollen, firm abdomen
- Vomiting or coughing up blood
- Blood in urine or stool (black, tarry)
- Weak, rapid pulse
- Cold, clammy skin
- Confusion, faintness

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Action:**
1. **Call 911 immediately**
2. **Treat for shock** (see below)
3. **Keep person still** (don't move if spine injury possible)
4. **Nothing by mouth** (no food or water)
5. **Monitor breathing and pulse**
6. **Evacuate to hospital ASAP**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**What is Shock:**
- Life-threatening condition
- Body not getting enough blood/oxygen
- Can result from: Severe bleeding, heart attack, allergic reaction, infection, trauma

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Pale, cold, clammy skin
- Rapid, weak pulse
- Rapid, shallow breathing
- Weakness, dizziness
- Confusion, anxiety
- Nausea / vomiting
- Thirst
- Loss of consciousness

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Call 911 / evacuate to hospital**
2. **Lay person down** on back
3. **Elevate legs 12 inches** (unless: head/neck/spine/leg injury, breathing difficulty)
4. **Maintain body temperature** (blanket under and over, but don't overheat)
5. **Nothing by mouth** (no food, water, smoking)
6. **Monitor ABCs** continuously
7. **Reassure** (calm, comfort)
8. **Treat cause** (stop bleeding, etc.)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**First-Degree (Superficial):**
- Red, painful
- No blisters
- Example: Mild sunburn
- Heals: 3-6 days

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Second-Degree (Partial Thickness):**
- Red, very painful
- Blisters
- Swelling
- Example: Severe sunburn, scalding
- Heals: 2-3 weeks

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Third-Degree (Full Thickness):**
- White, brown, or charred
- Painless (nerves destroyed)
- Leathery appearance
- Requires medical care
- Heals: Months, often requires grafts

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Immediate Actions (All Burns):**
1. **Stop the burning:**
 - Heat: Remove from source
 - Chemical: Brush off powder, flush 20 minutes
 - Electrical: Ensure power off before touching
2. **Remove jewelry/tight clothing** before swelling (don't remove stuck clothing)
3. **Cool the burn:**
 - Cool (not ice cold) running water 10-20 minutes
 - OR cool compress
 - Don't use ice (causes more damage)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**First-Degree Burns:**
1. Cool with water
2. Aloe vera gel or moisturizing lotion
3. Cover loosely if needed
4. Pain relief: Ibuprofen or acetaminophen
5. Keep out of sun

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Second-Degree Burns (Small, <3 inches):**
1. Cool with water 10-20 minutes
2. Gently clean with mild soap and water
3. **Don't break blisters** (infection risk)
4. If blister breaks: Clean, antibiotic ointment, cover
5. Apply antibiotic ointment (after cooled)
6. Cover with non-stick gauze and bandage
7. Change dressing daily
8. Pain relief: Ibuprofen or acetaminophen
9. Watch for infection signs

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Seek Medical Care If:**
- Third-degree burn (any size)
- Second-degree burn larger than 3 inches
- Burns on face, hands, feet, genitals, major joints
- Chemical or electrical burn
- Breathing difficulty (smoke inhalation)
- Signs of infection (increasing pain, pus, fever, red streaks)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**DO NOT:**
- Apply ice (damages tissue)
- Apply butter, oils, ointments immediately (traps heat)
- Break blisters intentionally
- Use fluffy cotton (fibers stick)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Watch for:**
- Increasing pain (after first 2 days)
- Redness spreading outward
- Red streaks from wound toward heart
- Increased swelling
- Pus (yellow/green discharge)
- Warmth around wound
- Fever over 100.4 degF
- Swollen lymph nodes

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Action:**
- Clean wound thoroughly
- Apply antibiotic ointment
- Change dressing twice daily
- If worsening or fever seek medical care (antibiotics may be needed)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Heard or felt a snap
- Severe pain
- Swelling, bruising
- Deformity (bent at odd angle)
- Can't move or bear weight
- Bone visible (open fracture)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**DO NOT move person if:**
- Suspect spine/neck injury
- Unconscious
- Multiple injuries

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**General Fracture Care:**
1. **Stabilize** - Don't try to straighten
2. **Immobilize** above and below fracture
3. **Splint** (see below)
4. **Ice** wrapped in cloth (20 min on, 20 min off)
5. **Elevate** if possible
6. **Pain relief** - Ibuprofen or acetaminophen
7. **Monitor circulation** - Check fingers/toes every 30 min (warm, pink, can feel?)
8. **Seek medical care**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Open Fracture (Bone Through Skin):**
- Life-threatening - infection risk
- **Don't push bone back in**
- Cover wound with sterile dressing
- Immobilize
- Treat for shock
- Evacuate immediately

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Purpose:** Immobilize injury to prevent further damage

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Principles:**
- Immobilize joint above AND below injury
- Splint in position found (don't straighten unless compromised circulation)
- Pad splint (prevent pressure sores)
- Check circulation after splinting

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Improvised Splints:**
- Sticks/branches
- Rolled newspapers/magazines
- Cardboard
- Foam pad
- Pillow (ankle)
- Opposite leg (tie injured leg to good leg)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**How to Splint:**
1. **Pad** splint material
2. **Position** splint to immobilize joints above and below
3. **Secure** with bandages, strips of cloth, belts (not too tight)
4. **Check circulation** (pink, warm, can feel?)
5. **Recheck** every 30 minutes

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Arm/Wrist:**
- Splint from elbow to past hand
- Sling to support (tie around neck)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Leg/Ankle:**
- Splint from above knee to past foot
- OR pillow around ankle, tape secure

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Sprain:** Stretched/torn ligament (connects bones)
**Strain:** Stretched/torn muscle or tendon (connects muscle to bone)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Pain, swelling
- Bruising
- Limited movement
- Popping sensation (sprains)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**R = Rest**
- Don't use injured area
- Crutches for ankle/knee sprains

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**I = Ice**
- 20 minutes at a time
- 20 minutes off between applications
- First 48-72 hours
- Use barrier (cloth) between ice and skin

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**C = Compression**
- Wrap with elastic bandage
- Firm but not tight (shouldn't cut off circulation)
- Loosen if: Numbness, tingling, increased pain, swelling below wrap

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**E = Elevation**
- Raise above heart level
- Reduces swelling
- As much as possible for first 48 hours

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Pain Relief:**
- Ibuprofen (reduces inflammation)
- Acetaminophen (if can't take ibuprofen)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Recovery:**
- Mild sprain: 1-2 weeks
- Moderate: 3-6 weeks
- Severe: Months

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Seek Medical Care If:**
- Can't bear weight
- Joint feels unstable
- Numbness
- Severe pain or swelling
- No improvement after 3 days

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Proper cleaning prevents 90% of infections**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Supplies:**
- Clean water (boiled and cooled, or bottled)
- Soap (mild, unscented)
- Clean cloth or gauze
- Tweezers (sterilized)
- Antibiotic ointment
- Bandages

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Steps:**
1. **Wash YOUR hands** thoroughly (soap, 20 seconds)
2. **Stop bleeding** (direct pressure 5-10 minutes)
3. **Rinse wound** under running water 5 minutes (removes debris)
4. **Clean around wound** with soap and water (not in wound)
5. **Remove debris** with sterilized tweezers if needed
6. **Irrigate wound** with clean water (syringe if available)
7. **Pat dry** with clean cloth
8. **Apply antibiotic ointment** thin layer
9. **Cover with bandage**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Deep or Large Wounds:**
- May need stitches if:
 - Edges don't come together
 - Deeper than 1/4 inch
 - On face, joints, or over bone
 - Caused by animal/human bite
- Seek medical care within 6-8 hours (best results)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
### Wound Closure (If Medical Care Unavailable)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Butterfly Bandages / Steri-Strips:**
1. Clean wound thoroughly
2. Stop bleeding completely
3. Bring edges together gently
4. Apply strips perpendicular to wound
5. Start at middle, work outward
6. Leave small gaps between strips (drainage)
7. Don't remove for 7-10 days

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Superglue (Medical-Grade Dermabond or Regular):**
- For small, clean cuts only
- Apply to DRY skin
- Bring edges together
- Apply thin line along (not in) wound
- Hold edges 60 seconds
- Forms waterproof seal
- Peels off naturally in 5-10 days

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**DO NOT use superglue if:**
- Puncture wound
- Animal/human bite
- Dirty wound
- Signs of infection
- Deep wound

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Danger:** Deep, high infection risk (tetanus)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Don't remove object if large** (knife, stake) - stabilize and seek help
2. **Small punctures:** Let bleed briefly (cleans wound)
3. **Clean thoroughly** - irrigate with water
4. **Soak in warm water** 15 minutes, 3-4 times daily
5. **Watch closely for infection**
6. **Tetanus shot** if not current (within 10 years, or 5 years for dirty wound)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**High Risk Punctures:**
- Stepping on nail
- Animal bites
- Stab wounds
- Deep wood splinters

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**High infection risk** - bacteria from mouth

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Wash immediately** under running water 10+ minutes
2. **Clean with soap** thoroughly
3. **Apply antibiotic ointment**
4. **Don't close wound** (needs to drain)
5. **Seek medical care** (may need antibiotics, tetanus, rabies evaluation)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Rabies Risk:**
- Wild animals: Bats, raccoons, skunks, foxes (HIGH RISK)
- Domestic dogs/cats: Lower risk if vaccinated
- If bitten by wild animal rabies post-exposure shots immediately (fatal if untreated)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Causes:** Exercise/work in hot environment, dehydration

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Heavy sweating
- Weakness, dizziness
- Headache
- Nausea
- Pale, cool, clammy skin
- Normal temperature or slightly elevated

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Move to cool area** (shade, AC)
2. **Lay down**
3. **Loosen clothing**
4. **Sip water** slowly (16-32 oz over 1 hour)
5. **Cool with wet cloths** or spray bottle
6. **Rest**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Causes:** Body can't cool itself, temperature 104 degF+

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- HIGH body temperature (104 degF+)
- Hot, DRY skin (or still sweating)
- Rapid pulse
- Confusion, disorientation
- Loss of consciousness
- Seizures possible

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Call 911 immediately**
2. **Move to cool area**
3. **Cool RAPIDLY:**
 - Remove excess clothing
 - Spray with water, fan aggressively
 - Ice packs on neck, armpits, groin
 - Wet sheets
4. **Monitor ABCs**
5. **Don't give fluids if unconscious**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Every minute counts - permanent damage/death possible**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Causes:** Prolonged cold exposure (doesn't have to be freezing)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Mild Hypothermia (95-90 degF):**
- Shivering (intense)
- Confusion, slurred speech
- Clumsy, stumbling
- Apathy

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Severe Hypothermia (<90 degF):**
- Shivering stops
- Rigid muscles
- Slow pulse and breathing
- Loss of consciousness
- Appears dead

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Mild:**
1. **Move to shelter**
2. **Remove wet clothing**
3. **Warm gradually:**
 - Blankets, sleeping bag
 - Warm (not hot) drinks if conscious
 - Body heat (skin-to-skin in sleeping bag)
4. **Dry clothing**
5. **Hat** (major heat loss from head)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Severe:**
1. **Call 911 / evacuate**
2. **Handle GENTLY** (rough movement can trigger cardiac arrest)
3. **Move to shelter carefully**
4. **Remove wet clothing** (cut off if needed)
5. **Insulate from ground**
6. **Wrap in blankets**
7. **Apply heat packs** to core (chest, neck, armpits, groin) NOT limbs
8. **Monitor breathing** - may need CPR
9. **Don't give fluids if unconscious**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**DO NOT:**
- Rub skin (damages tissue)
- Apply direct heat (heating pad, fire) to limbs
- Give alcohol
- Assume person is dead (can appear dead but survive)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Causes:** Freezing of skin and tissue (fingers, toes, nose, ears)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Signs:**
- Numbness
- White/grayish-yellow skin
- Hard, waxy feeling
- Blisters (severe cases)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Get to warm location**
2. **Don't walk on frostbitten feet** (causes more damage)
3. **Remove wet clothing/jewelry**
4. **Warm gradually** in warm (NOT hot) water (98-105 degF) for 15-30 minutes
5. **Extremely painful** as warming (give pain meds)
6. **Don't rub** (damages tissue)
7. **Protect blisters** - don't pop
8. **Wrap in sterile dressing** (separate fingers/toes)
9. **Seek medical care**

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**DO NOT rewarm if risk of refreezing** (worse damage)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Container:** Waterproof bag or plastic tote

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Bandages & Wound Care:**
- [ ] Adhesive bandages (Band-Aids): 50 assorted sizes
- [ ] Gauze pads (4x4"): 25 count
- [ ] Rolled gauze: 6 rolls
- [ ] Medical tape: 2 rolls
- [ ] Elastic bandage (ACE wrap): 2
- [ ] Triangular bandage: 2 (sling, large wound)
- [ ] Butterfly bandages/Steri-Strips: 20 count

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Instruments:**
- [ ] Scissors (trauma shears): 1
- [ ] Tweezers: 1
- [ ] Safety pins: 12
- [ ] Thermometer (digital): 1

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Medications:**
- [ ] Pain relief (ibuprofen 200mg): 100 tablets
- [ ] Pain relief (acetaminophen 500mg): 100 tablets
- [ ] Antihistamine (Benadryl 25mg): 50 tablets
- [ ] Anti-diarrhea (Imodium): 24 tablets
- [ ] Antacid (Tums): 1 bottle
- [ ] Aspirin 325mg: 50 tablets (heart attack)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Topical:**
- [ ] Antibiotic ointment: 3 tubes
- [ ] Hydrocortisone cream 1%: 1 tube (rash, itch)
- [ ] Burn gel/aloe vera: 1 tube

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Protection:**
- [ ] Disposable gloves (nitrile): 10 pairs
- [ ] CPR face shield: 2
- [ ] Hand sanitizer: 1 bottle (8 oz)
- [ ] Antiseptic wipes: 50 count

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Other:**
- [ ] Emergency blanket (Mylar): 2
- [ ] Cold packs (instant): 4
- [ ] First aid manual (printed): 1
- [ ] Emergency contact card: 1
- [ ] Pencil and notepad

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Wound Care:**
- [ ] Larger gauze pads (5x9"): 10 count
- [ ] Additional rolled gauze: 6 rolls
- [ ] Non-stick pads (Telfa): 20 count
- [ ] Surgical tape (paper): 1 roll
- [ ] Cohesive bandage (self-stick): 3 rolls
- [ ] QuikClot or similar (hemostatic): 2 packs

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Splinting:**
- [ ] SAM splint (moldable): 1-2
- [ ] Finger splints: 5 assorted

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Medications (increase quantity):**
- [ ] Pain relief: 200 tablets each
- [ ] Antihistamine: 100 tablets
- [ ] Prescription meds: 2-week supply
- [ ] Anti-nausea (Dramamine): 24 tablets

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Irrigation:**
- [ ] Irrigation syringe (60ml): 2
- [ ] Sterile saline solution: 2 bottles (16 oz)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Tools:**
- [ ] Scalpel or razor blades: 5 (sterile, wound debridement)
- [ ] Splinter probe: 1
- [ ] Tick removal tool: 1

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Medications:**
- Work with doctor to stockpile prescription meds
- Rotate as use (FIFO)
- Store properly (cool, dark, dry)

[src: MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md]
**Advanced Supplies:**
- Suture kit (if trained): $30-60
- Blood pressure cuff: $25-50
- Stethoscope: $20-40
- Tourniquet (CAT or SOFTT): $30 each (2-3 recommended)
- Chest seal (for puncture wounds): $15-25 each (2 recommended)
- Israeli bandage (pressure bandage): $10 each (3-5 recommended)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Building a Prescription & OTC Medicine Supply**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**By Category:**
- [Antibiotics](#antibiotics-priority-1)
- [Chronic Medications](#chronic-disease-medications)
- [Pain Management](#pain-management)
- [Common Illnesses](#otc-for-common-illnesses)
- [Allergy & Asthma](#allergy--asthma)
- [Gastrointestinal](#gastrointestinal)
- [First Aid Topicals](#topical-medications)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**By Strategy:**
- [Working with Doctors](#working-with-your-doctor)
- [Storage & Rotation](#storage--expiration)
- [Alternative Sources](#alternative-sources)
- [Veterinary Antibiotics](#fish-antibiotics-controversy)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**1. Travel Excuse:**
- "I'm traveling internationally for 3 months"
- "I need a 90-day supply instead of 30-day"
- Most doctors will accommodate

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**2. Disaster Preparedness:**
- "I live in a rural area and want extra in case of weather emergencies"
- "After [recent disaster], I want to be better prepared"
- Many doctors supportive, especially post-COVID

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**3. Insurance Workaround:**
- Ask doctor to prescribe 2x dose, split pills yourself
- Example: 40mg tablets, cut in half for 20mg dose = double supply
- Works for scored tablets only

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**4. 90-Day Fills:**
- Switch to mail-order pharmacy (often required for 90-day)
- Costs less, gets 3x supply
- Refill at day 60 = build up extra month

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**5. Pay Cash:**
- For cheap medications, skip insurance
- GoodRx, RxSaver apps (coupons, often cheaper than insurance)
- Pharmacist can't refuse valid prescription

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**What NOT to Do:**
- Lie about losing medications repeatedly (red flag)
- Exaggerate symptoms to get more (medical record impact)
- Doctor shop (tracked in prescription databases)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Why Critical:**
- Treat bacterial infections (life-saving)
- No natural alternatives for serious infections
- Require prescription
- Limited shelf life (but usable past expiration)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**1. Amoxicillin (500mg)**
- **Treats:** Ear infections, sinus infections, strep throat, UTIs, some pneumonia
- **Supply:** 90-120 capsules (3-4 courses)
- **Dosing:** 500mg 3x daily for 7-10 days
- **Cost:** $4-20 per course (generic)
- **Shelf life:** 2-3 years (longer if refrigerated)
- **Most versatile, first-line for many infections**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**2. Amoxicillin-Clavulanate / Augmentin (875mg/125mg)**
- **Treats:** Resistant infections, bite wounds, sinus infections, pneumonia
- **Supply:** 40-60 tablets (2-3 courses)
- **Dosing:** 875/125mg twice daily for 7-10 days
- **Cost:** $20-40 per course
- **Shelf life:** 2 years
- **Stronger than plain amoxicillin (covers more bacteria)**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**3. Azithromycin / Z-Pack (250mg)**
- **Treats:** Pneumonia, bronchitis, sinus infections, STIs
- **Supply:** 12-18 tablets (2-3 Z-packs)
- **Dosing:** 500mg day 1, then 250mg days 2-5
- **Cost:** $10-30 per Z-pack
- **Shelf life:** 2-3 years
- **Convenient (short course), good for respiratory infections**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**4. Ciprofloxacin / Cipro (500mg)**
- **Treats:** UTIs, severe diarrhea, anthrax (bioterrorism), skin infections
- **Supply:** 40-60 tablets (4-6 courses for UTI)
- **Dosing:** 500mg twice daily for 3-7 days (UTI) or 10-14 days (other)
- **Cost:** $10-40 per course
- **Shelf life:** 3-5 years
- **Broad spectrum, long shelf life**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**5. Doxycycline (100mg)**
- **Treats:** Tick-borne diseases (Lyme, Rocky Mountain spotted fever), pneumonia, acne, malaria prevention
- **Supply:** 60-90 capsules (3-4 courses)
- **Dosing:** 100mg twice daily for 7-14 days
- **Cost:** $5-20 per course
- **Shelf life:** 2-3 years
- **Essential for tick-prone areas**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
### Additional Antibiotics (If Budget Allows)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Cephalexin / Keflex (500mg):**
- Skin infections (cellulitis), UTIs
- Alternative to amoxicillin (penicillin allergy)
- 60-90 capsules
- $10-25 per course

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Metronidazole / Flagyl (500mg):**
- Anaerobic infections, dental abscesses, severe diarrhea (C. diff, giardia)
- 40-60 tablets
- $5-15 per course

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Sulfamethoxazole-Trimethoprim / Bactrim (800/160mg):**
- UTIs, MRSA skin infections, pneumonia
- 40-60 tablets
- $5-20 per course

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
| Antibiotic | Adult Dose | Pediatric Dose (per kg) | Duration |
|------------|------------|-------------------------|----------|
| Amoxicillin | 500mg 3x daily | 25-50mg/kg/day 3 doses | 7-10 days |
| Augmentin | 875mg 2x daily | 45mg/kg/day 2 doses | 7-10 days |
| Azithromycin | 500mg day 1, 250mg days 2-5 | 10mg/kg day 1, 5mg/kg days 2-5 | 5 days |
| Ciprofloxacin | 500mg 2x daily | NOT for children <18 | 3-14 days |
| Doxycycline | 100mg 2x daily | 2-4mg/kg/day 2 doses (age 8+) | 7-14 days |
| Cephalexin | 500mg 4x daily | 25-50mg/kg/day 4 doses | 7-10 days |

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Weight-based dosing for children requires calculation - keep reference chart**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**1. Regular Doctor:**
- Ask for extra during normal visit
- Explain preparedness concerns
- Success rate: 30-50%

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**2. Telemedicine:**
- Urgent care apps (Teladoc, MDLive)
- Request for travel or refill
- $40-80 per visit
- Success rate: Variable

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**3. Dental Antibiotics:**
- Dentists prescribe liberally for tooth infections
- Same antibiotics (amoxicillin, etc.)
- Can stockpile extras

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**4. Veterinary Antibiotics:**
- See section below
- Controversial but option

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**5. International Pharmacies:**
- Mexico, Canada (over-counter or easier prescription)
- Legal gray area (personal import)
- Quality concerns (counterfeit risk)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
### Life-Critical Medications (Highest Priority)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Insulin (Diabetics):**
- 3-6 month supply minimum
- Refrigerate (stable 28 days at room temp)
- Rotate stock (use oldest first)
- Backup: NPH and Regular insulin (cheaper, longer shelf life than modern analogs)
- **Without insulin, Type 1 diabetics die in days**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Heart Medications:**
- Beta-blockers (metoprolol, atenolol)
- ACE inhibitors (lisinopril, enalapril)
- Blood thinners (warfarin, apixaban)
- Nitroglycerin (for angina - expires quickly, rotate)
- 3-6 month supply

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Seizure Medications:**
- Phenytoin (Dilantin), valproic acid, levetiracetam (Keppra)
- **Missing doses can trigger seizures**
- 6-12 month supply if possible

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Thyroid (Levothyroxine):**
- Very stable, long shelf life (5+ years)
- Cheap ($4/month)
- Easy to stockpile year+
- Missing causes fatigue, but not immediately life-threatening

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Asthma Inhalers:**
- Albuterol rescue inhaler: 3-4 extras
- Controller inhalers (Flovent, etc.): 3 month supply
- Nebulizer + albuterol solution (if electricity available)
- **Severe asthma attack without inhaler = life-threatening**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Blood Pressure:**
- 3-6 month supply
- Monitor BP at home (cuff $25-50)
- Many cheap generics available

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Psychiatric Medications:**
- Antidepressants, antipsychotics, mood stabilizers
- **Abrupt discontinuation dangerous**
- 3-6 month supply minimum
- Taper gradually if must stop (doctor guidance)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
### Stockpiling Strategy for Chronic Meds

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**1. 90-Day Prescriptions:**
- Switch from 30-day to 90-day (often required for mail-order)
- Saves money and trips

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**2. Early Refills:**
- Refill at day 60-75 of 90-day supply
- Accumulates 15-30 day extra per quarter
- Over time builds surplus

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**3. Samples:**
- Ask doctor for samples at each visit
- Keep sealed in cool, dark place

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**4. Pharmacy Shopping:**
- Prices vary wildly between pharmacies
- Use GoodRx, check Costco (no membership required for pharmacy)
- Example: Metformin $4/month Walmart vs $50/month some chains

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**5. Pay Cash for Extras:**
- Second prescription, different pharmacy, pay out-of-pocket
- Insurance won't cover, but doctor can write prescription

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**6. Medical Tourism:**
- Medications dramatically cheaper in Mexico, Canada
- 3-6 month personal supply allowed across US border
- Bring prescription, receipts

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Ibuprofen (Advil, Motrin) 200mg:**
- **Uses:** Pain, inflammation, fever
- **Stockpile:** 500-1,000 tablets
- **Dosing:** 400-600mg (2-3 tablets) every 6 hours
- **Max:** 2,400mg/day
- **Cost:** $10-20 per 500 tablets (Sam's Club, Costco)
- **Shelf life:** 5+ years
- **Best for:** Muscle pain, dental pain, inflammation

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Acetaminophen (Tylenol) 500mg:**
- **Uses:** Pain, fever (NOT inflammation)
- **Stockpile:** 500-1,000 tablets
- **Dosing:** 500-1,000mg every 6 hours
- **Max:** 3,000mg/day (liver damage risk above)
- **Cost:** $10-20 per 500 tablets
- **Shelf life:** 5+ years
- **Best for:** Fever, pain if can't take ibuprofen

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Aspirin 325mg:**
- **Uses:** Pain, fever, heart attack (blood thinner)
- **Stockpile:** 200-500 tablets
- **Dosing:** 325-650mg every 4-6 hours (pain), 81-325mg daily (heart)
- **Cost:** $5-15 per 500 tablets
- **Shelf life:** 5+ years
- **Keep for heart attack:** Chew 325mg immediately if suspected heart attack

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Naproxen (Aleve) 220mg:**
- **Uses:** Pain, inflammation (longer-lasting than ibuprofen)
- **Stockpile:** 200-500 tablets
- **Dosing:** 220-440mg every 8-12 hours
- **Cost:** $15-25 per 500 tablets
- **Shelf life:** 5+ years

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Combining Pain Meds:**
- Ibuprofen + Acetaminophen: Safe, can take together (different mechanisms)
- Alternate every 3 hours for severe pain
- Don't combine ibuprofen + naproxen (both NSAIDs)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Tramadol:**
- Moderate pain (stronger than OTC, weaker than opioids)
- Less addiction risk than opioids
- Prescription required
- 50-100mg every 6 hours
- 60-90 tablets useful

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Opioids (Codeine, Hydrocodone, Oxycodone):**
- Severe pain (post-surgery, trauma, end-of-life)
- High addiction risk
- Heavily regulated (hard to stockpile)
- If prescribed, keep extras for emergencies
- Naloxone (Narcan) antidote for overdose

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Lidocaine patches/cream:**
- Numbing agent
- Muscle pain, neuropathy
- OTC 4%, prescription 5%

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Capsaicin cream:**
- Arthritis, nerve pain
- OTC, cheap

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Menthol/camphor (Biofreeze, Icy Hot):**
- Temporary relief, muscle aches

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Diphenhydramine (Benadryl) 25mg:**
- Allergies, itching, sleep aid, nausea
- Stockpile: 200-500 tablets
- Dosing: 25-50mg every 6 hours
- Cost: $5-15 per 100
- Shelf life: 5+ years
- **Keep for severe allergic reactions (anaphylaxis bridge until EpiPen)**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Cetirizine (Zyrtec) 10mg:**
- Daily allergies (non-drowsy)
- Stockpile: 200-365 tablets
- Dosing: 10mg once daily
- Cost: $10-25 per 365
- Shelf life: 3-5 years

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Loratadine (Claritin) 10mg:**
- Daily allergies (non-drowsy)
- Similar to Zyrtec
- Often cheaper

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Pseudoephedrine (Sudafed) 30mg:**
- Nasal congestion (decongestant)
- Behind pharmacy counter (ID required)
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Dosing: 30-60mg every 4-6 hours
- **More effective than phenylephrine (PE) versions on shelves**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Guaifenesin (Mucinex) 400mg:**
- Expectorant (thins mucus)
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Dosing: 400mg every 4 hours
- Drink lots of water with it

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Loperamide (Imodium) 2mg:**
- Anti-diarrheal
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Dosing: 4mg initial, 2mg after each loose stool (max 16mg/day)
- Cost: $8-15 per 100
- Shelf life: 5+ years
- **Essential for diarrhea emergencies**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Bismuth Subsalicylate (Pepto-Bismol):**
- Diarrhea, nausea, upset stomach
- Tablets better for storage than liquid
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Turns stool black (harmless)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Omeprazole (Prilosec) 20mg:**
- Heartburn, acid reflux, ulcers
- Now OTC (was prescription)
- Stockpile: 50-100 tablets
- Dosing: 20mg once daily
- Cost: $10-20 per 42

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Famotidine (Pepcid) 20mg:**
- Heartburn (faster-acting than omeprazole)
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Dosing: 20mg twice daily
- Cost: $10-20 per 100

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Calcium Carbonate (Tums):**
- Quick heartburn relief, calcium supplement
- Cheap, long shelf life
- Multiple bottles

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Docusate (Colace) 100mg:**
- Stool softener (constipation, post-surgery)
- Stockpile: 100-200 capsules
- Gentle, safe

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Sennosides (Senna, Ex-Lax):**
- Laxative (stronger than stool softener)
- Use sparingly (dependency risk)
- Stockpile: 50-100 tablets

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Dextromethorphan (Robitussin DM) 15mg:**
- Cough suppressant
- Stockpile: Tablets or syrup (tablets last longer)
- Dosing: 15-30mg every 6-8 hours
- Shelf life: Syrup 2-3 years, tablets 5+ years

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Phenylephrine (PE):**
- Decongestant (MUCH less effective than pseudoephedrine)
- Not recommended - use pseudoephedrine instead

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Zinc lozenges:**
- Reduces cold duration if taken early
- Stockpile: 3-4 bags
- Start within 24 hours of symptoms

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Vitamin C:**
- May reduce cold severity/duration (debated)
- Cheap, long shelf life
- 500-1,000mg daily

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Diphenhydramine (Benadryl) 25mg:**
- Sleep aid (causes drowsiness)
- Also for allergies
- See above

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Melatonin 3-10mg:**
- Natural sleep hormone
- Jet lag, insomnia
- Stockpile: 100-200 tablets
- Start with 3mg
- Very safe

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Doxylamine (Unisom) 25mg:**
- Sleep aid (stronger than Benadryl for some)
- Stockpile: 50-100 tablets

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Antibiotic Ointment:**
- Triple antibiotic (Neosporin): Bacitracin + neomycin + polymyxin
- Bacitracin (single antibiotic - less allergy risk)
- Stockpile: 10-20 tubes
- Shelf life: 3-5 years (use if not discolored/separated)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Hydrocortisone 1% Cream:**
- Anti-itch, rashes, eczema
- Stockpile: 5-10 tubes
- Shelf life: 3 years

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Clotrimazole 1% (Lotrimin):**
- Antifungal (athlete's foot, jock itch, yeast)
- Stockpile: 3-5 tubes
- Shelf life: 3 years

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Burn Gel / Aloe Vera:**
- Burns, sunburn
- Stockpile: 3-5 tubes/bottles
- Aloe: Refrigerated lasts longer

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Benzocaine (Orajel):**
- Topical numbing (toothache, canker sores)
- Stockpile: 2-3 tubes

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Witch Hazel:**
- Hemorrhoids, minor skin irritations
- Stockpile: 2-3 bottles
- Long shelf life

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Temperature:**
- Ideal: 68-77 degF (20-25 degC)
- Acceptable: 59-86 degF (15-30 degC)
- **Avoid:** Bathrooms (humidity), cars (heat), freezer (some degrade)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Light:**
- Keep in original containers (light-protected)
- Dark closet or drawer
- Avoid clear containers

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Moisture:**
- Low humidity critical
- Silica gel packets in storage bins
- Avoid bathrooms

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Container:**
- Original bottle best (labeled, light-protected)
- If repackaging: Label clearly with name, dose, expiration
- Vacuum seal for long-term

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**The Truth About Expiration:**
- Expiration = Guaranteed potency by manufacturer
- Most medications remain effective years past expiration
- Potency gradually declines (5-10% per year typically)
- Safety: Most remain safe (rare exceptions)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Study Results:**
- US military tested 100+ medications stored properly
- 90% retained potency 5+ years past expiration
- Some effective 10-15 years later

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Exceptions (Don't Use Past Expiration):**
- Liquid antibiotics (amoxicillin suspension)
- Tetracycline antibiotics (can become toxic - rare)
- Insulin (loses potency, critical dosing)
- Nitroglycerin (loses potency quickly, life-saving drug)
- Epinephrine (EpiPen) - loses potency

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Safe to Use Past Expiration (if stored properly):**
- Tablets/capsules: Most antibiotics, pain meds, etc.
- Ointments/creams (if not discolored or separated)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**How to Assess:**
- Check appearance (discolored, crumbling = discard)
- Smell (foul odor = discard)
- Clumping (moisture exposure = discard)
- If looks/smells normal and stored properly = likely OK to use

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Rotation Strategy:**
- Use FIFO (first in, first out)
- Replace medications 1-2 years before expiration
- Stockpile becomes your regular supply (rotate through)

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**What Are They:**
- Antibiotics sold for fish tank use (aquarium stores, Amazon)
- Same chemical as human antibiotics (often same manufacturer)
- No prescription required

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Common Fish Antibiotics:**
- Fish Mox = Amoxicillin 250mg
- Fish Mox Forte = Amoxicillin 500mg
- Fish Flex = Cephalexin 250mg or 500mg
- Fish Flox = Ciprofloxacin 500mg
- Fish Pen = Penicillin
- Fish Zole = Metronidazole

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Legal Status:**
- Legal to purchase
- NOT FDA-approved for human use
- Labeled "not for human consumption"

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Quality Concerns:**
- Same manufacturers as human versions (often)
- No FDA oversight for fish versions
- Purity/dosing not guaranteed
- Counterfeit risk from sketchy sellers

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Safety Considerations:**
- **Risk:** Taking wrong antibiotic (incorrect diagnosis)
- **Risk:** Allergic reactions
- **Risk:** Contributing to antibiotic resistance
- **Risk:** Quality/contamination

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**When Considered:**
- True emergency (no medical access)
- Knowledge of proper diagnosis and dosing
- Reputable source (Thomas Labs brand most recommended)
- Understand risks

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Ethical/Medical Perspective:**
- Most doctors strongly discourage
- Veterinarians say "not intended for humans"
- Preppers say "better than nothing in SHTF"
- **Best approach: Get human prescriptions properly**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Other Vet Meds Sometimes Considered:**
- Antibiotics for livestock (injectable)
- Dosing very different
- Higher contamination risk
- **Not recommended except true survival scenario**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Mexico:**
- Most antibiotics over-counter
- Walk into pharmacy, buy
- Personal import: 3-month supply allowed
- Quality variable (stick to known chains)
- Cost: 50-90% cheaper than US

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**Canada:**
- Online pharmacies ship to US
- Require prescription (more legit)
- Cost: 30-70% cheaper
- Quality generally good

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**India/Overseas:**
- Very cheap
- Counterfeit risk HIGH
- Shipping slow/unreliable
- Legal gray area
- Not recommended

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**If Limited Budget, Prioritize This Order:**

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**$50-100 Budget:**
1. Ibuprofen 500 tablets: $15
2. Acetaminophen 500 tablets: $15
3. Diphenhydramine 100 tablets: $8
4. Loperamide 100 tablets: $10
5. Antibiotic ointment 5 tubes: $15
6. Aspirin 200 tablets: $8
7. Basic wound care supplies: $25

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**$200-300 Budget (add):**
8. Prescription antibiotics: $50-100 (one course each of amoxicillin, azithromycin, ciprofloxacin)
9. Pseudoephedrine 100 tablets: $15
10. Omeprazole 42 tablets: $15
11. Hydrocortisone cream: $10
12. Electrolyte powder: $15

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
**$500+ Budget (add):**
13. More antibiotics (multiple courses, types)
14. Prescription pain meds (tramadol if available)
15. EpiPen (if allergies)
16. Chronic medication surplus (3-6 months)
17. Specialized meds for family needs

[src: MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md]
