[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
# MEDICAL GUIDE: Wilderness & Extended Care

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Medical Care When Hospitals Are Unavailable**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**By Scenario:**
- [Delayed Evacuation](#delayed-evacuation-scenarios)
- [Wound Management](#advanced-wound-management)
- [Illness Without Doctors](#common-illnesses--treatments)
- [Dental Emergencies](#dental-emergencies)
- [Childbirth](#emergency-childbirth)
- [Improvised Medical Supplies](#improvised-medical-supplies)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**By Timeline:**
- [First 24 Hours](#first-24-hours)
- [Days 2-7](#days-2-7-extended-care)
- [Week 2+](#week-2-long-term-care)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**When Medical Help is Hours/Days Away:**
- Remote wilderness (backcountry)
- Natural disaster (roads blocked)
- Grid-down scenario
- Rural/mountain isolation (winter)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Your Goals:**
1. **Stabilize** - Prevent condition from worsening
2. **Monitor** - Watch for complications
3. **Document** - Record vital signs, treatments
4. **Decide** - Evacuate vs. care in place

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Temperature:**
- Normal: 97.0-99.0 degF (36.1-37.2 degC)
- Low fever: 99.1-100.4 degF
- Fever: 100.5-102.9 degF
- High fever: 103 degF+
- Dangerous: 105 degF+ (brain damage risk)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Pulse (Heart Rate):**
- Resting adult: 60-100 bpm
- Athletes: 40-60 bpm
- Children: 70-120 bpm
- Infants: 100-160 bpm

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Respiratory Rate:**
- Adult: 12-20 breaths/min
- Child: 20-30 breaths/min
- Infant: 30-60 breaths/min

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Blood Pressure:**
- Normal: 120/80 or below
- Elevated: 120-129/<80
- High: 130/80 or higher
- Low: 90/60 or lower (shock concern)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Pulse:**
1. Two fingers on wrist (thumb side) or neck (carotid)
2. Count beats for 15 seconds
3. Multiply by 4
4. Note: Strong/weak, regular/irregular

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Respiration:**
1. Watch chest rise/fall
2. Count for 30 seconds, multiply by 2
3. Note: Labored, shallow, normal

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Temperature:**
- Digital thermometer (oral, armpit, rectal)
- Oral: Most common (wait 10 min after eating/drinking)
- Rectal: Most accurate (add 1 degF to oral equivalent)
- Armpit: Least accurate (subtract 1 degF from oral)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Level of Consciousness:**
- **Alert:** Awake, oriented (person, place, time)
- **Verbal:** Responds to voice
- **Pain:** Only responds to painful stimuli
- **Unresponsive:** No response
- (AVPU scale)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Record Every 4-8 Hours (or more if critical):**
- Date/time
- Temperature, pulse, respiration, blood pressure
- Level of consciousness
- Pain level (1-10 scale)
- Treatments given
- Food/water intake
- Urine output
- Any changes

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Why:** Tracks improvement/decline, critical for medical handoff

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Purpose:** Remove bacteria, debris (prevents infection)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Supplies:**
- Clean water (boiled and cooled, or sterile saline)
- Irrigation syringe (60ml) or squeeze bottle
- Clean basin
- Gauze

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Technique:**
1. **Wash your hands** thoroughly
2. **Fill syringe** with clean water/saline
3. **Position wound** over basin (catch runoff)
4. **Spray forcefully** into wound (2-3 inches away)
5. **Use entire syringe** (60ml minimum, more for dirty wounds)
6. **Refill and repeat** 3-5 times (300-500ml total)
7. **Pat dry** with sterile gauze
8. **Dress wound**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**High-pressure irrigation removes 10x more bacteria than rinsing**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
### Wound Debridement (Removing Dead Tissue)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**When Needed:**
- Dead/black tissue visible
- Wound not healing (week 2+)
- Signs of infection despite treatment

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Supplies:**
- Sterile scissors or scalpel
- Tweezers
- Sterile gauze
- Clean water
- Antibiotic ointment

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Technique (Basic):**
1. **Pain management** (ibuprofen before, 30-60 min)
2. **Clean wound** thoroughly (irrigation)
3. **Identify dead tissue** (black, gray, hard, or mushy)
4. **Cut away** only obviously dead tissue (bleeds = alive, don't cut)
5. **Irrigate again** after debridement
6. **Apply antibiotic ointment**
7. **Dress wound**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Warning:** Advanced skill - practice on raw chicken first if possible

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs of Infection:**
- Increasing pain after day 2
- Redness spreading (draw outline with marker, track spread)
- Swelling
- Warmth
- Pus (yellow/green/gray discharge)
- Fever
- Red streaks toward heart (lymphangitis - serious)
- Foul smell

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Aggressive Cleaning:**
- Remove bandage
- Irrigate wound 3-4x daily (300-500ml each time)
- Debride any dead tissue
- Apply antibiotic ointment
- Fresh bandage

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. Warm Soaks:**
- Warm water (not hot)
- Soak 15-20 minutes, 3-4x daily
- Promotes drainage, increases blood flow

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Elevation:**
- Keep injured area elevated above heart
- Reduces swelling

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. Antibiotics (If Available):**
- Oral antibiotics critical for spreading infection
- See MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md for stockpile options
- Common: Amoxicillin, cephalexin, doxycycline

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**5. Monitor Closely:**
- Check vital signs every 4-6 hours
- Watch for systemic infection (fever, confusion, rapid pulse)
- Mark red area with marker, check spread every 12 hours

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Red Flags (Evacuate Immediately if Possible):**
- Red streaks spreading rapidly
- Fever over 103 degF
- Confusion, disorientation
- Rapid pulse/breathing
- Dark/foul-smelling drainage
- Skin breakdown around wound

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Abscess:** Pocket of pus under skin (boil)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs:**
- Red, swollen, warm lump
- Very painful
- May see white/yellow center
- Feels "squishy" or fluctuant

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Option 1: Encourage Natural Drainage**
- Warm compresses 20 min, 4x daily
- May open on its own in 3-7 days
- If opens: Clean, apply antibiotic ointment, cover

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Option 2: Incision & Drainage (I&D)**
- Only if: Large, very painful, not draining naturally
- Last resort (infection risk)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**I&D Procedure:**
1. **Sterilize blade** (lighter flame, let cool, or alcohol)
2. **Clean skin** around abscess thoroughly
3. **Make small incision** at "ripest" point (usually center)
4. **Gentle pressure** around abscess (squeeze pus out)
5. **Irrigate cavity** with clean water/saline
6. **Pack lightly** with gauze wick (allows continued drainage)
7. **Cover** with bandage
8. **Remove packing** after 24 hours
9. **Continue warm soaks** and cleaning until healed

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**After I&D:**
- Watch for signs of worsening infection
- Oral antibiotics if available
- Healing time: 1-2 weeks

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Dangers:**
- Dehydration (life-threatening if severe)
- Electrolyte imbalance

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Prevent Dehydration:**
- **Oral Rehydration Solution (ORS):**
 - Best: WHO formula (see recipe below)
 - Good: Pedialyte, Gatorade diluted 50/50 with water
 - OK: Coconut water
- **Sip frequently:** Small amounts every 15 min (4-8 oz)
- **Goal:** Replace losses (input = output)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**DIY Oral Rehydration Solution:**
- 6 teaspoons sugar
- 1/2 teaspoon salt
- 1 liter (4 cups) clean water
- Optional: 1/2 teaspoon salt substitute (potassium)
- Mix thoroughly, sip throughout day

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. Medications:**
- **Anti-diarrhea (Imodium):** Slows diarrhea (don't use if bloody diarrhea or fever)
- **Anti-nausea (Dramamine, Zofran):** Stops vomiting
- **Pepto-Bismol:** Both diarrhea and nausea

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Diet:**
- **During illness:** Clear liquids only
- **As improving:** BRAT diet (Bananas, Rice, Applesauce, Toast)
- **Avoid:** Dairy, fatty foods, caffeine, alcohol

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. When to Worry:**
- Blood in stool
- High fever (>102 degF)
- Severe abdominal pain
- Signs of dehydration (see below)
- Lasting >3 days

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Dehydration Signs:**
- Decreased urination (dark yellow)
- Dry mouth, cracked lips
- Sunken eyes
- Dizziness when standing
- Confusion
- Skin "tenting" (pinch skin, stays pinched)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
### Respiratory Infections (Cold, Flu, Bronchitis)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Symptoms:**
- Cough, congestion
- Sore throat
- Fever
- Fatigue
- Body aches

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Rest:** Body needs energy to fight infection

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. Hydration:** 8-12 cups fluid daily (water, tea, broth)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Fever Management:**
- Ibuprofen or acetaminophen (dosing per package)
- Cool cloths if uncomfortable
- Don't treat low fever aggressively (fever helps fight infection)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. Cough:**
- Honey (1 tablespoon as needed, not for infants <1 year)
- Dextromethorphan (Robitussin DM)
- Steam inhalation (towel over head, bowl of hot water)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**5. Congestion:**
- Saline nasal rinse (neti pot)
- Pseudoephedrine (Sudafed) - decongestant
- Humidifier (or steam from shower)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**6. Sore Throat:**
- Salt water gargle (1/2 tsp salt in 8 oz warm water)
- Honey
- Throat lozenges

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Antibiotics Don't Help Viral Infections (most colds/flu)**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**When Antibiotics ARE Needed (Bacterial Infection):**
- Strep throat (white patches, severe pain, fever)
- Sinus infection lasting >10 days
- Ear infection (severe pain, fever)
- Pneumonia (see below)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs:**
- Productive cough (green/yellow/bloody mucus)
- High fever (>102 degF)
- Chest pain when breathing/coughing
- Shortness of breath
- Rapid breathing
- Confusion (elderly)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Antibiotics** (critical - azithromycin, amoxicillin, doxycycline)
2. **Fluids** (dehydration common)
3. **Fever control**
4. **Rest** (semi-upright position easier to breathe)
5. **Monitor breathing** (respiratory rate, oxygen levels if equipment available)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Danger Signs:**
- Breathing rate >30/min
- Confusion
- Bluish lips/nails
- Can't complete sentence without gasping
- **Evacuate if possible**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs:**
- Burning with urination
- Frequent urge to urinate
- Lower abdominal pain
- Cloudy or bloody urine
- Strong-smelling urine

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Antibiotics:** Required (won't clear without)
- Nitrofurantoin (Macrobid) - first choice
- Sulfamethoxazole/trimethoprim (Bactrim)
- Ciprofloxacin (Cipro)
- Course: 3-7 days

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. Hydration:** Drink 8-12 cups water daily (flushes bacteria)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Cranberry:** Unsweetened cranberry juice (may help, not cure)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. Pain Relief:**
- Phenazopyridine (AZO, Pyridium) - urinary pain reliever
- Ibuprofen

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Untreated UTI Can Progress to Kidney Infection (Serious):**
- Fever, chills
- Back/side pain
- Nausea, vomiting
- Needs stronger antibiotics

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
### Skin Infections (Cellulitis, Impetigo)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Cellulitis:** Bacterial skin infection (deeper layers)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs:**
- Red, swollen, warm area
- Spreading rapidly
- Pain/tenderness
- Fever

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Treatment:**
- Oral antibiotics (cephalexin, amoxicillin/clavulanate)
- Elevation
- Mark borders with marker (track spread)
- Warm compresses
- Course: 7-10 days

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Impetigo:** Surface skin infection (common in children)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Signs:**
- Crusty, honey-colored sores
- Usually around mouth/nose
- Contagious

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Treatment:**
- Topical antibiotic ointment (Mupirocin - prescription)
- OR oral antibiotics if widespread
- Keep clean, wash hands frequently

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Causes:** Cavity, abscess, cracked tooth

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Pain Control:**
- Ibuprofen 400-600mg every 6 hours (best for dental pain)
- Acetaminophen 500-1000mg every 6 hours
- Can alternate both for severe pain

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. Topical:**
- Clove oil on cotton ball, apply to tooth (natural anesthetic)
- Orajel (benzocaine gel)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Saltwater Rinse:**
- 1/2 tsp salt in 8 oz warm water
- Swish 30 seconds, 4-6x daily
- Reduces inflammation

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. Avoid:**
- Extremely hot/cold foods
- Chewing on affected side
- Sweet/acidic foods

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Abscess (Pus at Gum Line):**
- Needs antibiotics (amoxicillin, clindamycin)
- Warm saltwater rinses
- If abscess visible lump: May drain on own, or needs dentist
- Can be life-threatening if spreads to jaw/neck

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Immediate Actions (First 5 Minutes Critical):**
1. **Find tooth**
2. **Handle by crown only** (don't touch root)
3. **Rinse gently** if dirty (don't scrub)
4. **Reinsert into socket** if possible (bite down on gauze)
5. **If can't reinsert:** Keep in milk or saliva (not water)
6. **See dentist within 30 minutes** (best chance of saving)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**If Dentist Not Available:**
- Keep clean
- Antibiotics (prevent infection)
- Soft diet
- May need permanent extraction later

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**If Baby Tooth:** Don't reinsert (can damage adult tooth below)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Treatment:**
1. **Rinse mouth** with warm water
2. **If bleeding:** Gauze 10 minutes
3. **Cold compress** on face (reduce swelling)
4. **Pain control:** Ibuprofen
5. **Temporary filling:** Dental wax or sugar-free gum over sharp edge
6. **Soft diet**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Dental Filling Kit (Hardware Store or Amazon):**
- Temporary filling material (Dentemp)
- Can last weeks to months
- Follow package directions

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**If Birth is Imminent and No Medical Help:**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
- Contractions 2-3 minutes apart, lasting 60+ seconds
- Urge to push
- Water breaks with bloody show
- Baby's head visible (crowning)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
- Clean towels/blankets (many)
- Sterile gloves (or very clean hands)
- Bulb syringe (clear baby's mouth/nose)
- Shoelaces or string (tie umbilical cord)
- Scissors (sterilize in boiling water)
- Plastic sheet/shower curtain (under mother)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**1. Prepare:**
- Wash hands thoroughly (3 minutes, soap)
- Mother in comfortable position (semi-reclined, knees bent)
- Clean area as much as possible

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**2. As Head Emerges:**
- Support head gently (don't pull)
- If sac (membrane) covers baby: Break and peel away
- Check for cord around neck: If present, gently slip over head

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**3. Body Delivery:**
- Head rotates naturally
- Support head and shoulders as baby slides out (happens quickly after head)
- Baby will be slippery - firm but gentle grip

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**4. Immediate Care (First 30 Seconds):**
- Hold baby level with mother (don't lift high or dangle low)
- Wipe mouth and nose with clean cloth
- Bulb syringe: Suction mouth first, then nose
- Dry baby vigorously with towel (stimulates breathing)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**5. If Baby Doesn't Cry/Breathe:**
- Flick soles of feet
- Rub back vigorously
- If still not breathing: Infant CPR (see MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**6. Warmth (Critical):**
- Dry baby completely
- Place skin-to-skin on mother's chest
- Cover both with blankets
- Baby can't regulate temperature - hypothermia is danger

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**7. Umbilical Cord:**
- **Don't cut immediately** (wait 1-5 minutes, let it stop pulsing)
- Tie firmly with shoelace/string at 6 inches from baby
- Tie second tie 2 inches further from baby
- Cut between the two ties with sterilized scissors
- If no scissors: Leave cord attached (will dry and fall off)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**8. Placenta:**
- Delivers 5-30 minutes after baby
- Don't pull on cord (let placenta deliver naturally)
- Gentle push from mother with contraction
- Save placenta (doctor will inspect for completeness)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**9. After Placenta:**
- Massage mother's abdomen (helps uterus contract, stops bleeding)
- Firm massage just below belly button
- Moderate bleeding is normal (soaking 1 pad/hour for first few hours)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Severe Bleeding (Mother):**
- Soaking through pad in <15 minutes
- Large clots
- Dizziness, pale, weak
- **Action:** Massage uterus firmly, elevate legs, keep warm, get help ASAP

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Baby Not Breathing:**
- Blue color
- Limp
- **Action:** Vigorous rubbing, flick feet, infant CPR if needed

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Cord Around Neck (Tight):**
- Can't slip over head
- **Action:** Clamp cord in two places (or tie tightly), cut between clamps

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Bandages:**
- Clean t-shirts (cut into strips)
- Sheets (boiled and dried)
- Menstrual pads (sterile, absorbent - excellent for large wounds)
- Tampons (gunshot/puncture wounds - stops bleeding, but must be removed and cleaned properly)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Tape:**
- Duct tape (over gauze, not on skin)
- Superglue (wound closure)
- Butterfly strips: Cut tape into thin strips

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Gloves:**
- Plastic bags over hands (not ideal, but barrier)
- Wash hands thoroughly before/after

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Antiseptic:**
- Honey (raw, unpasteurized) - natural antibiotic
- Alcohol (drinking liquor 80+ proof)
- Betadine/iodine (water purification tablets diluted)
- Boiled water (cooled)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Irrigation:**
- Squeeze bottle
- Clean plastic bag with pinhole
- Syringe without needle (oral medication syringe)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Splints:**
- Sticks, branches
- Rolled newspaper/magazine
- Cardboard
- Foam sleeping pad

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Ice Pack:**
- Frozen vegetables
- Bag of ice/snow
- Cold water-soaked cloth

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Hot Pack:**
- Rice/beans in sock, microwaved (or heated by fire)
- Hot water bottle
- Warm wet towel

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Sling:**
- Triangular bandage
- T-shirt
- Belt

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Stretcher:**
- Blanket with poles through edges
- Sturdy door/board
- Two backpacks with poles

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Priorities After Major Injury/Illness:**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
1. **Stabilize ABCs** (Airway, Breathing, Circulation)
2. **Control bleeding**
3. **Treat for shock**
4. **Immobilize fractures**
5. **Clean wounds** (within 6-8 hours ideal)
6. **Document** (vital signs, injuries, treatments)
7. **Decide:** Evacuate vs. care in place
8. **Hydration/nutrition** (if conscious and no abdominal injury)
9. **Pain management**
10. **Monitor closely** (check vitals every 2-4 hours)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Goals:**
- Prevent infection
- Promote healing
- Maintain nutrition/hydration
- Monitor for complications

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Daily Routine:**
1. **Check vitals** 2-4x daily
2. **Wound care:**
 - Remove bandage
 - Inspect for infection signs
 - Clean/irrigate
 - Fresh bandage
3. **Pain management** (scheduled, not just as needed)
4. **Hydration:** 8-12 cups daily minimum
5. **Nutrition:** High protein, calories for healing
6. **Mobility:** Gentle movement if safe (prevents blood clots, pneumonia)
7. **Document** all changes

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Red Flags:**
- Worsening pain
- New fever
- Increased swelling/redness
- Confusion, disorientation
- Shortness of breath
- Reevaluate evacuation

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Recovery Phase:**
- Most acute danger passed
- Focus on healing, rehabilitation
- Continue monitoring for complications

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Physical Therapy:**
- Gentle range of motion
- Prevent stiffness, muscle loss
- Gradual increase in activity

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Nutrition:**
- High protein (healing)
- Calories (energy for recovery)
- Vitamins (C for wound healing, D for bones)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Mental Health:**
- Stress of injury + isolation
- Talk through experience
- Maintain routines
- Set small goals

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
## Key Reference: "Where There Is No Doctor"

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Free comprehensive medical manual for situations without doctors**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
- 500+ pages, illustrated
- Covers: Diagnosis, treatment, medications, procedures
- Written for laypeople
- Essential for long-term grid-down
- **Download free:**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Other Free Resources:**
- "Where There Is No Dentist" (same organization)
- US Army First Aid Manual (FM 4-25.11)
- WHO Emergency Care Manual

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**For extended care scenarios, prioritize:**

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
1. **Antibiotics** (various types - see MEDICAL_MEDICATIONS.md)
2. **Pain management** (ibuprofen, acetaminophen, stronger if possible)
3. **Wound care** (gauze, tape, antibiotic ointment, irrigation supplies)
4. **Suture kit** (if trained or willing to learn)
5. **Splinting materials**
6. **Diagnostic tools** (thermometer, BP cuff, stethoscope)
7. **Reference books** (printed, not digital)

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**Related Guides:**

- [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) - Master navigation

[src: MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md]
**This guide assumes extended care (days to weeks) without professional medical help. Always seek professional care when available.**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
# QUICK START - Emergency Preparedness in 30 Minutes

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Complete beginner? Start here. This is your fastest path to basic preparedness.**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
## The Absolute Minimum (Do This TODAY - 30 Minutes)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Step 1: Water (10 minutes)
**Goal: 3 days of drinking water**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Action:**
1. Go to your kitchen
2. Find empty 2-liter soda bottles (or buy bottled water)
3. Calculate: 1 gallon = 4 liters per person for 3 days
4. Fill bottles with tap water OR buy bottled water
5. Store in cool, dark place

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Cost: $0-15**
**You now have:** Basic hydration for 72 hours

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Step 2: Food (10 minutes)
**Goal: 3 days of ready-to-eat food**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Action:**
1. Check your pantry for:
 - Canned soup, beans, vegetables
 - Peanut butter, crackers
 - Energy bars, granola
 - Anything edible without cooking
2. Put aside enough for 3 days
3. Include a manual can opener!

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Cost: $0-30** (if you already have canned goods)
**You now have:** Basic food for 72 hours

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Step 3: Light & Communication (5 minutes)
**Goal: See in the dark, get information**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Action:**
1. Find: Flashlight + batteries
2. Find: Your phone charger
3. Optional: Buy a cheap battery-powered radio

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Cost: $0-20**
**You now have:** Light and communication

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Step 4: First Aid (5 minutes)
**Goal: Handle minor injuries**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Action:**
1. Check for existing first aid kit
2. OR gather: Band-aids, pain reliever, antibiotic ointment
3. Add any prescription medications (3-day supply)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Cost: $0-25**
**You now have:** Basic medical supplies

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**You just completed the bare minimum preparedness in 30 minutes!**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
You can now survive:
- Power outages
- Water disruptions
- Short evacuations
- Supply disruptions

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Total cost: $0-90** (if you already have some items)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Today (After reading this):
- [ ] Buy bottled water (3 gallons/person)
- [ ] Buy canned goods if pantry is empty
- [ ] Check flashlight batteries

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### This Weekend (2 hours):
- [ ] Build complete 72-hour kit [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)
- [ ] Create emergency contact list
- [ ] Take photos of important documents with phone

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Next Week:
- [ ] Read [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) for full understanding
- [ ] Learn basic water purification [TOPIC_WATER.md](TOPIC_WATER.md)
- [ ] Start building toward 2-week supply

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Don't Do This:
- **Don't wait** for "the perfect kit" - start with basics
- **Don't buy expensive** pre-made kits - DIY is better and cheaper
- **Don't ignore water** - it's #1 priority
- **Don't forget medications** - especially prescriptions
- **Don't tell everyone** - operational security matters

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Do This Instead:
- **Start small** - even 3 days is better than nothing
- **Build gradually** - add items weekly
- **Focus on basics** - water, food, shelter, medical
- **Test your kit** - practice using everything
- **Keep it simple** - complicated = won't do it

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Why 72 hours?**
- Emergency services take 3 days to reach most areas after major disasters
- Stores run out of supplies in 24-48 hours
- You need self-sufficiency for the critical first days

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**What you need:**
- Water: 3 gallons per person
- Food: 2,000-2,400 calories per day
- Shelter: Emergency blanket + weather protection
- Medical: Basic first aid + prescriptions
- Sanitation: Toilet paper, wet wipes, hand sanitizer

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Complete guide:** [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Level 1: Beginner (You are here)
**Goal:** 72-hour self-sufficiency
**Time:** 1-2 weeks
**Cost:** $150-300 per person
**Guide:** [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Level 2: Intermediate
**Goal:** 2-week self-sufficiency
**Time:** 1-3 months
**Cost:** $300-600 per person
**Guide:** [TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md](TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Level 3: Advanced
**Goal:** 1-month self-sufficiency
**Time:** 3-6 months
**Cost:** $600-1,200 per person
**Guide:** [TIMELINE_1_MONTH.md](TIMELINE_1_MONTH.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Level 4: Expert
**Goal:** 3-6 months self-sufficiency
**Time:** 6-12 months
**Cost:** $1,200-3,000 per person
**Guide:** [TIMELINE_3_6_MONTHS.md](TIMELINE_3_6_MONTHS.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Level 5: Master
**Goal:** 1+ year self-sufficiency
**Time:** 1-3 years
**Cost:** $3,000-10,000+ per person
**Guides:** [TIMELINE_1_YEAR.md](TIMELINE_1_YEAR.md), [TIMELINE_LONG_TERM.md](TIMELINE_LONG_TERM.md)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**If you only get 20 things, get these:**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Water & Food (6 items)
1. **Water** - 3 gallons per person (bottled or stored)
2. **Canned food** - Soup, beans, vegetables (9 cans minimum)
3. **Protein** - Peanut butter, tuna packets, jerky
4. **Energy bars** - Quick calories, long shelf life
5. **Can opener** - Manual, P-38 style
6. **Eating utensils** - Disposable or camping

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Shelter & Warmth (4 items)
7. **Emergency blanket** - Mylar/"space blanket" ($2-5)
8. **Tarp** - 810 minimum, multiple uses
9. **Paracord** - 50-100 feet
10. **Extra clothing** - Socks, underwear, jacket

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Medical (3 items)
11. **First aid kit** - Basic bandages, antiseptic
12. **Pain reliever** - Ibuprofen or acetaminophen
13. **Prescription meds** - 7-day supply minimum

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Tools & Light (4 items)
14. **Flashlight** - LED, extra batteries
15. **Radio** - Battery or hand-crank, NOAA weather
16. **Multi-tool** - Knife, pliers, screwdriver
17. **Lighter/matches** - Waterproof container

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Sanitation (3 items)
18. **Toilet paper** - 1 roll per person minimum
19. **Hand sanitizer** - 60%+ alcohol
20. **Garbage bags** - Heavy duty, multiple uses

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### If you have 10 minutes:
1. Fill water bottles (3 gallons/person)
2. Gather flashlight + batteries
3. Put aside canned food

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### If you have 1 hour:
1. All of the above
2. Build basic first aid kit
3. Gather important documents
4. Create emergency contact list

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### If you have 1 day:
1. All of the above
2. Complete 72-hour kit [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)
3. Practice using everything
4. Store properly

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### If you have 1 week:
1. All of the above
2. Learn water purification
3. Plan evacuation routes
4. Take first aid course

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### $0 Budget (Use what you have):
- Fill soda bottles with tap water
- Gather existing canned goods
- Use garbage bag + bucket = toilet
- Candles for light
- Existing medications

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### $50 Budget:
- Bottled water: $15
- Canned goods: $20
- Flashlight + batteries: $10
- First aid kit: $5

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### $100 Budget:
- Water (cases): $20
- Food (canned + bars): $40
- Light (flashlight, radio): $20
- First aid: $10
- Emergency blanket: $5
- Misc (bags, wipes): $5

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### $200 Budget:
- Complete 72-hour kit for 1 person
- Follow [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md) week-by-week guide

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Best Locations:
1. **Near main exit** - Easy to grab
2. **Closet by front door** - Accessible
3. **Under bed** - Out of way but reachable
4. **Hall closet** - Central location

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Avoid:
- Attic (too hot)
- Garage (temperature extremes)
- Basement (flooding risk)
- Far from exits

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Containers:
- Plastic tote with lid (waterproof)
- Backpack (portable)
- Rolling suitcase (wheels help)
- 5-gallon bucket (cheap, durable)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
## Emergency Contacts (Write These Down)

[src: QUICK_START.md]
Create a card with:
- Family member (out of state): _______________
- Local emergency contact: _______________
- Doctor: _______________
- Pharmacy: _______________
- Insurance: _______________
- Meeting location: _______________

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Done in 30 minutes?**
- [ ] 3 gallons water per person
- [ ] 3 days of food
- [ ] Flashlight + batteries
- [ ] Basic first aid supplies
- [ ] Important phone numbers written down

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**If yes, you're now better prepared than 90% of people!**

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Immediate (This Week):
1. **Test your kit** - Try using everything
2. **Read full guide** - [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)
3. **Make family plan** - Where to meet, who to call
4. **Learn basics** - Water purification, first aid

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Short-term (This Month):
1. **Add to kit** - Fill gaps, improve quality
2. **Learn skills** - Take CPR/first aid course
3. **Plan evacuation** - Routes, destinations
4. **Store documents** - Digital + physical copies

[src: QUICK_START.md]
### Long-term (This Year):
1. **Build 2-week supply** - [TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md](TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md)
2. **Develop skills** - Water purification, fire starting, shelter
3. **Join community** - CERT, neighborhood watch
4. **Help others** - Teach family/friends

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: Is this for crazy preppers?**
A: No! This is basic common sense. You have car insurance, right? Same idea.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: How long will this take?**
A: 30 minutes for basics, 2 hours for complete 72-hour kit.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: How much will it cost?**
A: $0-200 depending on what you have. Most people spend $100-150.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: What if I live in an apartment?**
A: Perfect! Smaller space = easier to prepare. Focus on compact items.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: What disaster am I preparing for?**
A: All of them. This works for power outages, storms, floods, earthquakes, etc.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: Do I need guns?**
A: Not for basic preparedness. Focus on water, food, shelter, medical first.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: What about my kids?**
A: Each person needs their own supplies. Include comfort items (toys, books).

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: What about my pets?**
A: Yes! Add 3 days of pet food, water, medications, carrier.

[src: QUICK_START.md]
**Q: Where do I start learning more?**
A: [INDEX.md](INDEX.md) - Master navigation hub

4. Download free resources

[src: SEARCH_INDEX.md]
### 72 Hours (3 Days)
**Primary Guide:** [TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md](TIMELINE_72_HOURS.md)

[src: SEARCH_INDEX.md]
**Topics Covered:**
- Water: 3 gallons/person
- Food: Ready-to-eat, 7,200 calories
- Shelter: Emergency blankets, tarp
- Medical: First aid kit
- Sanitation: Portable toilet, hygiene
- Energy: Batteries, flashlights
- Communications: Radio, phone charger
- Documents: Copies, waterproof storage
- Special needs: Infants, elderly, pets

[src: SEARCH_INDEX.md]
### 2 Weeks
**Primary Guide:** [TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md](TIMELINE_2_WEEKS.md)

[src: SEARCH_INDEX.md]
**Topics Covered:**
- Water: 14 gallons/person + purification
- Food: Canned goods, dry staples
- Shelter: Home weatherproofing
- Medical: Extended supplies, prescriptions
- Sanitation: Waste system, hygiene supplies
- Energy: Solar charger, battery bank
- Communications: HAM radio basics
- Skills: Water purification, fire starting

[src: SEARCH_INDEX.md]

- "?" for single character

### Phase 6: Location-Specific Deep Dive

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**LOCATION_GUIDES.md** (24KB) NEW THIS SESSION
- Comprehensive analysis of 4 environments:
 - Urban (Cities/Apartments) - 15KB
 - Suburban (Single-Family Homes) - 12KB
 - Rural (Countryside) - 10KB
 - Mountain (High Altitude) - 8KB

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Quick Comparison Matrix:**
- 12 critical factors compared
- Population density, space availability, water access
- Food storage capacity, gardening potential
- Emergency services response time
- Resource scarcity timeline
- Self-sufficiency feasibility
- Evacuation challenges
- Community support
- Climate control
- Fuel storage

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Urban Environment (Deep Coverage):**
- Space constraints (500-1,000 sq ft living)
- Infrastructure 100% dependence
- High population = rapid depletion
- Solutions: Water bricks, vertical storage, OPSEC
- Cooking: Camp stove SAFELY (ventilation critical)
- Heating: NEVER charcoal indoors = CO death
- Sanitation: Bucket toilet system
- Gardening: Container, balcony, sprouts
- Security: Don't advertise preps
- Evacuation: Bug out early, on foot plan

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Suburban Environment:**
- Advantages: 1,500-3,000 sq ft, garage, yard
- Water: 55-gal drums, rainwater collection
- Food: 6-12 month supply feasible
- Garden: 400-1,000 sq ft can feed family
- Solar: Roof space available
- Generator: 3,000-7,000W portable
- Chickens: 3-6 hens = 12-30 eggs/week
- Community: Neighborhood organizing

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Rural Environment:**
- Advantages: 1-40+ acres, well water, livestock
- Challenges: Isolation (emergency services 30-60+ min)
- Water: Hand pump backup CRITICAL
- Food: Large garden (2,000-10,000 sq ft)
- Livestock: Chickens, goats, pigs, cattle
- Hunting: Big game, fishing, foraging
- Preservation: Root cellar, canning, dehydrating
- Energy: Off-grid solar feasible
- Heating: Wood stove + 3-6 cords/year

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Mountain Environment:**
- Challenges: 7,000-12,000+ ft altitude
- Short season: June-August only (60 days!)
- Extreme weather: -20 degF to -40 degF
- Snow: 10-30 feet accumulation
- Isolation: Hours from services, no cell
- Water: Must not freeze (indoor storage)
- Food: 3+ months stored (snowed in)
- Heating: 6-10 cords/winter = life/death
- Medical: WFR training minimum (hospital 1-3 hrs)
- Communication: Satellite phone, HAM radio

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Location-Specific Checklists:**
- Urban: 13 critical items
- Suburban: 13 critical items
- Rural: 16 critical items
- Mountain: 15 critical items

[src: SESSION_SUMMARY.md]
**Impact:**
- Preparedness adapted to reality
- Location-specific challenges addressed
- Tailored solutions provided
- Avoid common location mistakes

# SHELTER GUIDE: Construction & Long-Term

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Scenarios:**
- Grid-down extended (months-years)
- Wilderness survival (lost, stranded)
- Homesteading (building cabin)
- Post-disaster rebuilding
- Bug-out location establishment

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Timeline:** Building shelter for weeks, months, or permanent occupation

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cutting:**
- Axe (3.5 lb, 36" handle): $40-100
- Hatchet (1.5 lb, 14" handle): $30-60
- Hand saw (crosscut or bow saw): $20-40
- Folding saw: $15-30

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Shaping:**
- Draw knife: $30-60
- Chisel set: $30-80
- Plane (hand plane): $40-100

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Fastening:**
- Hammer (16-20 oz claw): $20-40
- Mallet (wooden): $15-30
- Drill (hand brace & bits): $30-80

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Measuring:**
- Tape measure (25 ft): $10-20
- Level: $15-30
- Square (framing square): $10-25
- Chalk line: $10

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Digging:**
- Shovel (round point): $25-50
- Spade (square): $25-50
- Pickaxe: $30-60
- Post hole digger: $40-80

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Power Tools (If Available):**
- Chainsaw: $200-600
- Circular saw: $80-200
- Drill (battery): $100-200
- Generator: $300-1,000

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Wood:**
- **Logs:** Structural (walls, frame)
- **Poles:** Smaller supports (rafters, purlins)
- **Branches:** Thatching, wattle-and-daub
- **Bark:** Roofing, siding

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Best Trees:**
- Pine, fir, spruce (straight, tall, softwood - easier to work)
- Cedar, juniper (rot-resistant, lightweight)
- Oak, ash (hardwood - strong, durable, heavy)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Harvest:**
- Dead standing (already dry, but check for rot)
- Live trees (stronger, must dry/season 6-12 months)
- Diameter: 6-12" for logs, 2-6" for poles

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Earth:**
- **Clay:** Mortar, cob, daub, bricks
- **Sand:** Mixing, drainage
- **Soil:** Earthbags, sod roofs, rammed earth
- **Stone:** Foundation, walls, fireplace

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Vegetation:**
- **Grass:** Thatching, bedding, insulation
- **Leaves:** Insulation (2-3 ft thick)
- **Moss:** Chinking (fill gaps), insulation
- **Reeds/cattails:** Thatching, matting

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Urban/Suburban:**
- Pallets (walls, flooring): Free
- Corrugated metal (roofing): Salvage
- Plywood/OSB: Construction sites (ask permission)
- Tarps, plastic sheeting: Waterproofing
- Insulation batts: Demolition sites
- Windows, doors: Habitat ReStore ($5-50)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Rural:**
- Barn wood: Siding, flooring
- Fence posts: Framing
- Tin roofing: Waterproof roof

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Why Foundation Matters:**
- Keeps structure off damp ground (rot prevention)
- Level base (structural integrity)
- Protects from moisture, pests, frost heave

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**1. Post Foundation (Simplest):**
- 4-6 posts set in ground
- 2-3 ft deep (below frost line if cold climate)
- Gravel in hole (drainage)
- Tamp firmly
- Posts support floor beams

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cost:** $50-150
**Time:** 4-8 hours
**Best for:** Cabins, pole barns

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**2. Pier Foundation (More Stable):**
- Concrete blocks or poured piers at corners and 6-8 ft intervals
- Set on gravel (drainage)
- Level with shims
- Beams span piers

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cost:** $100-300
**Time:** 1-2 days
**Best for:** Larger cabins, permanent structures

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**3. Rubble Trench (Stone Foundation):**
- Dig trench 12-18" deep, 12-18" wide
- Fill with stone/gravel
- Level top with flat stones or concrete
- Timbers rest on stones

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cost:** $50-200 (if stone available)
**Time:** 2-4 days
**Best for:** Log cabins, stone structures

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**4. Slab (Requires Concrete):**
- Excavate and level
- Gravel base (4-6")
- Rebar grid
- Pour concrete (4-6" thick)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cost:** $500-2,000+
**Time:** 1 week (including curing)
**Best for:** Permanent homes (not primitive)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Size:** 8 ft wide 6 ft deep 6 ft tall (1-2 people)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Materials:**
- 2 posts (6 ft tall, 4-6" diameter): Ridge support
- 1 ridge pole (10 ft long, 4" diameter)
- 10-15 rafters (8 ft long, 2-3" diameter)
- Thatching: Branches, grass, bark, or tarp
- Cordage or nails

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Steps:**
1. Set 2 posts 8 ft apart, 6 ft tall
2. Lash ridge pole across top of posts
3. Lean rafters against ridge pole (angle 45-60 degrees)
4. Space rafters 6-12" apart
5. Weave horizontal branches between rafters (lattice)
6. Thatch from bottom up (overlap like shingles)
7. Thick layer 6-12" (waterproof)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 1-2 days
**Cost:** $0-50 (if buying tarp/cordage)
**Shelter life:** Months to 1-2 years

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Size:** 10 ft wide 8 ft deep 6 ft tall

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Materials:**
- 2 tarps (1012 ft): Roof and floor
- Ridge pole: 12 ft
- 2 end posts: 6 ft
- Paracord
- Stakes

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Steps:**
1. Set posts 10 ft apart, 6 ft tall
2. Ridge pole across tops
3. Drape tarp over ridge (A-frame shape)
4. Stake out corners
5. Second tarp on ground (floor)
6. Optional: Close ends with debris/tarp

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 2-4 hours
**Cost:** $50-100
**Shelter life:** 6 months to 2 years (tarp degrades)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Size:** 8 ft wide 12 ft long 8 ft tall (peak)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Materials:**
- Lumber (if available) OR logs
- Roof material (shingles, metal, thatch)
- Door, window (optional)
- Nails, screws

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Frame:**
1. **Floor:** 26 joists on piers, plywood decking
2. **Walls:** 24 A-frame trusses every 24" (7 total)
 - Each truss: Two 10 ft boards meet at peak (60-degree angle)
3. **Ridge:** 26 board along peak
4. **Purlins:** Horizontal boards across trusses (roof support)
5. **End walls:** Triangular, one with door

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Roof:**
- Plywood sheathing
- Tar paper
- Shingles OR metal OR thick thatch

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 1-2 weeks (with help)
**Cost:** $500-1,500 (lumber), $0-300 (logs)
**Shelter life:** 10-30 years

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Advantages:**
- Simple design (no complex joints)
- Sheds snow/rain well (steep sides)
- Minimal lumber
- Strong structure

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Downsides:**
- Limited headroom (sloped walls)
- Less usable space

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Concept:** Partial underground, earth-covered roof

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Advantages:**
- Insulated (earth = 50-60 degF year-round)
- Hidden (low profile)
- Fireproof
- Quiet

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Challenges:**
- Labor-intensive (digging)
- Moisture/drainage critical
- Requires strong roof (earth is heavy)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 1: Site Selection**
- Hillside (easier excavation)
- Well-drained soil (not clay that holds water)
- South-facing (sunlight into entrance)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 2: Excavation**
- Dig into hillside 8-10 ft deep, 10-12 ft wide, 12-14 ft back
- Slope floor toward entrance (drainage)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 3: Walls**
- Timber supports (66 posts, logs)
- Stacked stone (dry stack or mortar)
- Earth walls (stable soil only, rib with timbers)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 4: Roof**
- **Critical:** Must support 300+ lbs per sq ft (earth weight)
- Log beams (8-12" diameter) every 2-3 ft
- Crossbeams perpendicular
- Plywood or planks (deck)
- Waterproof layer (plastic sheeting, pond liner)
- 12-18" earth on top
- Sod/grass cover (erosion control)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 5: Entrance**
- Timber framed
- Door at angle (shed water)
- Drainage ditch around entrance

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Step 6: Ventilation**
- Pipe through roof (stove pipe or PVC)
- Intake near door, exhaust high on back wall

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 2-4 weeks (digging by hand)
**Cost:** $200-800 (timbers, waterproofing)
**Shelter life:** 10-50 years (if well-built)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Drainage is CRITICAL:**
- French drain around uphill side (gravel-filled trench)
- Ditches divert water away
- Floor slopes out
- Check after rains (fix leaks immediately)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Concept:** Woven branch walls (wattle) plastered with mud/clay (daub)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Size:** 10 ft diameter round hut, 8 ft tall

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Materials:**
- Posts (4-6" diameter): 12-20, 8 ft tall
- Wattle (flexible branches - willow, hazel): Hundreds
- Daub: Clay soil, sand, straw, water
- Roof poles
- Thatching (grass, reeds)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Frame:**
1. Mark circle (10 ft diameter)
2. Set posts every 2-3 ft around circle, 2 ft deep
3. Top plate: Horizontal pole lashed around top of posts (creates ring)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Walls (Wattle):**
1. Weave flexible branches horizontally between posts (basket weave)
2. Start at bottom, work up
3. Tight weave (daub base)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Walls (Daub):**
1. Mix: 3 parts clay soil, 2 parts sand, 1 part straw/grass, water to thick paste
2. Apply to both sides of wattle (press through gaps)
3. Build up 2-3 coats (each 1-2" thick)
4. Smooth surface
5. Let dry (1-2 weeks per coat)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Roof:**
1. Center pole (10 ft tall)
2. Rafters from top plate to center pole (cone shape)
3. Horizontal lashing around rafters (support thatch)
4. Thatch thick (12-18") from bottom up

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Door:** Frame opening, hang door or hides

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 3-6 weeks
**Cost:** $0-100
**Shelter life:** 5-20 years (needs daub repairs)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Logs:** 40-60 logs (8-12" diameter, 12-16 ft long)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Notching (Corner Joints):**
- **Saddle notch:** Round notch fits over log below (strongest, traditional)
- **Dovetail:** Wedge-shaped notch (very strong, complex)
- **Butt-and-pass:** Simple (logs alternate, rest on each other)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Foundation:**
- Pier or stone foundation
- Sill logs (first layer) on foundation, squared and level

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Walls:**
1. Lay first logs on sills (parallel, 12 ft apart)
2. Cut notches on ends (practice on scrap first)
3. Lay perpendicular logs (16 ft, notch over first)
4. Continue stacking, alternating direction
5. Check level frequently
6. Leave openings for door/windows (frame after)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Chinking (Fill Gaps):**
- Moss, oakum, or modern chinking between logs (seal drafts)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Roof:**
- Gable roof (A-shape)
- Rafters every 2 ft
- Ridge beam at peak
- Purlins across rafters
- Roofing (shingles, metal, shakes)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Floor:**
- Joists across foundation
- Plank or plywood decking

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Time:** 3-6 months (solo), 1-2 months (crew)
**Cost:** $500-3,000 (tools, roof, windows, door)
**Shelter life:** 50-100+ years

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Learning Curve:** High (practice notching)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Materials:** Grass, reeds, cattails, palm fronds, straw

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Steps:**
1. Horizontal supports (rafters + purlins)
2. Bundle thatch (6-12" diameter bundles)
3. Start at bottom edge
4. Tie bundles to purlins (overlap upward)
5. Work up to peak (shingle pattern)
6. Thick layer 12-18" (waterproof)
7. Peak cap (bundles straddling ridge, tied down)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Pros:** Free, insulates, lasts 10-20 years (if done right), beautiful
**Cons:** Labor-intensive, fire risk, needs steep pitch (45+ degrees)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Material:** Split cedar, pine (rot-resistant)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Steps:**
1. Sheathing (plywood or skip-boards)
2. Tar paper (optional, helps)
3. Shingles from bottom up
4. Overlap 1/3 to 1/2 (2-3 layers thick at any point)
5. Stagger joints (water can't run straight down)
6. Nail twice per shingle

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Pros:** Durable (20-40 years), attractive, insulating
**Cons:** Labor, requires nails, fire risk

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Material:** Corrugated steel/aluminum (scavenged or bought)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Steps:**
1. Rafters/purlins (metal spans 3-4 ft)
2. Lay sheets perpendicular to ridge (overlap 6")
3. Screw down (roofing screws with washers)
4. Overlap sheets at ridge, seal

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Pros:** Long-lasting (30-50 years), waterproof, fire-resistant, lightweight
**Cons:** Expensive (if buying new), noisy in rain, hot in sun

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Heat Loss Without Insulation:**
- Freezing in winter, hot in summer
- Fuel waste (if heating)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Dried Grass/Hay:**
- Pack in walls (if framed), under roof
- 6-12" thick
- Fire risk, rodent habitat

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Leaves:**
- Fill cavities 12-24"
- Cheap, available
- Compresses over time (refill)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Moss:**
- Chinking between logs
- Moderately insulating

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Earth:**
- Earth-covered roofs (dugout)
- Massive insulation (12-18" = R-20)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Straw Bales:**
- Stack like bricks
- Plaster both sides (earth or lime)
- Excellent insulation (R-30 to R-40)
- Fireproof (if plastered), cheap

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Fiberglass Batts:**
- R-13 to R-30
- Salvage from construction/demo
- Install in framed walls

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Rigid Foam:**
- R-5 to R-10 per inch
- Scavenge or buy
- Walls, under roof

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Concept:** Super-efficient wood stove, stores heat in mass

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**How It Works:**
- Burn small sticks in insulated chamber (super hot, clean burn)
- Exhaust through horizontal pipe embedded in bench (cob, brick)
- Mass absorbs heat, radiates for hours
- Uses 1/10th wood of regular stove

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**DIY Build:**
- Barrels, stovepipe, cob/bricks
- Plans online (free)
- $100-300 materials
- 1-2 weeks to build

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Benefits:**
- Minimal wood (twigs, scraps)
- Radiant heat (comfortable)
- Bench (sit on warm mass)
- Can integrate oven

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Outdoor oven for baking:**
1. Stone base (3 ft tall)
2. Dome of cob (clay, sand, straw)
3. Door opening
4. Chimney hole at back (draws smoke out)
5. Fire inside, heats mass
6. Remove coals, bake in retained heat

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Cost:** $0-50
**Time:** 2-3 days
**Use:** Bread, pizza, roasting

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Roof Catchment:**
- Gutters (bamboo, split logs, metal)
- Downspout to barrel/tank
- Filter (cloth, sand)
- 1" rain on 1,000 sq ft roof = 600 gallons

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Storage:**
- 55-gallon barrels: $10-50 (food-grade)
- IBC totes (275 gal): $50-150
- Cistern (1,000+ gal): $200-2,000

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Urban/Suburban:**
- Permit requirements (legal builds)
- Setback rules (distance from property line)
- HOA restrictions
- Focus: Workshop, shed, greenhouse (less regulated)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Rural:**
- More freedom (still check codes)
- Larger builds possible
- Off-grid systems (solar, well)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Wilderness:**
- No permits (public land = illegal)
- Private land: Get permission
- Leave No Trace if temporary

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Mountain:**
- Extreme weather (snow load, wind)
- Steep roof (snow shed)
- Insulation critical
- Shorter building season

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**While Building:**
- Eye protection (sawdust, chips)
- Gloves (splinters, blisters)
- Boots (foot protection)
- Ladder safety (stable base, 3 points of contact)
- Chainsaws (chaps, training)
- Axe (sharp = safer, controlled swings, clear area)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Fire:**
- Wood stove: Clearance (36" from walls), chimney (proper draft, no creosote buildup)
- Smoke detector, fire extinguisher
- Escape plan (2 exits)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Structural:**
- Roof load: Snow, thatch (heavy)
- Overbuilt better than underbuilt
- Tie-downs (wind)
- Foundation solid (frost heave, erosion)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Moisture:**
- Ventilation (prevents mold, rot)
- Drainage (away from foundation)
- Vapor barrier (if insulated)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Weekend:** Tarp shelter, upgraded lean-to
**1-2 Weeks:** A-frame, basic pole structure
**1 Month:** Small cabin, dugout, wattle-and-daub
**3-6 Months:** Log cabin, stone cottage, permanent structure

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Minimal ($0-100):**
- All-natural materials
- Hand tools (borrow or minimal)
- Simple designs (lean-to, debris hut)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Low ($100-500):**
- Tarp, cordage, nails
- Basic hand tools
- Scavenged lumber/materials
- A-frame, pole structure

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Moderate ($500-2,000):**
- New lumber (small cabin)
- Power tools (chainsaw, drill)
- Metal roofing
- Windows, door
- Insulation

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**High ($2,000-10,000+):**
- Large cabin (1220+)
- Concrete foundation
- Premium materials
- Full off-grid systems (solar, well)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Books (Library or Free PDFs):**
- "Shelter" by Lloyd Kahn (classic)
- "The Handbuilt Shelter Book" by Steen & Steen
- US Army Survival Manual (FM 3-05.70) - Free online
- "Primitive Technology" YouTube channel (John Plant)

[src: SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md]
**Plans:**
- Ana White (free woodworking plans): ana-white.com
- Instructables (DIY shelters)
- Permies.com (forums, natural building)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
## Shelter Priorities (Order of Importance)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**The Rule of Threes:**
- 3 minutes without air
- 3 hours without shelter (extreme weather)
- 3 days without water
- 3 weeks without food

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Protection from:**
1. **Weather** (rain, snow, wind, sun, cold, heat)
2. **Temperature** (hypothermia, hyperthermia)
3. **Elements** (UV, precipitation)
4. **Danger** (animals, people, debris)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Psychological Benefits:**
- Sense of security
- Rest and recovery
- Privacy
- Morale

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Most Common Scenario: Stay at Home During Disaster**

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
- Hurricane (if not evacuation zone)
- Winter storm / blizzard
- Power outage
- Pandemic
- Civil unrest
- Earthquake aftermath
- Tornado (in basement/interior room)
- Chemical spill (seal home)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**1. Identify Safe Rooms:**
- **Tornado:** Basement or interior room (lowest floor, no windows, center of home)
- **Hurricane:** Interior room away from windows, first floor
- **Earthquake:** Doorway, under sturdy table, away from windows
- **Chemical/biological:** Interior room, upper floor (gases sink), seal windows/doors

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**2. Prepare Safe Room:**
- Remove heavy objects from shelves
- Keep flashlights, radio, supplies
- Battery/crank power
- First aid kit
- 72-hour supplies (water, food, meds)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**3. Seal Capability (Chemical/Biological):**
- Plastic sheeting (10x10 ft per window): $20
- Duct tape: 3-4 rolls
- Cut plastic to fit windows/doors in advance
- Practice sealing (can do in <10 minutes)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**4. Temperature Control:**
- **Heat:** Fireplace, wood stove, propane heater (Mr. Heater Buddy)
- **Cool:** Battery fans, wet towels, basement retreat
- **Insulation:** Heavy curtains, draft stoppers

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**5. Lighting:**
- LED lanterns (battery): 2-3
- Headlamps: 1 per person
- Candles: 20-50 (fire risk - careful)
- Glow sticks: 20-30 (safe for kids)
- Extra batteries

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**6. Communications:**
- Battery/crank radio (weather alerts)
- Charged phone + power banks
- Paper map of area
- Emergency contact list (printed)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Minimum Supplies to Ride Out Short Disaster:**

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Water:**
- 1 gallon/person/day 3 days
- See TOPIC_WATER.md

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Food:**
- No cooking required (if no power)
- See TOPIC_FOOD.md

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Warmth/Shelter:**
- Sleeping bags or blankets (layered)
- Extra clothing layers
- Hats, gloves (cold weather)
- Emergency blankets (Mylar)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Sanitation:**
- Toilet paper (2 rolls per person)
- Bucket toilet setup (see below)
- Hand sanitizer
- Trash bags

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Light & Power:**
- Lanterns, flashlights
- Batteries (50-100 AA, 20-40 D)
- Matches/lighter
- Candles

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**First Aid & Meds:**
- See MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md
- Prescription medications (2-week supply)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Supplies:**
- 5-gallon bucket with lid
- Heavy-duty trash bags (13-30 gallon)
- Pool noodle or toilet seat (fits bucket)
- Kitty litter, sawdust, or peat moss
- Toilet paper
- Hand sanitizer

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Setup:**
1. Line bucket with trash bag
2. Add 2-3 inches kitty litter/sawdust
3. Cut pool noodle to fit rim (cushion), or use portable toilet seat
4. Place in private area

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Use:**
1. Do business as normal
2. Add scoop of litter/sawdust after each use (covers smell, absorbs)
3. Wipe with TP (put in bucket, not separate)
4. Hand sanitizer

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Disposal:**
- Change bag when 1/2-2/3 full
- Tie tightly
- Store outside in trash can (lid secure)
- Municipal pickup when restored, or bury (see below)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Smell Control:**
- Use litter/sawdust generously
- Keep lid on
- Change bag frequently
- Baking soda sprinkle helps

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Urine Separation (Advanced):**
- Pee in separate container (milk jug)
- Dilute 10:1 with water, pour on garden/grass (fertilizer)
- Reduces bucket fill rate, smell

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Dangers:** Carbon monoxide (CO) poisoning kills many in disasters

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**NEVER Use Indoors:**
- Charcoal grill (high CO)
- Propane camp stove (prolonged use)
- Gas-powered generator (must be 20+ ft from home)
- Gasoline heater

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**1. Fireplace (if you have one):**
- Stock firewood: 1/2-1 cord (short-term)
- Keep chimney swept (prevents fire)
- Fireplace screen (prevent embers)
- Ash bucket

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**2. Wood Stove:**
- More efficient than fireplace
- Heats larger area
- Can cook on top
- Requires chimney/vent installation

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**3. Propane Heater (Mr. Heater Buddy):**
- Indoor-safe with oxygen depletion sensor
- Uses 1 lb propane canisters (9 hrs per canister on low)
- Heats 200-300 sq ft
- Cost: $80-120
- Propane: $4-6 per canister (stock 20-50)
- **Still need ventilation** (crack window)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**4. Kerosene Heater (Indoor-rated):**
- 10,000-23,000 BTU
- Burns 8-12 hours per gallon
- Ventilation required
- Fuel storage concerns
- Cost: $150-300

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**5. Passive Heating:**
- Close off rooms (heat only 1-2 rooms)
- Hang blankets over doorways (insulate)
- Everyone in same room (body heat)
- Dress in layers (see below)
- Hot water bottles in sleeping bags

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Carbon Monoxide Detector:**
- Battery-powered required
- Check batteries monthly
- $20-40
- **ESSENTIAL if using any combustion heat**

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Base Layer (Next to Skin):**
- Synthetic (polyester) or wool
- NOT cotton (stays wet, makes you cold)
- Long underwear top and bottom

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Middle Layer (Insulation):**
- Fleece jacket or wool sweater
- Puffy jacket (down or synthetic)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Outer Layer (Wind/Water Protection):**
- Windbreaker or rain jacket
- Keeps warmth in, elements out

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Extremities (Lose Heat Fast):**
- Hat (lose 40% heat from head - myth, but head/neck important)
- Gloves or mittens (mittens warmer)
- Warm socks (wool or synthetic, 2 pairs)
- Insulated boots

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Sleeping:**
- Sleep in base layers + hat
- Use sleeping bag (rated for temp)
- Emergency blanket (mylar) under sleeping bag (reflects heat)
- Hot water bottle at feet
- Multiple people share warmth (sleep close)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Passive Cooling:**
1. **Block sun:** Close blinds/curtains on sunny side
2. **Retreat to coolest area:** Basement (underground = cooler), north side of home
3. **Create airflow:** Open windows at night, close during day (if outside cooler than inside)
4. **Cross-ventilation:** Windows on opposite sides
5. **Wet towels:** Hang in windows (evaporative cooling)
6. **Spray bottle + fan:** Mist skin, fan evaporates (cools)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Hydration:**
- Drink 1-2 gallons per day (hot weather)
- Electrolytes (see TOPIC_WATER.md)
- Avoid alcohol, caffeine (dehydrate)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Clothing:**
- Light colors (reflect sun)
- Loose, breathable (cotton OK for heat)
- Wide-brimmed hat
- Wet bandana around neck

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Battery Fans:**
- USB fans + power bank
- 10" battery fan (20-40 hours on batteries): $30-50
- Stock extra batteries

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Avoid Heat Illness:**
- Stay in shade
- Reduce activity during hottest hours (10am-4pm)
- Watch for signs (see MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Mandatory Evacuation Orders:**
- Hurricane (coastal)
- Wildfire
- Flood
- Chemical spill
- Dam failure

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Self-Evacuation (Before Mandatory):**
- Safer than waiting
- Less traffic
- More options

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**1. Friend/Family (50-100+ miles away):**
- Free
- Familiar, comfortable
- Arrange in advance

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**2. Hotel/Motel:**
- Book early (fills fast)
- Costs $80-200+ per night
- Bring supplies (may not be able to buy)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**3. Official Evacuation Shelter:**
- School, community center, church
- Free
- Crowded, noisy, no privacy
- Cots, basic supplies
- Red Cross often operates

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**4. Campground:**
- If you have RV/trailer/tent
- $20-50 per night
- Access to facilities

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**5. Rest Stop / Parking Lot (Last Resort):**
- Overnight in vehicle
- Free but uncomfortable
- Safety concerns

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
### Bug-Out Bag (72-Hour Evacuation Pack)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Container:**
- Backpack (40-60L adults, 20-30L kids)
- Hands-free for travel
- Durable, waterproof or with rain cover

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Clothing:**
- 3 sets underwear, socks
- 2 shirts, 2 pants
- 1 jacket/sweater
- Rain gear
- Hat, gloves (cold climate)
- Sturdy shoes (worn, extras in bag)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Shelter:**
- Emergency blanket (mylar): 2
- Tarp (8x10 ft): 1
- Paracord (50-100 ft)
- Tent (optional, if room/weight allows)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Water:**
- 2-3 liters in bottles
- Water filter (Sawyer Mini, LifeStraw)
- Purification tablets (backup)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Food:**
- 3 days, 2,000+ calories/day
- Energy bars, trail mix, jerky
- Lightweight, no cooking required
- See TOPIC_FOOD.md

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Fire:**
- Lighter: 2-3
- Waterproof matches
- Ferro rod (backup)
- Tinder (dryer lint, petroleum jelly cotton balls)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**First Aid:**
- See MEDICAL_FIRST_AID.md kit
- Personal medications (2-week supply)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Tools:**
- Knife (fixed blade or quality folder)
- Multi-tool
- Duct tape (wrap around water bottle)
- Safety pins
- Paracord

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Light:**
- Headlamp + extra batteries
- Flashlight
- Glow sticks

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Documents (Waterproof Bag):**
- ID copies (license, passport)
- Insurance papers (home, auto, medical)
- Bank account info
- Property deed/lease
- Medical records
- Emergency contacts
- Cash ($200-500, small bills)
- USB drive (scanned documents)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Communication:**
- Phone charger + power bank
- Battery/crank radio
- Whistle (signal for help)
- Paper map of region

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Hygiene:**
- Toothbrush, toothpaste (travel size)
- Soap, shampoo (travel size)
- Toilet paper (1 roll, remove cardboard, compress)
- Hand sanitizer
- Feminine products
- Ziplock bags (waste, waterproofing)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Personal:**
- Glasses (spare pair)
- Contact lenses + solution
- Prescription meds
- Comfort item for kids (small toy, book)
- Notebook, pencil

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Total Weight:**
- Adults: 20-40 lbs max (1/4 body weight)
- Kids: 10-20 lbs (lighter)
- Elderly: Reduce weight (prioritize essentials)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Storage:**
- Near main exit
- Everyone knows location
- Check/rotate every 6-12 months

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**If Forced to Build Shelter (Wilderness, Homeless, Disaster)**

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Priorities:**
1. **Flat ground** (comfort, water won't pool)
2. **Drainage** (avoid low spots, gullies)
3. **Protection from wind** (trees, rock walls, hills)
4. **Water access** (100+ ft away to avoid flooding/contamination, but close enough to access)
5. **Avoid hazards:**
 - Dead trees (widowmakers - fall in wind)
 - Flash flood areas (dry creek beds)
 - Avalanche/rockfall zones
 - Insect nests
6. **Sun exposure** (balance: morning sun good, afternoon shade in summer)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
### Tarp Shelter (Fastest, Most Versatile)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Tarp Sizes:**
- 8x10 ft: 1-2 people
- 10x12 ft: 2-3 people
- 12x16 ft: 4+ people

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Cost:** $15-50 (quality matters - reinforced grommets)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Setup #1: A-Frame (Classic)**
1. Tie paracord between 2 trees (6-8 ft high)
2. Drape tarp over cord (ridge line)
3. Stake out corners (45-degree angle)
4. Optional: Fold sides under for floor

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Setup #2: Lean-To (Simple)**
1. Tie one edge of tarp high (6-7 ft) between trees
2. Stake opposite edge low (ground or 1-2 ft)
3. Stake sides
4. Open on one side (face away from wind)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Setup #3: Plow Point (Wind/Rain)**
1. Stake one corner to ground
2. Opposite corner tied high to tree
3. Stake other two corners out (forms triangle)
4. Wind/rain hits point, diverts

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Setup #4: Flying Diamond (Full Coverage)**
1. Stake center of tarp high (pole or tree)
2. Stake all 4 corners out (pyramid shape)
3. Good for sun/rain, poor for wind

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Tarp Tips:**
- Use paracord (550 cord: Strong, lightweight)
- Taut-line hitch (adjustable knot)
- Pad stakes (prevents tearing grommets)
- Collect water in sag (rain collection)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**How to Build:**
1. **Frame:** Long ridge pole (8-10 ft) propped at 45 degrees on tree/fork
2. **Ribs:** Sticks laid against ridge pole (ribcage shape, both sides)
3. **Debris:** Pile leaves, pine needles, grass 2-3 ft thick (insulation)
4. **Door:** Stuff opening with leaves/pack
5. **Interior:** More leaves inside (bedding)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Pros:** Warm, windproof, insulated
**Cons:** Time-consuming (2-4 hours), gets dirty
**Use:** Cold weather, multi-day stay

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**How to Build:**
1. **Ridge pole:** Horizontal pole between 2 trees (4-5 ft high)
2. **Rafters:** Lean long sticks against ridge pole (angle)
3. **Thatching:** Weave/layer branches, leaves, bark across rafters (shingle from bottom up)
4. **Bedding:** Thick layer of leaves/pine needles

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Pros:** Fast (1-2 hours), simple
**Cons:** Open on one side, poor in wind
**Use:** Warm weather, overnight

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Snow Cave (if snowdrift available):**
1. Dig entrance tunnel into drift (upward angle)
2. Hollow out chamber (dome shape, 5-6 ft wide)
3. Smooth ceiling (prevents dripping)
4. Poke ventilation hole (ski pole, stick) in roof
5. Block entrance partially (snow block, pack)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Quinzhee (build snow pile):**
1. Pile snow in dome (8-10 ft wide, 5-6 ft tall)
2. Let consolidate 2-3 hours
3. Dig entrance tunnel
4. Hollow out (1 ft thick walls - use stick markers)
5. Ventilation hole in roof

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Warmth:** Insulated, reflects body heat (can be 30-40 degF warmer inside)
**Danger:** CO buildup (MUST have ventilation hole), collapse (make walls strong)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Cost:** Free (labor intensive)
**Time:** 3-6 hours

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
### Advantages
- Weatherproof
- Lockable (security)
- Can run heat (limited)
- Mobile (can relocate)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Keep in Vehicle Year-Round:**
- Blankets or sleeping bags: 1 per seat
- Water: 1 gallon per person
- Food: Granola bars, nuts (replace every 6 months)
- First aid kit
- Flashlight, batteries
- Jumper cables
- Ice scraper, shovel (winter)
- Sand/cat litter (traction in snow)
- Duct tape, paracord
- Portable phone charger

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Warmth:**
- Sleeping bag + blankets
- Dress in layers
- Hat and gloves (critical)
- Emergency blanket (mylar) over sleeping bag
- Cardboard under sleeping bag (insulation from cold seat)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Ventilation:**
- Crack windows 1-2 inches (CO and condensation)
- **NEVER run car continuously** (CO poisoning)
- Run 10-15 min per hour to warm up, then turn off

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Privacy:**
- Curtains or sunshades in windows
- Tinted windows help
- Park inconspicuously

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Safety:**
- Lock doors
- Keys accessible (quick escape)
- Phone charged
- Park in well-lit area (if possible) or hidden (depends on threat)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Condensation:**
- Breathing creates moisture (will freeze on windows in cold)
- Crack windows
- Wipe down windows in morning
- Desiccant packs help

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Priorities:**
1. Insulation from ground (sleeping pad, cardboard, pine boughs)
2. Block wind
3. Retain body heat (small space, seal drafts)
4. Stay dry (wet = hypothermia)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Shelter Types:**
- Enclosed (4 walls, roof)
- Small (body heat warms faster)
- Insulated (debris, snow)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Bedding:**
- Thick insulation from ground (lose heat to ground faster than air)
- Layer sleeping bags if cold

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Priorities:**
1. Shade (sun protection)
2. Airflow (ventilation)
3. Elevation (off ground if possible)
4. Water access

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Shelter Types:**
- Open sides (cross-breeze)
- Light-colored materials (reflect heat)
- Large (allows hot air to rise)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Avoid:**
- Enclosed shelters (trap heat)
- Dark materials (absorb heat)
- Low areas (hot air sinks)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Priorities:**
1. Waterproof roof (tarp, thick thatching)
2. Drainage (slope ground away, dig trench around shelter)
3. Elevated floor (off wet ground)
4. Seal gaps (wind-driven rain)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Tarp Tips:**
- Steep angle (45+ degrees) sheds rain
- Taut (no sagging pools)
- Extend beyond walls (drip line)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Natural Shelter:**
- Thick thatching (shingle from bottom up, overlap)
- 2-3 ft thick debris (waterproofs)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
1. **Assess safety:** Structure damage, gas leaks, fires
2. **Account for people:** Everyone safe, injured?
3. **Secure shelter:** Board broken windows, tarp roof damage, close off unsafe areas
4. **Establish sanitation:** Toilet system if plumbing out
5. **Inventory supplies:** Water, food, meds (what you have, what you need)
6. **Communicate:** Contact family, authorities (if phones work)
7. **Information:** Radio for updates, instructions
8. **Security:** Lock doors if civil unrest, board windows
9. **Rest:** Adrenaline crash - take shifts if needed
10. **Plan next 24 hours:** Ration supplies, assign tasks

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Goals:**
- Establish routine
- Conserve resources
- Maintain hygiene
- Gather information
- Prepare for longer duration

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Daily Routine:**
- Wake/sleep regular times (maintain normalcy)
- Meals at set times (ration)
- Hygiene (wash hands, brush teeth)
- Radio check (news updates)
- Inventory check (track supplies)
- Security patrol (check perimeter, damage)
- Entertainment (books, games, conversation - morale)
- Exercise (maintain health, prevent cabin fever)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Improvements:**
- Insulate shelter better (blankets on windows)
- Organize supplies (easy access)
- Set up charging station (solar, car)
- Establish better sanitation (privacy, odor control)
- Expand water collection (tarps, buckets)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**By Week 2:**
- Emergency likely subsiding OR long-term scenario clear
- Supplies running low
- Routines established
- Morale challenges

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Assess:**
- **End in sight?** Power/water restoring, evacuations lifting hold out
- **Long-term?** No services, government collapse transition to long-term plans (see SHELTER_CONSTRUCTION.md)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Resupply:**
- If stores opening: Restock immediately
- Barter/trade with neighbors
- Mutual aid groups

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Mental Health:**
- Stress, anxiety normal
- Talk through experience
- Kids need routine, play, normalcy
- Recognize signs of crisis (see MEDICAL_WILDERNESS.md)

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Minimal ($50-100):**
- Tarp 10x12: $25
- Paracord 100ft: $10
- Emergency blankets (4): $10
- Battery lantern: $15
- Bucket toilet setup: $25

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Standard ($200-400):**
- Above plus:
- Mr. Heater Buddy + propane: $150
- Sleeping bags (2): $60
- Bug-out bag supplies: $100
- Extra batteries, flashlights: $30

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
**Premium ($500-1,000):**
- Above plus:
- Generator (small): $300-500
- Solar panels + battery: $200-400
- Extensive camping gear: $200

[src: SHELTER_EMERGENCY.md]
